By all the dictates of good sense, the thing to do was to turn back, to leave the dog barking at the water’s edge and the ewe mourning on the shore. There were no human spectators to praise him for the deed or condemn him for its omission. He knew that even if he were put on trial no sensible man on the broad earth would hold it against him if he left the lamb to its fate.

===Chinese===
但他把愈涨愈高的恐惧从心里排开，飞快地研判了一回水势。羔羊看来在劫难逃。那样骇人的河没法蹚水过去。对岸有一根原木斜插进水里，可是没有路能过去。唯一的指望，是纵身跃入急流，一路搏到河心，抓住羔羊，再带着它游到安全的地方。

狗是帮不上忙的。这条河尽是巨岩和暗藏杀机的漩涡，水冰冷刺骨，黑暗里游泳的人很容易被冲到下游送命。这些休都看得分明。而在他心里，他觉出另一重不相上下的威胁——对岸有个朦胧的影子正候着，要同他争夺那几磅活肉。就在那一线微光里，他看出只剩几秒钟可以试行营救。狂乱的水冲击着羔羊受困其间的岩石；再过几秒钟，它就要停止挣扎了。

那不过是一头牲口，三千只羊里的一只羊羔，一小抱暖热的肉，没了也不会有谁惦记。救它，要把休身上最后一丝气力都榨尽，还很可能搭上他的性命。这不值得。照一切常理的指示，该做的是转身回去，任那条狗在水边狂吠、母羊在岸上哀鸣。没有一个人在场，做了没人为他称许，不做也没人责难。他知道，哪怕把他送上法庭受审，普天之下也没有一个明白人，会因他抛下羔羊任其自毙而怪罪于他。

===Original===
Yet in that moment of inner trial a great and a serene knowledge came to him. He knew﻿—in one instant of vision﻿—that it did not lay with the shepherd to consider abstract issues of life and death. It was not for him to try to balance the value of a lamb’s life with his own. His business was to watch the flock. His work was to guard the sheep. Nothing could come between.

All the voices of prudence and good sense were stilled before the voice of his own soul. He was afraid, but his fear could not come between the shepherd and his work. He wanted to turn away; but a power greater than his own will made him stand fast. The laws of his own being had given their decree﻿—and they could not be denied.

“All right, old fellow,” he said simply to the dog. “We’ll get the little devil.”

===Chinese===
然而就在那内心交战的一刻，一种伟大而安定的了悟降临到他身上。他明白了——在一瞬的洞见之中——轮不到牧羊人去思量生与死这类的抽象命题。也不是要他把一只羔羊的性命同自己的放在天平上称量。他的本分是看住羊群。他的职守是护卫羊只。什么都无法插足其间。

一切审慎与常理的声息，都在他自己灵魂的声音面前归于沉寂。他害怕，可他的恐惧横不到牧羊人与他的职守中间去。他想转身走开；可一股比他的意志更强大的力量叫他站定了。他生命自身的法则已经颁下裁断——而裁断无从违抗。

「行，老伙计，」他简简单单地对狗说。「咱们去把那小捣蛋弄回来。」

===Original===
The dog plunged in. It was the voice he had awaited. The man dropped his coat, his gun, and the small pack that he wore on his back, then ran a short distance up the stream. And the muscles of his body seemed to shiver and vibrate with strength as he plunged into the dark waters.

Broken Fang, the cougar, had seen the dog and the man on the opposite bank, and at first he had been afraid. He retained enough of his natural caution not to wish to reveal himself at once. “Most of all, fear men,” was one of the first of the long scroll of forest laws, and time had been when he had shivered and skulked with fear at just the human smell on the wind. But tonight he was very hungry. And the game was almost in his claws.

He hadn’t forgotten that he was the master of the forest, who had felled even the horned steer. What were these slight figures to stand against him? Many of the forest laws he had already broken; he might even yet break the law that forbade the death-feast﻿—and why should he obey now? A slow, terrible anger began to overcome him.

===Chinese===
狗纵身跃入水中。这正是它等候的那一声号令。休丢下外套、枪，还有背上的小背包，往上游跑了一小段，随即扎进幽暗的水里——浑身的肌肉仿佛蓄满了力量，震颤着、搏动着。

美洲狮断牙已经看见了对岸的狗和人，起初他心里发怵。他天生的谨慎尚存几分，不愿立刻现身。『最要紧的是怕人』，这是森林法典长卷上开篇的律条之一；想当年，光是风里飘来的人的气味，就足以叫他战栗着潜形匿迹。可今夜他饿得狠。而猎物几乎已经在他爪下。

他没有忘记自己是森林之主，连长角的阉牛都曾被他放倒。这两个瘦条条的身形，凭什么同他作对？森林的法条，他已犯过许多；连那道禁绝死亡盛宴的法度，他说不定也要破了——事到如今，凭什么还要遵从？一股缓慢而可怕的怒火渐渐压倒了他。

===Original===
He had missed his kill too many times that night. He felt a blind desire not to run away but to stay and fight. He crept down nearer the water’s edge, his glaring eyes on the two figures on the opposite bank. At that instant he saw both of them plunge into the torrent.

This was the action of the deer when the wolves pressed them close﻿—to jump into the river to escape. These creatures were like the deer, a breed known to him of old. Little Death the mink had done the same thing, the night he had been so hungry and in which the miniature slayer had slipped between his talons. The river was always a place of refuge for those that were afraid. Thus it was plain that if the dog and the man feared him there was no further cause for him to be afraid of them. He crept out boldly, a magnificent, tawny figure in the dim light, on to the log that led down into the water. His eyes shone with blue fire.

Perhaps the dog saw him first. He swam with wide-open eyes. He had jumped in almost opposite the little pile of driftwood where the lamb lay and was immediately swept downstream. Hugh, however, had leaped in farther up, and he had had a chance to work into the middle of the current as he was carried down.

===Chinese===
这一夜他扑空太多次了。他生出一股盲目的欲望：不是逃走，而是留下来打一架。他悄悄往下爬，逼近水边，怒睁的双眼盯死对岸那两个身影。就在这一瞬，他看见两个身影双双跃进了急流。

这是鹿被狼群逼急了时的举动——跳进河里逃生。这两个东西跟鹿一个样，是他素来识得的一族。「小死神」（Little Death）那只水貂也这么干过——就在他饿得要命、那小小的杀手却从他爪缝间溜走的那个夜里。河，从来是胆怯者的避难之所。这么说来，狗和人既然怕他，他自然更没有理由怕他们了。他大着胆子爬了出来，微光里一副气度堂堂的黄褐色身影，登上那根插进水里的原木。他的眼睛燃着蓝色的火。

兴许狗先瞧见了他。它睁大着眼睛游水。它跳下去的地方几乎正对着羔羊受困的那一小堆浮木，立刻就被冲往下游。休却是在更上游的地方跃入的，被冲着往下的当口，还有机会一路挣进主流的正中。

===Original===
He had always been a strong swimmer, and he needed that strength now. The water was icy cold; it gripped him with resistless strength. He fought it with powerful strokes, but it seemed to him that he was tossed about like a straw. Even he headed upstream and out, not hoping to make headway but only to offset, in some degree, the incredible power of the current. He had never done such swimming as this before. Silver Creek was not at all like the swimming tank in his athletic club. He gave his whole strength; and it seemed to him that he was fighting for his life.

His plan was to seize the log that extended down into the water, brace himself while he rescued the lamb, and either climb up the log or be carried on down and swim to the bank. He didn’t know that the log was already occupied: that a great forest creature had come down to contest the lamb’s life with him. He was full in the middle of the current, by now; and then, with a powerful lunge of his body, his arms swept about the log.

He braced himself, then turned in order to reach the lamb. He reached for a hold higher on the log. Something brown, with extended talons, rested upon the rough bark within a few inches of his hand, and at first he did not understand. Then the spray cleared from his eyes, and he knew the truth.

===Chinese===
他素来是个游泳的好手，此刻正用得着这份膂力。水冰冷刺骨；它以不可抗拒的力量攫住他。他以有力的划水与之相搏，却觉得自己像根稻草似的被抛来掷去。他一路朝上游、朝外侧游，并不指望前进分毫，只求多少抵消一点那股不可思议的水流之力。这样的泳他从不曾游过。银溪同他体育俱乐部里的游泳池根本是两回事。他使出了全部力气；他觉得自己是在为性命而搏。

他的计划是抓住那根斜伸入水的原木，撑稳身子救出羔羊，然后要么顺着原木爬上去，要么被继续往下冲、再游向岸边。他不知道那根原木已有了主：一头伟大的森林生灵已经下来，要同他争夺羔羊的性命。此刻他正当主流的正中；随后，身子猛力一挺，双臂一把抱住了原木。

他撑稳身子，随即转过身去够羔羊。他往原木更高处探找一个抓握之处。一样褐色的东西，张着利爪，正搭在粗糙的树皮上，离他的手不过几英寸；起初他没有明白过来。随后浪花从眼前散去，他知道了真相。

===Original===
It was not a thing to forget: that wilderness scene in midstream in which Broken Fang and this man of cities came face to face for the first time. It made a grim and moving picture: the magnificent outline of the great cat in silhouette against the slowly brightening eastern sky; the lamb, struggling in the little patch of driftwood; the watching forest, the dark river with its ivory foam; and the gray half-light of dawn casting dimness over all. Yet there was no such folly as mistaking its reality. The form on the log was not just a shadow, an image, a drunken fancy. It was as real as the icy touch of the river and so near that he could see the creature’s ears, lying flat against the savage head.

Hugh’s head, shoulders and one arm were clear of the water: a few inches distant gleamed the white fangs of the puma. The fight for the life of the lamb was at close quarters at last. And Broken Fang had all the advantage. He was above and had room in plenty to strike; he had only to tap down with his paw or lash out with his deadly fangs. Hugh, on the other hand, had braced himself against the log and had only one arm free.

It came to Hugh that he might give up his attempt to save the lamb, slip back into the water, and be carried down out of the reach of the rending fangs. But even before the thought went fully home he had taken the opposite course. Perhaps it was because he was the shepherd﻿—for the moment at least forgetting and ignoring all things but his guardianship of the sheep﻿—and Broken Fang was an ancient enemy. Perhaps he instinctively realized that if he turned even for an instant those terrible talons would lash down at him. His instincts came sure and true, and he knew he must make no sudden motions. He began to pull himself upward toward the great cat.

===Chinese===
那不是能忘怀的事：河心这幅荒野景象里，断牙与这个城市里的人第一次面对面。它构成一幅狞厉而动人的画：大猫雄伟的轮廓，剪影般衬在东方渐渐亮起的天幕前；羔羊，在小片浮木间挣扎；守望的森林，黑沉沉的河与河上象牙色的浪花；还有黎明的灰色半明，给这一切罩上昏蒙。然而，绝没有把它错认成幻境的道理。原木上的那个形体不只是个影子、一个映像、一场醉酒的妄想。它同河水的冰凉触碰一样真实，近得他能看清那畜生的双耳——平平地贴在那颗凶蛮的头颅上。

休的头、肩膀和一条手臂露在水面上：几英寸开外，美洲狮的白牙闪着寒光。争夺羔羊性命的一仗，终于到了近身相搏的地步。而断牙占尽了便宜。他居高临下，有的是出手的余地；他只需一爪拍下，或用那致命的獠牙一挥。休呢，只能靠原木撑住身子，腾得出一条手臂。

休起过一个念头：他可以放弃救羔羊的打算，滑回水里，任水把自己冲到那撕裂一切的獠牙够不着的地方。可这念头还没有想透，他已走上了相反的路。也许，这是因为他是牧羊人——至少这一刻，除了看守羊群的职守，其余一切都忘了、都不顾了——而断牙是古老的仇敌。也许他凭本能意识到：哪怕只转过身去一瞬，那可怕的利爪也会当头劈落。他的本能来得又稳又准，他知道绝不可做突然的动作。他开始向着那头大猫，一寸寸向上攀去。

===Original===
He knew one single moment of blighting terror when he thought that the cougar was going to stand its ground. The animal crouched, his lips drew in a snarl, he raised one foot with extended claws. He was the great Broken Fang; and this creature that rose up from the waters was but a fawn in strength compared to him. The lamb was almost in his grasp. His sinuous tail twitched at the tip.

It was the most terrible moment in Hugh’s life, a moment of test in which the basic metal within the man was tried in the fire. He could not turn back now. He shouted with the full power of his lungs; but on the might of his own eye and will, on the old elemental superiority of man over the beasts of the field and the fowls of the air rested life or death. He was of the dominant breed, and if he forgot that fact for one instant﻿—in this moment of stress﻿—those white fangs would lash out.

For the first time in his life Hugh Gaylord had to rely on his own manhood for success or failure. His wealth, his influential social connections, breeding and birth and class could not aid him here. It was simply the trial of man against beast, the power of man’s will against resistless physical might. If he flinched, if he turned for an instant, all was lost.

===Chinese===
有那么仅仅一瞬，他尝到了一种摧肝裂胆的恐怖——他以为那美洲狮要守住地盘寸步不让了。那畜生伏低了身子，双唇绷起一阵狞吼，抬起一只脚，利爪尽张。他是伟大的断牙；这个从水里升上来的东西，论力气同他相比不过是一头小鹿。羔羊几乎已在他掌握之中。他蜿蜒的长尾，尾尖一抽一抽。

这是休平生最可怕的一刻，是一刻试炼，人内里的基底金属正在火中受验。此刻他已无法回头。他放开肺腑的全部力量喝了一声；然而生死所系的，是他自己的目光与意志的威力，是人对于田野的走兽和空中的飞禽那古老而本原的优越。他属于居统治地位的族类，只要有一瞬忘了这个事实——在这危急的关头——那些白牙便会劈面挥来。

平生第一次，休·盖洛德要靠自己的男子气概来定成败。他的财富、他煊赫的社会关系、教养、门第与阶级，在这里全都帮不上忙。这干脆就是人对兽的一场审判，是人的意志之力对不可抗拒的膂力的一场较量。他只要瑟缩一下，只要转身一瞬，便万事皆休。

===Original===
His face was white as white foam, the lines cut deep like brands, the heart of the man was like ice. Yet the eyes didn’t flinch. They burned steady and straight﻿—into the glowing twin circles of blue fire just above him.

There was a long, strange moment of silence in which it seemed that the river flowed like a soft wind. All the power and strength of his being was at test. And slowly﻿—snarling at every step﻿—the puma began to back away up the log into the thickets.

Still Hugh held firm. He waited until the great cougar was ten feet distant, then his arm shot out like a serpent’s head toward the lamb. He seized it in a strong grasp, released the log, and yielded himself to the full force of the current. As he swept down he saw the cougar﻿—his courage come back to him now that the masterful eyes no longer glared into his﻿—spring out with a savage snarl to the end of the log. But he was out of reach now, and safe, struggling once more against the might of the current.

===Chinese===
他的脸白得像雪白的泡沫，脸上的纹路刻得深如烙痕，这人的心像冰一样。可那双眼睛没有退缩。它们平稳而笔直地烧着——烧进正对头顶上方那对灼灼发亮的蓝色火环里。

有一段漫长而奇异的静默，静默里河水仿佛像一阵软风般流淌。他生命的全部力量与气力都在受验。而慢慢地——一步一声低吼——美洲狮开始退上原木，退进灌木丛里去了。

休仍旧撑得纹丝不动。他直等到那头大美洲狮退开十英尺，手臂才像蛇头一样霍地探出，直取羔羊。他一把将它牢牢攥住，松开原木，把自己交给水流全部的力量。顺流冲下的当口，他看见美洲狮——那双慑人的目光不再逼视着它，它的胆气又回来了——狂吼一声，扑向原木的尽头。可他如今已在其够不着的地方，安全了，只是又要同急流的神力较量一番。

===Original===
He fought with all his strength and slowly worked his way into the shore. The guide came running toward him: the dog﻿—fifty feet farther down﻿—pulled himself up, dripping and exhausted, on the rocky margin. Hugh caught at the overhanging bushes and slowly he gained the bank. And with a queer, dim smile he set the lamb down beside the ewe.

It seemed endless moments before he felt able to speak. His breath seemed gone, he felt weak as a child, his muscles ached and his wet clothes chilled him, yet he felt strangely, deeply happy. He didn’t know why. He was too tired for introspection. He only knew a great, unfamiliar joy, an inner peace.

“Don’t wait any longer for me,” he said at last when he got his breath. Pete looked down at him in amazement. Hugh smiled into his dark eyes.

===Chinese===
他拼尽全力搏斗，慢慢挣到岸边。向导朝他奔来；那条狗——在五十英尺更下游的地方——浑身滴着水、筋疲力尽地爬上了乱石的岸缘。休攀住低垂的灌木，缓缓上了岸。他带着一丝古怪而恍惚的笑，把羔羊放到母羊身旁。

仿佛过了无穷无尽的时刻，他才觉得能够开口。他的气仿佛断了，人虚弱得像个孩子，肌肉酸痛，湿衣冰凉地贴在身上，然而他感到一种奇异的、深深的快乐。他不知道为什么。他累得无力自省。他只知道一股巨大的、陌生的欢喜，一种内心的安宁。

「别再等我了，」他缓过气来，终于说道。皮特惊愕地低头望着他。休迎着他那双黑眼睛笑了笑。

===Original===
“What you mean?” Pete asked in bewilderment.

Hugh smiled again but felt too tired to explain. There was no use of explanations: he didn’t know that he could find words for them. For the moment he had lost faith in words: only deeds mattered now. He didn’t seem to be able to tell why Hugh Gaylord, the son of wealth and of cities, should yield himself to such folly. The body of the dead herder still lay across the horse’s back: the fact that another week might find himself in the same position could not matter either.

“You’re to go on alone,” he explained quite clearly. “I’m going to stay here﻿—until someone comes up and takes my place﻿—and watch the sheep.”

===Chinese===
「啥意思？」皮特一头雾水地问。

休又笑了笑，却累得无心解释。解释也无用：他不知道自己能不能找出话来解释。这一刻他已对言语失了信心：如今要紧的只有行动。他似乎说不出，为什么休·盖洛德——财富与城市之子——竟会把自己交给这样的痴举。死去牧羊人的遗体还横在马背上；再过一星期，说不定自己也会落得同样的姿势，这一点同样无关紧要了。

「你一个人接着赶路，」他解释得清清楚楚。「我要留在这儿——直到有人上来接了我的位置——看守羊群。」

===Original===
For Hugh knew the truth at last. A new power, a greater strength had risen within him. His eyes saw clear at last. In that wild moment in the heart of the stream he had given service, he had risked all for a cause. None of his old, soft delights had yielded one part of the pleasure that had been his as his strong strokes braved the current; no false flattery had ever been so satisfying as his victory over Broken Fang. It was service, it was conquest, it was manhood at last.

He had no sense of self-sacrifice as he made his decision to stay with the sheep. The joy of strong deeds does not lie in self-pity. Rather it was an inner knowledge that he had found happiness﻿—at least the beginnings of it﻿—and only a fool would throw it away. He had no wish to forego the first pleasure that life had ever given him.

He started away into the forest with his sheep.

===Chinese===
因为休终于认清了真相。一种新的力量、一股更大的气力已在他心中升起。他的眼睛终于看得清了。在河心那狂烈的一刻，他作出了奉献，他为一项事业押上了全部。他从前那些软绵绵的欢愉，没有一样给过他一分那样的快乐——那是他有力的划水搏击水流时所尝到的；任何虚妄的谀辞也不曾像战胜断牙这样令他餍足。那是奉献，是征服，是终于成就的男子气概。

他作出与羊群留下的决定时，没有半点自我牺牲之感。壮行烈举之乐，不在自怜里。毋宁说那是一种内心的了悟：他找到了幸福——至少是幸福的开端——只有傻子才会把它扔掉。生活有生以来头一回给他的这份快乐，他无意舍弃。

他带着他的羊群，动身走进森林去了。

## X / 第十章

===Original===
No observer could make any mistake about Spot’s position in the flock. Through all the maneuvers of the great band he kept the foremost place; and the other sheep followed him without question. The young and inexperienced herdsman thought at first that he was the old “bellwether” of the flock.

But in a very few moments he had to reconstruct some of his ideas. In the first place Spot was not old, and in the second he was obviously not a wether. He was not wrinkled and withered like the old ewes: sheep that had yielded their wool for many seasons. To Hugh’s eyes he looked like a yearling ram; and rather amazing horns had begun to curve from his bold, rugged head.

Hugh was considerably puzzled. He was not a sheepman, but for the simple reason that there were no other yearling rams in the flock he believed that most of the previous season’s crop of male lambs had been sold as wethers to the markets. The mature lambs were obviously kept apart until the breeding season in late fall or winter. Some exception had thus been made in the case of Spot. And Hugh found himself watching the sturdy sheep with a singular interest.

In the first place the animal lacked the uniform color of the other sheep. His basic color was neither white nor black, but a decided dark brown with white at the backs of the legs and the rump. This variation of color immediately won him his name. He was the only sheep in the flock with horns, and in some vague way the man realized that the outline of his figure was entirely different from that of the ewes.

===Chinese===
任谁观察，也不会弄错斑点在这羊群里的位置。大群进退腾挪、种种调度之间，他始终占着头一份；其余的羊毫无异议地跟着他走。这位年轻而无经验的牧羊人起初还当，他是羊群里那头老的『头羊』（bellwether）。

可只消片刻，他就不得不修正自己的一些想法了。头一件，斑点并不老；第二件，他分明不是阉羊。他不似那些老母羊皮皱毛枯——那是出过许多个季节羊毛的羊。在休眼里，他像是一岁口的公羊；那颗神气而粗犷的脑袋上，已经弯出两支相当惊人的角来。

休好生纳闷。他不是羊倌出身，可单凭群里再没有旁的一岁公羊这一层，他便认定上一季的公羊羔大半阉了、当作阉羊卖进了市场。长成个儿的公羊显然另行隔开，留待深秋或冬天的配种季。斑点这一头，显然破了个例。于是休发现自己怀着一种异样的兴味，端详起这头结实的羊来。

头一样，这畜生没有别的羊那样一律的毛色。他的底色既非白、也非黑，而是一种分明的深棕，腿后和臀上缀着白。这一处毛色的变异，立刻替他赢得了名字。他是全群唯一带角的羊；而且不知怎么隐隐约约地，这人觉出他身形的轮廓，同母羊们全然是两回事。

===Original===
Although he was massively and boldly built, there was an actual grace about his motions not to be seen in the ewes. His form looked lean and hard, for all its massive development, and he stood several good inches above the tallest ewe in the flock. Hugh found it hard to believe, however, that he was some imported, high-bred yearling ram that would later be used to help build up the standard of the flock. In spite of his exceptional height and strength, his trim body probably weighed less than some of the fat ewes, his wool was not unusual, and certainly one of his rugged, agile form would not yield tender mutton. The man found it increasingly difficult to explain his presence in the flock.

Hugh tried to head him off, only to find that he was surprisingly quick on his feet. Only because his flock were unable to follow him and the young ram whipped back to join them was Hugh able to round him up at all. And when he darted off in a half-circle the whole band came pell-mell after him as if, young as he was, they were afraid to trust themselves without his guidance.

The longer Hugh looked at him, the more he was marked from all the other sheep in the flock. Of course it was possible that the mere fact that he was a male of the species was enough thus to distinguish him; yet ignorant though he was of the ways of sheep, Hugh found this explanation insufficient. In the first place Spot was just a yearling and evidently not yet mature. He could not imagine some of those strong-minded old ewes trusting their lives and fortunes to any ordinary immature ram. But this creature had a carriage not to be seen in the others, seemingly a spirit and pride and self-reliance that didn’t quite fit in with the nature of domestic sheep. His very stride revealed a bold, fearless, domineering disposition.

He did not graze easily, as did most of his followers. He skipped about across the whole front of the flock, now perching upon some lofty crag, now scampering up a narrow trail where the flock could scarcely follow. But in the hot, still late afternoon, as the sheep were beginning to stir after a long rest in the forest shade, Spot gave him even further cause to wonder.

===Chinese===
他骨架雄阔、生相勇悍，举止间却自有一种母羊身上见不出的真正的优雅。尽管体格雄壮，那身形看去却精瘦硬扎；他立在那里，比群里顶高的母羊还高出好几英寸。然而休很难相信，他是什么外来的高种一岁公羊、日后要用来改良羊群品位的。别看他身量膂力超群，他那精干的身体，分量说不定还不如几只肥母羊；他的毛也并无出奇之处；而他这样一副粗犷矫健的身架，断然出不得细嫩的羊肉。这人越发难以解释，他缘何身在这羊群里。

休想把他截住，却发现他脚下快得出奇。只因他手下的羊群跟不上，那小公羊又折身回来归了队，休才算把他拢住。而当他撒开步子兜起半个圈子，整群羊便乱纷纷一拥而上跟了过去——仿佛他年纪虽小，离了他的引领，它们便不敢放心自己行走似的。

休越端详他，越见他同群里其余的羊判然有别。当然，也许单凭他是这一族里的雄的，便足以这般出众；可是休虽不谙羊的习性，也觉得这解释不够。头一条，斑点不过一岁口，分明尚未长成。他没法想象，那几只主意极硬的老母羊，肯把身家性命托给一头寻常的、未成的公羊。可这畜生自有一副别的羊身上见不出的仪态，仿佛怀着一股家羊天性里装不下的精魂、骄傲与自恃。连他那步子，都透出一副大胆、无畏、称雄的性子。

他不像他手下大多数的羊那样安安分分地啃草。他在羊群整个前头蹦来跳去，忽而登上某处高岩，忽而窜上一条羊群几乎跟不上的窄径。可到了炎热而沉静的午后时分，羊群在林荫里歇了长长一觉、开始蠢蠢而动的时候，斑点更叫休添了一层惊奇。

===Original===
To Hugh, it seemed the hottest hour of the day. His human sense, however, misled him: in reality, the highest point had been reached, and the cool winds from off the snow fields were on their way to bring relief. The sheep were still resting in a compact band: Spot, at the extreme front rank of the flock, had climbed upon a little rocky eminence on the hillside. It was such a promontory as is to be seen often in the high Rockies, an outcropping of the rocky skeleton of the hill emerging﻿—like the end of a broken bone through the flesh﻿—from the brown earth of the hillside. It was such a place as the wolf chooses for her lair, and the poison people﻿—the gray speckled rattlesnakes whose bite is death﻿—like to stretch out upon through the long, still afternoons. Because it overlooked the whole glen and was at the edge of the stony hillside where he had been doomed to live, Running Feet, the coyote, found it a convenient place to look for game.

Why Spot had chosen such a place for his rest Hugh could not guess. It was not shaded and comfortable like the glen beneath. Nor did he lie down like the ewes below him, but stood alert, head up, nostrils open to receive any message that the wind might bring. Hugh found himself watching him with fascinated interest. It seemed to him that the animal was on guard over the flock, even as he himself and the dog﻿—a watchful sentinel for any danger that might threaten them.

If it were true, he had a right to his wonder. The domestic animals do not ordinarily appoint one of their number as a sentinel. They have depended on the protection of men for so many generations that this habit has died from the breeds. The wild creatures, however, still retain it. If indeed the ram should detect danger for the flock, there was no conceivable refuge for them, so there could have been no conscious intelligence behind the act. It was obviously a long-remembered instinct. Any of the fleet-footed hunters of the wild could overtake them, and the slaying was easy even for a little lynx, biting sure and true.

No one knew this fact any better than Running Feet, the coyote, emerging from the brushy thickets of the hillside in late afternoon. His second name was Coward﻿—cowardice was part of the curse that Manitou had put upon him﻿—but even a seasonal fawn would be brave enough to attack a sheep. It might be, approaching in the shelter of the thickets, he could get close enough to the flock to steal a lamb or ewe from its flanks.

===Chinese===
在休看来，这仿佛是一天里最热的时辰。然而他人类的感官哄骗了他：实际上，热的顶点已经到头，雪原上来的凉风正在赶来的路上。羊群仍拢作紧紧的一团歇着：斑点在羊群最前排，已经爬上山坡一座小小的石阜。那是高落基山里常常见到的那等岬岩，山体岩石的骨架破土而出——像折断的骨头自血肉里戳出头来——从山坡棕色的泥土间露出。这样的地方，母狼爱拣来作窝；毒族（poison people）——灰斑点点、咬一口便是一条命的响尾蛇——爱在漫长而沉静的午后摊开身子躺着。因为这地方俯瞰整条幽谷，又正当那片多石山坡的边上——那山坡是疾足命中注定栖身之地——郊狼疾足便觉得，这是个觅猎的便当去处。

斑点缘何拣了这么个地方歇息，休猜不透。这地方不像脚底下的幽谷，又荫凉又安逸。他也不似下方的母羊们卧倒，而是警醒地立着，昂着头，鼻孔张开，承纳风里可能捎来的任何音信。休发现自己入了迷似的望着他。在他看来，这畜生是在替羊群放哨——同他自己和那条狗一样——一个提防种种威胁的、警醒的哨兵。

倘若这是真的，他那份惊奇便惊得有理。家畜寻常并不从自己群里推举一个哨兵。它们倚赖人的保护倚赖了这么多代，这习性早已从各族群里死尽了。野性的生灵却还留着它。即便这头公羊真替羊群察出了危险，它们也没有一条想得出来的退路，所以这举动背后谈不上有什么自觉的智虑。那分明是一桩铭记久远的本能。荒野里随便哪个飞毛腿的猎手都追得上它们；这杀戮的营生，就连一头小小的猞猁做来也不难——一口咬下，又准又稳。

这层道理，谁也不比疾足知道得更透——那头郊狼，此刻正趁着午后较晚的时分，从山坡的灌木密丛里钻出来。他的第二个名字叫懦夫——懦弱正是马尼图加在他身上那道诅咒的一部分——可就连一只当年生的幼鹿，也壮得起胆子来攻击一只羊。说不定，借着密丛的遮蔽一路挨近，他能贴到羊群近旁，从侧翼叼走一只羊羔或一只母羊。

===Original===
It was not that he had forgotten the shepherd dogs that kept watch, or the herder with his gun. Running Feet was never able to forget things like these. Part of his curse was a far-reaching and calculating intelligence almost equal to that of a dog, a trait that would have been a tremendous advantage if he had been blessed with courage to go with it, but which to a coward meant only realization of a thousand dangers to make life a torment. He understood perfectly what displeasing treatment would be his if the dogs came upon him at the killing. Not even Broken Fang, the puma, can always protect himself against the onslaught of the Barking One on guard over the flocks. He is fast as lightning and as terrible as the she-wolves at the lair mouth. And Running Feet knew quite well the deadly qualities of the glittering stick that the herder carried in the hollow of his arm.

Today, however, there was only one dog, and he worked on the far side of the flock. The herder rested under a tree; if Running Feet kept to the shelter of the brush, there was nothing to fear from him. True, he would be obliged to dip out into the open to seize one of the lambs, but if fortune only permitted them to lie close enough to the edge of the thicket, he could do it without danger. Fortune wasn’t usually good to Running Feet; but today perhaps his luck had turned. And at that point in his wolfine thoughts he made out the figure of Spot, in tireless vigil on the highest point of the rock pile.

It was too good to be true. The distance was full two hundred yards from the herder; the dog was equally distant. The brush went clear to the base of the rocks; one or two little leaps into the open would put him within killing range. Then one or two slashing bites with his white fangs﻿—and *that* would complete a perfect afternoon.

Running Feet looked the ground over carefully. He didn’t want to make any avoidable mistakes. It might be that the young ram would dash down and into the flock at the first sight of him, and although he could overtake any domestic sheep in a short run, it might necessitate a chase into the clearing and some danger from the herder’s rifle. The wise course was to circle about the promontory, keeping as close as possible to the shadow of the thicket, and advance up the trail between Spot and his flock.

===Chinese===
倒不是他忘了那些看守着的牧羊犬，忘了带枪的牧羊人。这类事，疾足从来忘不掉。他那道诅咒里还有一条：一副深远而精于盘算的智力，几乎赶得上一条狗——这品性，他若得天独厚、配上相称的胆量，本是天大的便宜；可落在懦夫身上，便只意味着识破一千样危险，把日子熬成一场苦刑。他完全明白，行凶时倘若叫狗们扑上，等着他的是怎样一番不客气的款待。就连断牙——那头美洲狮——也不能回回护得住自己，躲过守卫羊群的吠者（the Barking One）的猛攻。那一位快如闪电，凶起来像守在洞口的母狼。而牧羊人臂弯里抱的那杆亮闪闪的夺命棍有怎样的致命能耐，疾足也清楚得很。

然而今天只有一条狗，而且在羊群那一头做工。牧羊人在一棵树底下歇着；只要疾足守着灌木的遮蔽不走开，就没什么可怕的。不错，他总得蹿到亮处才抓得着羊羔；可只要时运肯叫羊羔们卧得离密丛边子足够近，他便能毫无凶险地得手。时运平日待疾足不厚；可今天，说不定他的运气转了。而他这狼魂里的念头正想到这里，便认出了斑点的身影——正在那堆乱石的至高点上，不知疲倦地守望。

这好得简直不像是真的。离牧羊人足有两百码；狗也是一样远。灌木一直长到岩石根下；往亮处蹿上一两个小跳，他便进了杀程。然后白牙一两口狠劈——*那*一下，便成全了一个完美的下午。

疾足把地形细细打量了一番。他不愿犯任何可以避免的错处。说不定那小公羊第一眼望见他，便会冲下山、扎进羊群里去；虽说短程之内他追得上任何一只家羊，可那便免不了要一路追进林间空地，从牧羊人的步枪上招来几分危险。稳当的路数是绕着那处岬岩兜个圈子，尽量贴着密丛的影子，再顺着斑点与他的羊群之间的那条小径往上推进。

===Original===
The wind was right, the shadows were long and strange, and even Running Feet the coward could see no chance of failure. He crept slowly from the thicket, a gray shadow few wilderness eyes were keen enough to see. His white fangs gleamed, the blood leaped in his veins. He made the stalk with complete success: Hugh had not been able to tell what living form had stirred the thicket at the base of Spot’s lookout. And then the gray killer came bounding up the rocky trail.

Hugh saw him then. It was only a gray glimpse: by no possible chance could he have found a target for his rifle. The distance was far, the coyote’s body half-obscured. He had only one thought: that Spot was doomed beyond any power of his to save him. Already, it seemed to him, he had developed a real affection for the self-reliant, spirited ram; and he had a sense of acute personal loss as he read its doom. To lose the brave leader of the flock in his first day of service! No event in his life had ever caused more regret.

No instruments may measure the speed of the human mind in a second of crisis. The glimpse of the charging coyote was of infinitesimal duration, yet Hugh had time in plenty for an overpowering wave of regret and rage. The time he lacked, however, was that for muscular response. There was none whatever to raise his rifle and take aim.

And at that instant his regrets were cut short. He suddenly shouted with delight. Spot was not to die so tamely﻿—in the fangs of Running Feet. All at once the young ram snapped about in the trail, making no attempt whatever to flee into the thickets on the other side of the rock pile. And he lowered his head in a posture of defense.

===Chinese===
风向对头，影子又长又古怪，连懦夫疾足也看不出半点失手的可能。他慢慢地从密丛里爬出来，一道灰影，荒野里少有几双眼睛尖得能瞧见。他的白牙闪着光，血在血管里直跳。他这一趟潜近做得滴水不漏：休根本没能看出，是什么活物惊动了斑点瞭望台底下的那片密丛。紧接着，那灰色的杀手便顺着岩石小径腾跃而上。

休这才望见了他。那不过是灰影一闪：他无论如何也寻不出一个能搁步枪打的目标。距离太远，郊狼的身子又掩去了一半。他心里只剩一个念头：斑点是劫数难逃了，他再没有半点力道救他。仿佛连他自己都不曾察觉，他已对这头自恃而有神采的公羊生出了真感情；此刻读出它的死劫，他有一种切身的痛失之感。上任头一天，便折了羊群那位勇敢的头领！他平生还没有哪一桩事，叫他这样懊恨过。

世上没有一件仪器，量得出危殆一秒钟里人心的速度。郊狼扑来那一瞥，短得微不足道，休却有的是工夫，掀起懊恨与狂怒一阵压倒一切的巨浪。可他缺的工夫，是肌肉应命的工夫。端起步枪、瞄上一瞄的工夫，一分一毫也没有。

而就在这一瞬，他的懊恨被拦腰斩断。他欢喜得突然喊出了声。斑点不会就这么窝囊地死去了——死在疾足的牙口里。猛然间，小公羊在小径上霍地转过身来，丝毫没有要往石堆那头的密丛里逃的意思。他压低了头，摆出一副防御的架势。

===Original===
For long seconds he stood motionless, statuesque, his horns ready for the coyote’s onslaught. There was something masterful, noble about the posture, not at all to be expected in the timid and defenseless domestic sheep. And the coyote drew up short in the trail.

The other sheep had sprung up, by now, and were crowding away from the rock pile: the dog sprang forward, barking, at the other side of the flock. Hugh stood waiting for a chance to aim his weapon. And still the tableau on the rocks remained unchanged: the young ram with lowered head, the coyote﻿—his blood turning to milk inside of him﻿—on the trail.

Hugh’s astonishment was nothing compared to the coyote’s own. He had expected flight, panic﻿—anything on earth except an actual attitude of self-defense. Just for a moment he stood motionless, snarling, trying to find courage to attack. But not for nothing had Manitou put his curse upon him.

All at once it came to him that he had made a mistake. There was something familiar about the sturdy figure, the lowered head, the curling horns. He remembered certain passes in the High Rockies﻿—and various trim, horned creatures that might occasionally be met there. Even Broken Fang did not care to meet these people on a narrow trail﻿—and Running Feet remembered with some haste that he had an appointment on the other side of the hill.

===Chinese===
好几秒钟，他纹丝不动地立着，如雕像一般，双角备好了迎郊狼的猛扑。这架势里有一种威风而高贵的东西，在怯懦而无以自卫的家羊身上，是万万料不到的。郊狼便在小径上猛然收住了脚。

其余的羊这时都已跳起身来，挤挤挨挨地躲开石堆：狗在羊群另一头吠着扑上前去。休立在那里，等一个瞄准的机会。石堆上那幅画却依然如故：小公羊低着头；郊狼——血在他体内化成了奶——僵在小径上。

休的惊讶，比起郊狼自己的惊讶来，又算不得什么了。他原等着的是逃窜、是惊溃——天底下什么都想到了，独独没有想到一副实实在在的自卫姿态。有一小会儿，他一动不动地立着，低声咆哮，想寻出一口攻击的胆量来。可马尼图把诅咒加在他身上，原不是白白加的。

蓦地，他醒悟到自己错了。那结实的身形、低垂的头、卷曲的角，有几分眼熟。他记起高落基山里的几处山口——还有在那里偶尔撞得见的、种种精瘦带角的生灵。就连断牙，也不乐意在窄径上会一会这些角色——于是疾足相当匆忙地想起，山坡那一头自己还有个约要赴。

===Original===
At that instant Hugh shot. The distance was far; the bullet whizzed hot along Running Feet’s shoulder. He didn’t wait for a second shot. He turned and fled at the fastest pace he knew. And with the air whizzing past him he wholly missed the curious words that the herder uttered﻿—the strange remark that he made to Spot, still standing defiantly with lowered horns on the trail.

“Good Lord, Spot,” he cried, “you’re not just a sheep. Sure as I live, you’re a﻿—” But he didn’t finish telling what Spot was. Perhaps he didn’t know; and the ideas that were glowing dimly in his brain did not yet take the form of a concrete thought. But more likely his attention was merely called away. For at that instant he saw his camp-tender advancing slowly up the trail.

===Chinese===
就在那一瞬，休开了枪。距离很远；子弹灼灼地贴着疾足的肩头呼啸而过。他没等第二枪。他转过身去，拿自己所知最快的步子逃了。风声在耳边呼呼掠过，牧羊人说出的那几句古怪话，他全然没有听见——那是他对斑点说的，对那头依旧低着双角、昂然不屈立在径上的斑点。

「老天爷，斑点，」他喊道，「你可不单单是一只羊。我拿性命担保，你是一头——」可他终究没有说完斑点是什么。也许他自己也不知道；他脑子里幽幽发亮的那些念头，还没有凝成一个具体的想法。可多半不过是他注意力被引开了。因为就在那一瞬，他望见他的送补人正顺着小径缓缓走上来。

## XI / 第十一章

===Original===
Hugh Gaylord had, like all men, experienced some rather violent surprises in his time. They had been coming exceptionally thick and fast since he had come to Smoky Land. His changed attitude toward life and his behavior in regard to the flocks had been amazing experiences in themselves. He had just experienced a rather violent shock on beholding the warlike behavior of Spot. The previous night he had been through the unusual experience of finding the body of a murdered man in a tent. But he suddenly realized that he was facing the most amazing situation of all.
Death, after all, in some manner or other comes to all living creatures. Hugh had no real reason for amazement at that still form in the herder’s tent. He was ready to confess he didn’t know a great deal about sheep: possibly Spot’s episode with the coyote would not have so surprised a more experienced herder. But now he felt as if a number of his preconceived ideas had been violently knocked out of him, and that is always dumbfounding. His camp-tender was not a Mexican, nor yet a laboring man of a certain type, but a girl.
He had seen some thousands of girls in his life, yet he found himself staring at the slim form on the horse as if he were gazing upon a miracle of nature. Every experience of the past few days might have been in some manner expected and he had only reacted because of qualities within himself; his experiences with Broken Fang and the coyote were wholly fitting in this wild, mountainous land, but he had never dreamed but that he had left all womankind a thousand miles behind him. This was a man’s land, not a place for the tender flesh of girls. This was the home of savage beasts, a region of dark forests and forbidding peaks, never a land of gentleness and ease such as women should know. He realized with a start that evidently his good manners had been left behind him also, for he was gazing at her in open-mouthed astonishment.
If the girl on the horse had been an Indian squaw, even an aged and wrinkled frontiersman’s dame, he would have felt that the axis of the world still stood at the proper angle. But this girl was white, in spite of her tan and the high color﻿—not put on with a rabbit’s foot﻿—in her cheeks. She was young, not more than twenty-two at most. And strangest of all she was pretty past all denial.
Hugh felt as if there must be some mistake. Perhaps in these lonely days in the hills he had lost the power of discrimination. He hadn’t seen a girl for endless weeks﻿—centuries they seemed to him﻿—and he had heard that such isolation affects the point of view. The girl might be white; by a long chance she might even be young; but by no possible circumstance had she a right to be pretty. Beauty dwelt in far cities, in gentle lands and distant, not in these rugged mountains. Yet the truth of his first observation became ever more apparent.
He steadied himself, closed his mouth, and tried to stand at his ease. The girl swung down from the horse. That motion, graceful as the leap of a deer, explained in a measure the mystery. It revealed a suppleness, a strength and litheness of body such as might stand the test of existence even on the frontier.

===Chinese===
休·盖洛德同所有的男人一样，一生中也经历过几桩相当剧烈的意外。自打来到烟之乡，这样的意外来得格外又密又急。他对人生态度的转变、他对羊群的种种行止，本身就已是令人惊叹的经历。方才目睹斑点那副好斗的架势，他算是吃了不小的一惊。前一天夜里，他又经历了一桩不寻常的事：在一顶帐篷里发现一个被人谋害的死者。可此刻他蓦然醒悟，自己正面对的，是所有经历里最叫人瞠目结舌的一桩。
死亡，说到底，总要循着这样那样的路数降临到一切生灵头上。对牧羊人帐篷里那具僵卧不动的躯体，休原本没有什么真正值得惊愕的地方。他也乐于承认，他对羊懂不了多少：斑点同郊狼的那一段公案，若是落在更老练的牧羊人眼里，未必会惹起这样大的惊奇。可此刻他觉得，自己心里一长串先入为主的成见像是被人狠狠地一拳一个打了出去，而这种事向来叫人哑然失措。他的送补人不是墨西哥人，也不是那一类做工的汉子，而是一个姑娘。
平生他见过的姑娘何止几千，此刻却发现自己直勾勾地望着马背上那纤细的身影，仿佛在瞻望一件造化的奇迹。过去几天的种种遭遇，多少都还可以料想，他之所以为之震动，只因自己性子里的根由；他与断牙的遭遇、与郊狼的遭遇，同这片荒蛮的山地原本再相称不过；可他做梦也不曾想过别的可能——他一直认定，自己早已把整个女性世界撂在千里之外了。这是男人的土地，不是安放姑娘家柔嫩血肉的地方。这是猛兽的家园，是黑森林与险峰绝壁之乡，绝不是女人家该识得的那种温存安逸的土地。他猛然惊觉，自己的好教养显然也一并被撂在了身后，因为他正张着嘴，愣愣地望着她。
倘若马背上的姑娘是个印第安女人，哪怕是拓荒人家上了年纪、满脸皱纹的婆娘，他也会觉得世界的轴线仍旧立得端正。可这姑娘是白人，任凭她一身晒痕，任凭颊上那片红晕——那可不是拿兔脚涂抹上去的。她年轻，至多不过二十二岁。而最奇的是，她漂亮得不容置辩。
休觉得这里头准是出了什么差错。也许在山里这些孤寂的日子里，他失去了辨别的能力。数不清的几个星期——在他简直像几个世纪——他没见过一个姑娘；他也听说过，这样的孤绝会影响人的眼光。这姑娘或许是白人；再退一万步，兴许还年轻；可无论如何，她也没有权利生得漂亮。美住在遥远的城市里，住在温柔而辽远的地方，不住在这些粗砺的山里。然而，他第一眼所见是真实的，这一点却越来越显豁了。
他定住神，合上嘴，想装出一副从容的样子站好。姑娘翻身下了马。那动作翩然如鹿跃，把这桩谜团解释开了几分。它显出一种柔韧，一副强健轻捷的身骨——那样的身骨，就是在边疆上也经得起生存的磨炼。

===Original===
Hugh noted that she was a slender girl, rather tall, that she wore a soft felt hat over her chestnut hair, and that she had dark eyes under rather marvelous brows. Hugh was no amateur in regard to women. Now that he had regained his self-control he made a swift and unerring appraisal; yet found his amazement deepening at every instant. There was a freshness, an appeal about her slight figure, suggesting perfect health and superb physical development rather than weakness, and he was not blind to the gentleness and breeding in her soft features.
She seemed perfectly composed, wholly at ease. In her simplicity she found no embarrassment under his frank gaze. “Where’s Dan?” she asked.
Hugh straightened, somewhat startled. He had expected some sort of a formal greeting, a few words in apology or introduction, not this straight-out, uncompromising “Where’s Dan?” It seemed to him she acted somewhat suspicious of him, also.
She had, he observed, a well-bred voice. She spoke in clear, level tones that pleased his ear. The voice was wholly lacking in affectation, but it was simply brimming and vibrant with health and high spirits. Hugh noticed something else, too, and smiled inwardly. He couldn’t remember ever having been spoken to in just that way before. The level, impersonal tone implied an insurmountable social barrier between them. It was a somewhat similar tone, he remembered, to that in which he had occasionally spoken to a servant. In this case he obviously was the inferior.

===Chinese===
休注意到，这姑娘身量纤细，个子颇高，栗色头发上扣着一顶软毡帽，毡帽下一双深色的眼睛，配着两道出奇好看的眉。论女人，休不是外行。这会儿既然稳住了自己，他便做了一番迅捷而分毫不爽的品评；可他的惊异却一刻深似一刻。她那瘦小的身影自有一种鲜润、一种动人处，昭示的是十全的健康与出众的体格，而非孱弱；她眉目温软之间的那点温婉与教养，他也没有看漏。
她显得从容至极，全无半分局促。她天真未凿，在他毫不掩饰的注视下也不见丝毫羞窘。「丹（Dan）在哪儿？」她问。
休挺直了身子，倒有几分错愕。他原指望来一番正式的招呼，几句致歉或是引见的话，而不是这句直挺挺、毫无通融的『丹在哪儿』。而且在他看来，她待他似乎还带着几分疑忌。
他听出她有一副教养良好的嗓音。她吐字清朗，声调平稳，入耳受用。那嗓音里毫无矫揉造作，只是满满当当地漾着、颤着健康与朝气。休还听出了另一层意思，心里暗自一笑。他不记得从前有谁用这样一副腔调同他讲过话。那平稳的、不分亲疏的声调，意味着两人之间隔着一道不可逾越的社会藩篱。他记得，自己从前偶尔便是用这样的声调对仆人说话的。眼下的情形，他分明是居于人下的那一个。

===Original===
He paused and reflected as to the whereabouts of Dan. The truth came to him in a moment. Dan of course referred to his predecessor. “He’s dead,” Hugh answered simply.
For the instant he was frightened. It occurred to him, when the words had gone too far to recall, that he should have been gentler. He might have prepared the girl, in some degree, for the shock. He didn’t want her to faint. But if he had expected any hysteria or excitement he was doomed to a fresh surprise. She opened her eyes; and it seemed to Hugh that she closed her lips﻿—in a fine, hard line﻿—just for an instant. “Dead?” she repeated slowly. “Killed﻿—or did he just die?”
“He was killed,” Hugh answered in the straightforward way of one on a witness stand. “I found him dead in his tent. He’d been shot. The black dog was shot too.”
“And who are you?”

===Chinese===
他顿了一顿，思量丹的下落。真相转念便来了。丹指的，当然是他的前任。「他死了。」休简单地答道。
那一瞬他心里发慌。话已出口、收不回来了，他才想到自己本该温和些。他原可以先给姑娘稍稍打点招呼，让她受个预备。他可不想让她晕过去。但他若是指望她歇斯底里、大惊小怪，那他注定要再吃一惊。她睁大了眼睛；在休看来，她的双唇只是刹那间抿成了一条细细的、坚硬的直线。「死了？」她缓缓地重复道。「被人杀了——还是自己死的？」
「他是被人杀死的。」休答道，口气直截，像证人席上作证的人。「我发现他死在帐篷里。他中了枪。那条黑狗也中了枪。」
「那你是谁？」

===Original===
Hugh wasn’t exactly accustomed to this straight-out sort of questioning, but he mustered his faculties and made his answers. “Gaylord﻿—Hugh Gaylord,” he said simply. “And if you want me to I’ll tell you all I know of this affair.”
“Perhaps that would be the best plan,” she agreed.
“I came over to the camp﻿—to borrow something. I was with another chap﻿—an Indian, Pete. We found this poor beggar dead. Pete started with him into the settlements today. It’s a wonder you didn’t meet him on the trail.”
“He probably took the other fork, heading toward Seven Mile. I came up from Horse Creek.”
As Hugh didn’t know the two places apart, or what they represented, this information did not clear matters up for him to any great extent. “He left me here,” Hugh went on, “and as I didn’t have anything in particular to do﻿—I took care of the sheep﻿—until you could hire a regular herder.”

===Chinese===
这样直来直去的盘问，休实在不大习惯，可他还是打起全副精神，一一作答。「盖洛德——休·盖洛德。」他简单地说。「您要是愿意，这桩事的来历，凡我知道的都可以奉告。」
「那也许是最妥当的办法。」她表示赞同。
「我到这牧羊营来——原是来借点东西。同我一起的还有一个伙计——一个印第安人，皮特。我们发现这可怜的家伙死了。皮特今天带着他动身往村镇去了。您竟没在小径上碰见他，倒真是件怪事。」
「他多半是走了另一条岔道，往七英里（Seven Mile）去了。我是打马溪（Horse Creek）上来的。」
休本就分不清这两处地方，也不知道它们各自是什么名堂，所以这话并没有替他把事情理清多少。「他把我留在这儿，」休接着说，「我手头反正没什么特别要紧的事——便照看起羊群来——等你们雇到一位正牌的牧羊人。」

===Original===
The girl looked puzzled but made no immediate reply. “And you’re not an experienced herder?”
“No. I’ve never worked﻿—I’ve never worked at it before.”
“Then how did you know what to do?”
“I didn’t know. I let ’em do what they wanted to, and followed along.”

===Chinese===
姑娘面露困惑，却没有立刻答话。「这么说，你并不是个老练的牧羊人？」
「不是。我从来没做过工——从来没做过这一行。」
「那你怎么知道该做些什么？」
「我不知道。我由着它们爱怎么便怎么，跟在后头走就是了。」

===Original===
Then the girl laughed﻿—for the first time. It was a tinkling, musical sound, inexpressibly girlish, and Hugh laughed boyishly himself. It was their first real moment of understanding; and it seemed to Hugh that a new impulse, a curious sense of impending events, a new stir and vitality had been born in the air. He might have wondered at the freshness and happiness in his own laugh, as much as in hers. It was an hour of miracles.
“You couldn’t have done better﻿—and I needn’t tell you that you’ve probably saved me﻿—and my father﻿—hundreds of dollars. The coyotes and the wolves would have been busy all night and today. I’d be glad enough to pay you well for this time you’ve spent﻿—and give you a steady job if you want it.”
She spoke perfectly naturally, and Hugh knew that his torn and soiled clothes and his unshaven face had done their work. Obviously she never guessed his true position. He wondered how she explained his presence in the hills and his reasons for staying with the sheep.
She did have her own theories, but they were far indeed from the truth. Her mind leaped at once to what seemed to her the most plausible explanation﻿—that Hugh was a humble white man, friend of Pete’s, possibly a laborer out of work, or a hungry wanderer from the East. He had taken the herder’s place in hopes of securing a permanent position when the camp-tender returned.

===Chinese===
这时姑娘笑了——头一回。那是一串叮咚的、乐音似的声响，说不出的少女气，休自己也孩子气地笑了。这是两人头一遭真正会心的时刻；在休感觉里，空气中仿佛新生了一股冲动、一种对临头之事的奇妙预感、一阵新的躁动与生气。他自己笑声里那份鲜快与欢喜，原不下于她那一串笑，他本该为此诧异的。这是奇迹的时辰。
「你做得再好不过了——也不消我说，你多半替我省下了——替我父亲省下了——几百块钱。不然的话，郊狼和狼昨夜今天都要忙个不亦乐乎。你这一回花掉的工夫，我乐意好好酬谢——你要是愿意，还能给你一份固定的差事。」
她说得再自然不过，休也明白，自己那身破烂肮脏的衣裳、那张没有刮过的脸，已经尽了它们的功效。她显然从没猜到他的真实身份。他心里纳闷，她把他到山里来这件事、把他留在羊群跟前的缘由，都是怎么解释的。
她自有她的一套猜想，只是离真相实在遥远。她的心思一下子便跳到了她看来最说得通的解释上——休是个地位低微的白人，皮特的朋友，多半是个失了业的做工的，或是东部来的、饿着肚子的流浪汉。他顶了牧羊人的缺，指望送补人回来时，能给自己谋下一个长久的位置。

===Original===
“Before I decide to stay,” Hugh replied steadily, “I’d like to know a few things.”
“We’ll pay you two dollars a day﻿—and furnish you with supplies,” she assured him soberly.
Hugh did not smile. After all, the wage was an important consideration. The girl was evidently a partner with her father in this sheep-raising venture, and possibly for the sake of economy but probably because of the acute shortage of labor (Hugh had not forgotten the Indian’s words) had worked as camp-tender herself. Her perfect health, her strong, lithe body, a skill with horses and a wholesome scarcity of nonsense in her disposition had enabled her to fill the position well and in all probability to enjoy it.
Hugh studied her face with growing interest. In his sphere of life girls did not drive trains of pack horses into the rugged hills, do a man’s work in the open, have dealings with uneducated herders, and still laugh like silver bells.

===Chinese===
「在我定夺留不留之前，」休沉静地答道，「我想先知道几件事。」
「我们一天付你两块钱——口粮也由我们供给。」她郑重地向他担保。
休没有笑。这工钱到底是一层要紧的考虑。这姑娘显然同她父亲合伙经营着这桩养羊的事业，也许是图节省，但多半是因为劳力奇缺（印第安人的话休没有忘记）——便亲自充任了送补人。她十全的健康、她强健轻捷的身骨、一身驾马的本事，再加上性子里那份难得的少废话、不胡闹，都使她把这差事当得妥妥帖帖，而且十有八九还当得快活。
休怀着愈来愈浓的兴味端详她的脸。在他那个生活圈子里，姑娘家不会赶着一队驮马深入崎岖的山地，不会在露天下干男人的活计，不会同没受过教育的牧羊人打交道，却还照样笑得像银铃。

===Original===
She wore, he noticed, a rather heavy revolver slung at her hip. Her hand was small and shapely, but it was also brown and firm. They would make, Hugh thought, a rather dangerous combination. The eyes, wide apart and bright, looked unusually healthy and clear, and Hugh imagined that they could see quite straight over revolver sights. The man understood why she had been able to ply her occupation in safety. Woe to the herder that would presume upon their isolation!
“Labor is scarce, I suppose?” Hugh asked. What he was really trying to find out was how long this position of sheep herder would be thrust upon him. He had yielded himself to enough folly for one day, and he had no intention of committing himself to a position as sheep herder for the rest of his natural life. As soon as they could find a substitute﻿—but Hugh didn’t finish the sentence. He suddenly realized that thence on he had no plans.
The girl looked up, rather sober of face. “*Good* labor is very scarce,” she agreed honestly. “But we can’t pay more than two dollars a day. You see﻿—you’re inexperienced.”
Secretly he thought that she was bluffing, that she would pay a much higher price to retain him as shepherd of the flock. But he didn’t voice the thought. “Two dollars a day is all right,” he said. “That wasn’t what I was going to ask you. There’s some other things I want to know﻿—that I feel I have a right to know. That man was murdered, and the guide thought it was because of a fight between the sheepmen and cattlemen. I don’t care to have someone come up here and find me murdered, too.”

===Chinese===
他还注意到，她腰胯上挎着一柄相当沉重的左轮枪。她的手小巧好看，却也晒得黑棕、握得结实。这两样凑在一处，休想，怕是一副相当危险的搭配。那双眼睛生得开阔明亮，看上去格外健康清澈，休想象得出，它们顺着左轮枪的准星望出去，是望得笔直的。这人明白了，她的差事何以能当得平平安安。哪个牧羊人若敢趁着这份荒山孤绝起歹念，那他可就要遭殃了！
「劳力很缺，我猜？」休问。他真正想探明的，是这牧羊人的位子还要强加在他身上多久。这一天里由着性子疯够了就是够了，他无意把余生都许给牧羊人这个行当。只等他们找到个接替的人——可这句他没有想完。他突然明白，打这会儿起，他再没有什么打算了。
姑娘抬起眼来，面色颇为郑重。「『好』劳力才真是缺得很。」她老老实实地表示同意，「可我们出不起一天两块以上的工钱。你要明白——你还没有经验。」
私下里他想，她这是在唬人；真要把他留下看羊，她肯出的价码要高得多。但这话他没有说出口。「一天两块钱行。」他说，「我要问的不是这个。另外还有几件事我想弄明白——我觉得我有权利弄明白。那个人是被人谋害的，向导认为，那是牧羊人与牧牛人相争的缘故。我可不想有朝一日，有人上山来发现我也遭了谋害。」

===Original===
The girl seemed distressed. It was the first time since their meeting that she seemed to lack words. Then she looked up fearlessly.
“I wish I could tell you differently,” she said. “A sheep woman has no right to be honest, in these days. The Indian told you the truth. Dan was murdered, not for personal reasons, but because the cattlemen﻿—a little, evil group of them﻿—want to destroy this flock of sheep﻿—just why I’ll tell you later. And that’s the chance you must take.”
“It’s a real chance?”
Again she flinched. “They seem to be willing to go to any lengths to beat us.”

===Chinese===
姑娘似乎很是为难。这是两人相遇以来，她头一回像是没了言语。随后，她毫无惧色地抬起头来。
「我但愿能告诉你另一个说法。」她说，「这年头，养羊的女人没有说实话的权利。印第安人告诉你的都是实情。丹是被谋杀的，不为私仇，而是因为牧牛人——其中一小撮心肠歹毒的——要毁掉这群羊——究竟为什么，我以后再告诉你。这便是你必须担的风险。」
「这风险是当真的？」
她又瑟缩了一下。「为了整垮我们，他们像是什么手段都使得出来。」

===Original===
“But it’s a chance worth taking,” he said with a sudden lightness of heart. “I’ll keep the job for a while at least.”
He watched her face as he spoke, and he saw the light﻿—as unmistakable as the dawn that he had seen come over the mountains﻿—grow in her face. It was reward enough. The joy that he got out of the work itself was henceforth simply clear profit; for another motive﻿—one that had just come into his life﻿—justified beyond all question the expenditure of his time and the chance of death.
He didn’t try to explain the matter to his own satisfaction. He only knew that he felt a great and resistless desire to help this straight young mountain girl in her venture, to take sides with her against the monstrous odds that opposed her. He had committed himself: he noticed with an inward laugh that the girl had not promised even to attempt to get another herder to take his place. And he felt vaguely and secretly glad.
The two of them started to drive the white flocks back to the camp.

===Chinese===
「可这风险值得一冒。」他说，心头蓦地一轻。「这差事我至少先干上一阵。」
说这话时他注视着她的脸，他看见那道光——分明不误，就像他见过的漫过群山而来的黎明——在她脸上亮了起来。有这个，便算足够的报偿了。往后，这活计本身给他的喜悦，简直是白赚的净利；因为另一个动机——一个方才走进他生命的动机——已经无可置疑地抵得过他光阴的花销与死亡的风险。
他没有设法把这件事给自己解释个心服口服。他只知道，他心里涌起一股巨大而无可抗拒的愿望：要帮这个坦直的山地姑娘成全她的事业，要站到她那一边，去对抗压在她面前的重重凶险。他把自己许出去了：他暗自好笑地注意到，姑娘甚至没有答应试着另找一位牧羊人来接他的班。而他觉出一种说不清的、藏在心底的高兴。
两人便赶起白色的羊群，往牧羊营去了。

## XII / 第十二章

===Original===
As the stars emerged and the little mountain wind sprang up and crept forth in its never-ending explorations of the thickets, the shepherdess explained all things to Hugh’s satisfaction. But first there was supper: a meal the like of which he had never tasted before.

The girl cooked it. Hugh watched her, her swift, graceful motions, the ease and strength with which she went about her tasks, and he found an unlooked-for delight in the sight. She had brought fresh stores; and the meal﻿—from Hugh’s suddenly rejuvenated point of view﻿—was more nearly a banquet. Were there not new potatoes, roasted in the ashes, flapjacks with syrup, a fresh, white-breasted grouse that she had beheaded with her pistol on the way out, and for dessert dried apples stewed to a succulency and tenderness that passed all description? Hugh had eaten some thousands of meals in his time. He had dined in the most famous cafés and restaurants of Europe, he had been at pretentious dinner parties. Yet he couldn’t remember ever experiencing the simple and healthy zest for food, the sheer delight of eating, the amazing appetite that had come upon him now. No meal in his whole life had ever tasted so good or satisfied him more.

In the first place he had done a man’s work. For the first time in recent years his body actually demanded food: plenty of it and soon, for he had missed his lunch; besides, he had that inner peace and satisfaction of a day’s work completed. Then its very preparation made it appetizing﻿—the slim, sure hands of the girl, her brown arms flashing, the fragrant wood smoke, the long, impressive vista of the Rockies behind the camp.

After supper he helped her wash the camp dishes; then cut fir boughs for her bed that was to be situated nearly a quarter of a mile away, across the meadow. The idea of being afraid of him seemingly didn’t even occur to her. She would have slept out the same way had he been from any other class of men, and Hugh could understand how his predecessors had respected her heavy revolver. Then there was a quiet hour with his newfound friend, his pipe﻿—and the girl telling her story in the fire’s glow.

===Chinese===
星星一颗颗露了头，山间的小风刮起来，悄悄爬出去，开始它对灌木丛永无止境的勘探；牧羊女便把种种情由对休讲了个透，直讲得他心满意足。不过头一桩先是晚饭：这样一顿饭，他还从来不曾尝过。

饭是姑娘做的。休望着她——望着她那轻快优雅的动作、她操持活计时那份从容与气力——看着看着，心里生出一种意想不到的欢喜。她带来了新鲜的给养；这顿饭——在骤然焕然一新的休看来——简直近于一席盛筵。可不是么：灰烬里煨熟的新土豆，浇了糖浆的薄煎饼，一只白胸脯的鲜松鸡——来的路上被她用手枪打断了头——还有饭后那炖到软糯香甜、简直无从形容的干苹果。休这辈子吃过的饭数以千计。他在欧洲最有名的咖啡馆和餐馆用过餐，也赴过场面十足的宴会。可他不记得自己何曾体味过眼下这种单纯而健旺的口腹之兴，这种纯粹的吃的快乐，这样惊人的好胃口。他一生中还不曾有一顿饭这样好吃，也没有哪一顿更叫他餍足。

一来，他干了一份男子汉的活计。多年来头一遭，他的身子当真开口讨吃的：要得多，还要得急，因为午饭他错过了；再者，他心里揣着一天活计做完后那种内在的安宁与满足。何况这饭光是看着做就够开胃——姑娘那双纤细而稳当的手，她那褐色手臂的闪动，喷香的柴烟，还有营地背后落基山绵延而去、气象庄严的远景。

饭后他帮她洗了营地的碗碟；又去砍冷杉枝，替她铺床——床安在差不多四分之一英里外、草地的另一头。怕他这么个念头，她似乎压根儿不曾有过。哪怕他出身于旁的任何一类人，她也会照样睡在外头；休这才明白，他的几位前任何以敬重她那把沉甸甸的左轮枪。随后，他同新交的朋友——他的烟斗——安安静静消磨了一个钟头，姑娘便在火光里讲起了她的故事。

===Original===
“My father’s name is Crowson﻿—Ezra Crowson,” she began in the direct mountain way. “And mine﻿—out here we don’t bother with last names﻿—is Alice Crowson. You don’t have to call me Miss, Mr. Gaylord﻿—”

“And by the same token,” he replied, “my first name is Hugh.”

“Then Hugh﻿—”

“Then Alice﻿—” And they laughed across the fire. It had become quite easy to laugh at simple, wholesome pleasantries. Yet there was no familiarity here. Alice had told the truth: last names take time; and time, in the West, is precious. Names were designations of people, rather than people the representatives of names. Names didn’t matter and people did.

“Dan called me Alice too,” she went on, suddenly remembering to remind Hugh that he was in the same class, as far as privileges went, with his predecessors. “My father lives down at a place called Horse Creek﻿—and unlike many sheepmen in the West, his whole capital is tied up in this one flock. He isn’t a big sheepman﻿—only a little one. That is what makes it so important that we win.

===Chinese===
「我父亲姓克劳森（Crowson）——埃兹拉·克劳森（Ezra Crowson），」她用山里人直来直去的方式开了腔。「我呢——我们这儿不兴计较姓氏——叫爱丽丝·克劳森（Alice Crowson）。你不必叫我小姐，盖洛德先生——」

「那么照同样的规矩，」他答道，「我的名字叫休。」

「那就休——」

「那就爱丽丝——」两人隔着火堆笑了起来。为几句单纯而健全的玩笑发笑，竟变得这样容易。然而这里头没有半点轻狎。爱丽丝说的是实情：姓氏是要费工夫的；而工夫，在西部是金贵的东西。名字是人的记号，而不是人替名字作代表。名字不打紧，人才打紧。

「丹从前也是这么叫我爱丽丝的，」她接着说，忽然想起该提醒休一声：论到这份情分，他同他那些前任是一样的待遇。「我父亲住在下面一处叫马溪的地方——同西部许多羊主不一样，他的全部本钱都押在这一群羊上。他算不得大羊主——只是个小羊主。正因为这样，我们才非赢不可。

===Original===
“He bought the stock from an old friend to whom he had loaned everything he had﻿—and it was either take the sheep or lose everything. He wasn’t an experienced sheepman. Otherwise he wouldn’t have come here﻿—where Landy Fargo and his gang control everything. You see, Hugh, they are cattlemen﻿—managers﻿—and they’re part owners, too﻿—for a number of rich men in the East. For years and years they’ve had everything their own way in regard to the range.

“Maybe, if you’re an Eastern man, you don’t understand about range. The Western stock business depends on having acres and acres of open land for the stock to run in through spring, summer, and fall, feeding them just in winter. The land is either privately owned, public domain, school lands or government national forest: in this case it is﻿—except for this big track we have been using﻿—public domain. Of course the sheepmen had just as much right to it as the cattlemen, but because they wanted to keep all the range for themselves, they’ve driven off every man who tried to run sheep in Smoky Land.

“Oh, it was easy to do. Sometimes they did it just by threatening, sometimes by poisoning the stock, and *sometimes*﻿—”

Hugh leaned forward. “By killing the herders?”

“Yes.” The girl’s lips set tight. “I didn’t know they would go that far, but Fargo’s got a new right-hand man now﻿—a Mexican named José. He’s used to killing﻿—he learned it in the South﻿—and I haven’t a bit of doubt but that he shot Dan last night. Perhaps they’ve killed before, but before it was always open warfare, at least. They’ve always won﻿—and for all I see they’ll win now.

“You see, as far as public domain is concerned, they’ve got a certain right to oppose the sheepmen. They were the first here, and cattle won’t feed after sheep. But in this case there’s a wide track﻿—almost a whole township﻿—through the center of Smoky Land that isn’t public domain but belongs to an old woman down at Boise. It is the best sheep range in the State, and father found out that the cattlemen weren’t renting it. The old woman had tried to rent it to them, but they wouldn’t take it. We found out why, later. They were using it without paying for it﻿—and they thought that giving any money to this helpless old woman was just throwing it away. Incidentally it was all she had, and because there are no mills here, it is practically valueless except for range.

===Chinese===
「这群羊是他从一个老朋友手里接下的——他原先把身外之物全都借给了那人——到头来不外两条路：要么收下这群羊，要么血本无归。他本不是个有经验的羊主。不然他决不会上这儿来——这地方，兰迪·法戈和他那一伙人一手遮天。你要知道，休，他们是牧牛人——是经理人——自己也算持股——替东部好几个有钱人经营产业。一年又一年，这片草场上的事向来由着他们独断专行。

「兴许你要是个东部人，就不明白草场是怎么回事。西部的畜牧生意，全指着一片又一片的敞地，供牲口在春夏秋三季里撒开跑，只在冬天喂料。这些地要么是私产，要么是公有土地、学田，要么是政府的国家森林：眼下这一块——除了我们一直在使用的这一大片——是公有土地。羊主们本来对它享有同牧牛人一样的权利，可是他们要独自占尽草场，便把凡是想在烟之乡放羊的人统统赶跑了。

「嗐，这还不容易。有时单靠吓唬，有时就毒死羊只，还有*有时候*——」

休身子前倾。「——就是杀牧羊人？」

「是的。」姑娘的嘴唇抿紧了。「我原不知道他们竟会走到这一步，可法戈如今有了个新的左右手——一个名叫何塞的墨西哥人。这人杀惯了人——这门本事他是在南方学的——昨夜打死丹的，我一丁点儿也不怀疑就是他。或许他们从前也杀过人，但先前至少还是明火执仗的干仗。他们向来赢——照我看，这一回他们还是会赢。

「你要知道，单就公有土地而论，他们同羊主们作对，倒也有几分站得住的道理。他们来得最早，而牛又不肯吃羊啃过的草。可是有一大片地——差不多整整一个镇区——横贯烟之乡的中央，那不是公有土地，是博伊西（Boise）城里一个老婆婆的。那是全州顶好的牧羊草场，父亲打听得牧牛人并没有租它。老婆婆曾想租给他们，他们不肯要。个中缘故，我们后来才弄明白。他们一直白用着这片地——在他们看来，把钱花在这么个无依无靠的老婆婆身上，纯属白白糟蹋。偏巧这又是她的全部家当；这一带没有锯木厂，这地除了当草场，几乎一文不值。

===Original===
“Father thought that by renting it, the cattlemen would leave him alone. They had all the public domain; and in this way, there couldn’t be any particle of doubt about his being in his rights, both lawfully and from the customs of the range. The rent for it﻿—for a term of years﻿—cost him what little money he had left.

“We’ve been at war ever since. We’ve got to hang on﻿—it’s ruin for father if he quits. If the cattlemen would take the lease off our hands﻿—as is only fair﻿—we might ship off the flock and get out without losing everything; but they won’t do it. They’d sooner shoot and kill our herders. If you hadn’t come along when you did last night﻿—hundreds of sheep would be dead today from the coyotes and cougars. I’m two days early myself﻿—the flock would be practically wiped out before the date I was expected to arrive. They have intimidated or bought up all the labor in the region so we can’t get help﻿—that partly is the reason why I’m doing this work.

“It’s got down now to a simple matter of holding on﻿—for a few months more. In October we start the sheep down﻿—we’ll be nearer the settlements and the protection of law. Besides, a lot of the public domain becomes National Forest on the same date﻿—by an act of Congress﻿—and then there will be a big force of forest rangers here to protect us. If we can stay, and fight them off, and protect the flock until that time﻿—we’ve won. But I’m almost tired of trying.”

Her voice dropped from tone to tone, then ceased. The silence of the wilderness was left. Hugh glanced across the glowing coals, haunted by the girl’s beauty, wondering at the flood of new emotions that swept over him. “I suppose﻿—if I hadn’t happened along﻿—you’d not know where to look for another herder,” he suggested.

===Chinese===
「父亲盘算，把地租下来，牧牛人总会放他过门。公有土地尽在他们手里；这样一来，他立身于理直的一方便确凿无疑——于法理如此，于草场的规矩也如此。这笔租金——一租便是若干年——花光了他手头所剩的那一点钱。

「从那以后，我们就一直在交战。我们只能咬紧牙关撑住——父亲若半途撒手，便是倾家荡产。牧牛人若肯把租约从我们手里接过去——这本是天公地道——我们还能把羊群装运出去，全身而退，不至于输个精光；可他们偏不干。他们宁可开枪打死我们的牧羊人。昨夜你若不是恰好在那时赶到——今天早有几百只羊填了郊狼和美洲狮的肚子。我自己也提前两天赶了来——真到我原定该到的日子，羊群差不多要叫他们斩尽杀绝了。这一带的劳力，他们不是吓跑就是收买，害我们雇不着人手——这也是我来干这活的部分缘故。

「如今事情归结成一件简单的事：撑下去——再撑几个月。十月里我们就赶羊下山——离移民点和法律的保护都近了。况且同一日期，这大片公有土地有好些要划成国家森林——凭国会的一纸法案——到那时，这里就会开来一大批护林员保护我们。只要我们守得住、把他们顶回去、护住羊群挨到那一天——我们就赢了。只是我差不多快要试得没有力气了。」

她的声音一声低过一声，随后止息了。留下的是荒野的寂静。休隔着灼灼的炭火望她，为姑娘的容色所萦绕，又惊异于漫过心头的那阵新奇情感的激流。「我想——我要不是恰好路过——你连该上哪儿另找一个牧羊人只怕都不知道，」他搭话说。

===Original===
She nodded slowly. “It would have pretty near been the end.”

“And what if I should decide﻿—to stay here clear through the summer, clear to the time to take the flocks down to the lower levels.”

She looked up, a strange, brooding concern in her face. “I don’t know that I have a right to ask you,” she said slowly. “This isn’t play, Hugh﻿—it seems so natural to call you that. One man already has been killed. I don’t know that I have a right to ask you to risk your life. But father is old﻿—and he had such high hopes﻿—and it means so much. No, I can’t ask you to stay.”

He leaned forward, more earnestness in his face than had ever dwelt there before. “But what if I wanted to stay﻿—clear to the end?”

===Chinese===
她缓缓点头。「那就差不多是穷途末路了。」

「那要是我拿定主意——干脆在这儿待满一夏，一直待到把羊群赶下低地去的时候呢？」

她抬起眼来，脸上有一种奇异的、沉沉的关切。「我不知道自己有没有权利开口求你，」她慢慢地说。「这不是儿戏，休——这样叫你，竟觉得这样自然。已经搭进去一条人命了。我不知道自己有没有权利要你拿性命去冒险。可是父亲老了——他曾抱着那么高的指望——这事情关系太大了。不，我不能求你留下。」

他俯身向前，脸上那份恳切，是从未在那张脸上住过的。「可要是我自己想留下呢——一直留到最后？」

===Original===
A curious luster was in her eyes. “I wouldn’t dare believe it﻿—and I wouldn’t understand.”

“And I’m not sure that I understand, either,” he told her. “But my days have never been much use to me before. It’s the first time I’ve ever had a chance to do something﻿—not only for myself but somebody else﻿—the first time I’ve really had an opening﻿—to do something worth while.”

She thought she understood. She knew the West﻿—this mountain girl﻿—and she knew a certain unfortunate breed of men that often come wandering down its long trails. Mostly they come from the busy cities in the East: human derelicts, men who have broken and failed in the struggle for existence. Sometimes they come looking for new opportunities, sometimes they are merely tramps, the wanderlust in their veins, more than often they are men of good families who have sunk to the lowest levels of life. She marked his well-bred speech, and she thought she knew his type. Her keen eyes saw the deep lines in his face, his bloodshot eyes, and she didn’t understand how Dan’s supply of whisky had been left intact. Possibly Hugh had failed to find it!

Perhaps he would want to go on, in a few more days; yet she couldn’t banish the hope that he was of different stuff than most of his kind. “If you did stay﻿—and help us out﻿—we’d make it right with you in the end,” she promised. No longer was she the employer, speaking from the heights. Her tone was almost pleading. “Perhaps you could buy a share in the business﻿—and get a fresh start in life.”

===Chinese===
她眼里闪着一种奇异的光。「我不敢相信——也弄不明白。」

「要说弄明白，我自己也没有把握，」他对她说。「只是从前，我的日子对我自己来说从来没有多大用处。这是我头一回有机会做点事——不光为自己，也为旁人——头一回真正有个门路——做一件值得做的事。」

她想她是明白的。她认得西部——这个山里长大的姑娘——也认得某一类时运不济的男人，他们常顺着西部的漫长小径一路漂泊而来。这些人多半出自东部繁忙的都市：是人生的落魄者，是在生存之争里垮了、败了的人。有时他们是来寻新机会的，有时不过是流浪汉，血脉里淌着流浪的癖性；更多的往往是好人家出身、却一路沉到生活最底层的男子。她听得出他谈吐有教养，自认摸准了他的路数。她那双锐眼看见他脸上刀刻似的深纹、眼中的血丝，心里纳罕丹留下的那点威士忌怎么竟原封未动。多半是休没能找到！

说不定再过几天他就要继续赶路了；可她抑不住心头的指望：他未必是他那一类人的坯子。「你要是当真留下——帮我们熬过这一关——到头来我们一定不亏待你，」她允诺道。此刻她不再是那个居高临下发号施令的雇主。她的语气几乎是在恳求。「说不定你还能在这份营生里搭上一股——在人生里重新起个步。」

===Original===
He suddenly got up and found a curious satisfaction in swinging mighty blows with the axe at the fir log Dan had used for fuel. It acted as a relief valve for emotions that he felt would soon get away from him. He looked up, smiling boyishly. “I’ll stay﻿—to the end,” he promised. “But Lord knows﻿—I don’t know anything about sheep.”

“And you give me fresh heart.”

Thus they made a pact in the firelight, and they had a few moments of sheer joy as she gave him simple directions as to how to take care of the flock, when to salt them, and how to direct their feeding. “And while you’re telling me these things,” the man said, “for Heaven’s sake tell me about Spot.”

===Chinese===
他霍地起身，抡起斧头，朝丹劈来当柴烧的那段冷杉木狠狠劈去，一下又一下，从中得了种说不出的快意。这成了个泄压的阀门，宣泄那股他自觉转眼就要按捺不住的情绪。他抬起头来，笑得带着孩子气。「我留下——到最后，」他应承道。「只是天晓得——我对羊可是一窍不通。」

「你倒给了我新的胆气。」

两人便这样在火光里立了约。她把照管羊群的粗浅门道一一传授——几时给羊喂盐，怎样引导它们采食——这几分钟里，两人都享了一阵纯粹的欢愉。「趁你讲这些的时候，」那男人说，「千万看在老天的份上，给我讲讲斑点吧。」

===Original===
“Of course you mean the yearling ram﻿—” Hugh nodded. “You must have learned a lot about sheep in one day﻿—or you’d never have noticed him. Spot *is* a mystery﻿—has been since he was born. And what has he been doing today?”

Hugh narrated with much enthusiasm the encounter between the flock leader and the coyote, so interested in the story itself that he quite failed to wonder and be amazed at his own unlooked-for lightness of heart, his buoyant spirits.

“It’s typical of Spot,” the girl said at the end. “Perhaps you’ve noticed that he’s oversized﻿—taller and stronger than the rest?”

“Yes﻿—”

“If it hadn’t been for that, he would have likely been lamb stew long ago. He was exceptionally large at birth, and father had him retained partly because he was interested in his unusual coloring, and partly because he thought that his extra size would give him value as a ram. He endeared himself to the herder, and this year﻿—he’s a yearling ram but he’s not yet mature﻿—father let him run with the flock. None of us know what to make of him.”

===Chinese===
「你说的准是那只一岁的公羊——」休点了点头。「你必定是一天之内就学了不少羊的学问——不然绝不会留意到他。斑点*就是*个谜——打生下来便是。他今天都干了些什么？」

休眉飞色舞地讲了头羊同郊狼遭遇的那一幕；他讲得入了迷，竟全然忘了诧异自己这份出乎意料的轻快心境、这股飘飘然的兴头。

「斑点向来是这个路数，」姑娘听罢说。「兴许你注意到了，他个头特大——比其余的羊都高、都壮？」

「正是——」

「若非仗着这个，他早就多半成一锅炖羔羊肉了。他落地时便大得出奇，父亲把他留了下来——一半是那身异样的毛色引起了他的兴味，一半是他想这副超群的大个头日后做种羊必有价值。他又深得牧羊人的欢心，到今年——他虽是一岁的公羊，却还没长足——父亲便放他随群跑了。我们谁都摸不透他是个什么路数。」

===Original===
They got up, built the fire high so that its glow went out over the flock, and tried to get another glimpse of him. They found him easily enough, at the very foremost of the band, his brown color in vivid contrast to the whiteness of the ewes. And in finding him Alice made another, less pleasing discovery.

Hugh didn’t understand at first. He saw that she was making some kind of a count, first leisurely, then in frantic haste. A troubled look came into her fresh face. Once more she verified the count, then turned to him with a rueful smile.

“My day’s work is not yet over,” she said slowly.

“Why not?” he demanded.

===Chinese===
两人起了身，把火拨得旺旺的，好让火光铺到羊群身上，再想看他一眼。他们轻而易举就找到了他：正在全群的最前头，他那一身褐色，在母羊们的雪白衬映下格外鲜明。而就在找他的时候，爱丽丝又有了另一桩发现——一桩不那么叫人痛快的发现。

休起初不明白。他见她在数着什么，起先从从容容，继而火急火燎。她那张明艳的脸上浮起一层忧色。她又把数目核了一遍，才转向他，露出一个苦笑。

「我今天的活计还没有完，」她慢慢地说。

「怎么还没完？」他追问。

===Original===
“One of the markers﻿—one of sixteen black ewes﻿—is missing. Do you know what that means?”

“Good Heavens﻿—that you’ve got to go out﻿—in this dark forest﻿—to look for him?”

“The ninety and nine,” she quoted, still with the same, inscrutable smile. “But it isn’t just one, Hugh. You see, sheep keep relatively the same position in the flock. Of course it might be just a single disaster﻿—a coyote snatching her from the flanks﻿—but ordinarily when one of the black sheep is gone, it means that a hundred or so others have gone with her. I can’t take the chance.”

His own face grew mournful. “You see﻿—what a good herder I am. Lose a hundred sheep the first day.”

===Chinese===
「丢了一只记号羊——十六只黑母羊里的一只。你知道这是什么意思吗？」

「老天爷——你得出去——到这黑黢黢的林子里——去找它？」

「那九十九只，」她引了句经文，脸上仍是那副莫测高深的浅笑。「可这不止是丢一只的事，休。你要知道，羊在群里的位次是相对固定的。当然，也可能只是单独的一场灾祸——一只郊狼从侧翼叼走了它——但通常，黑羊丢了一只，便意味着还有百十来只跟它一道走了。这个险我冒不起。」

他自己的脸也愁了下来。「你瞧——我这个牧羊人多称职。头一天就丢一百只羊。」

===Original===
“It happens to the best of herders.”

“Then why can’t I go out to look for them﻿—and let you stay here? That’s what I’m going to do.”

“No. You must stay here. You don’t know sheep yet, Hugh, and likely you don’t know these mountains. The band of them is somewhere through the stretch you fed over today﻿—and I would know just how and where to look for them. At night with no dog﻿—the dog must stay here with you﻿—you wouldn’t be able to drive them. I even have to go on foot, so I can climb down into the steep canyons and go through the brush. It’s part of the life of the camp-tender.”

“But you won’t go﻿—in this darkness﻿—”

===Chinese===
「顶好的牧羊人也会赶上这种事。」

「那为什么不能我出去找——让你留在这儿？我打定主意这么办。」

「不成。你必须待在这儿。你还不识羊性，休，这些山你多半也认不得。走失的那群羊就在你今天放牧经过的那一带的什么地方——该怎么找、上哪儿找，我心里一清二楚。夜里没有狗——狗必须留下陪你——你赶不动它们。连我都得步行，才好下到陡峭的峡谷里、穿过灌木丛。这本是送补人生活的一部分。」

「可你不能就这样去——在这样的黑地里——」

===Original===
“I’ll wait till the moon rises. Besides, I know this Smoky Land from end to end. So don’t be afraid for me.”

They stood silent by the leaping flame. The sheep lay quiet, the shepherd dog slept at Hugh’s feet. And subtly stealing into their consciousness above the sound of the leaping flame they heard the voice of the forest, mysterious and profound﻿—the little sounds of the wind in the thicket, the rustling of leaves, the hushed footfall of the wild creatures. Into that darkness Alice would venture for her lost sheep. Hugh felt a strange weight of dread.

Above the far mountains the clouds gleamed with the first beams of the rising moon.

===Chinese===
「我等月亮上来再走。再说，这烟之乡我从这头到那头都摸熟了。所以不用替我担心。」

两人在腾腾的火焰旁默默站着。羊群安卧，牧羊犬睡在休的脚边。跳动的火焰声之上，森林的声音悄悄潜进他们的意识里——神秘而深沉——风过树丛的细响，树叶的窸窣，野兽们放轻的脚步声。爱丽丝就要走进那片黑暗里去，为她迷失的羊冒一回险。休觉出一阵异样的、沉甸甸的恐惧。

远山的上空，云朵映着初升月亮的最初一线光，微微发亮。

## XIII / 第十三章

===Original===

In that momentous twilight that brought Hugh Gaylord to the sheep camp for the first time, there was unfamiliar traffic on the brown, pine-needle trail that wound down to the meadows from the darkening forest. There was the sound of a footfall not often heard. And one can imagine the lesser forest people﻿—the little gnawing folk that have underground lairs and to whom the ferns are a beautiful, tropical forest﻿—gazing up with bright eyes to see who came.

Perhaps at first they thought it was merely one of the hunters: a great creature of claw or fang such as a wolf or cougar. This was the hour when the beasts of prey started forth to hunt, and it was true that the step had a stealthy, hushed quality of one who does not care to have his presence known. It can be understood why a little gopher, so fat of cheeks that he gave the impression of being afflicted with mumps, lay rather close and still among his tree roots until the creature got past. He didn’t care to feel a puma claw impaling him as a fishhook impales a worm.

The gopher has not particularly acute vision, so this traveler on the pine-needle trail was to remain ever a mystery to him. He was aware of a tall, dark form that glided softly and departed; and life became the same puzzling grayness that it had been before. A chipmunk, however﻿—like a little patch of light and shadow against a brown tree trunk﻿—could see much better. And he lay very still, only his eyes busy, until he found out the truth.

The passerby was only a man, after all, such a creature as usually did not take the trouble to hunt chipmunks. Still he felt afraid, and it is extremely doubtful that the small-sized, always addled brain in his miniature skull could tell him why. The truth was that in that stealing figure there was something terribly suggestive of the beasts of prey themselves, creatures that﻿—more than often﻿—did devote unwelcome attentions to chipmunks. The man crept through the forest with the same caution. His eyes were strange and glowing like those of the lynx as it climbs through the branches. And over him﻿—an aura too dim and obscure for the blunt senses of human beings﻿—hung an essence with which the wilderness creatures are only too familiar: that ancient lust and fever that comes to Broken Fang when he strikes down his prey.

===Chinese===

就在那个把休·盖洛德头一回带到牧羊营的重大黄昏里，那条从渐暗的森林里蜿蜒而下、通往草甸的褐色松针小径上，出现了一番不寻常的行踪。响起了一个不常听见的脚步声。可以想见，森林里的小民们——那些住在地下洞穴、把蕨丛当作美丽热带森林的小小啮齿族——正睁着亮晶晶的眼睛向上张望，看是谁来了。

起初，它们或许只当来的不过是又一个猎手：某个爪牙俱全的庞然大物，比如狼，比如美洲狮。这正是猛兽动身出猎的时辰，而那脚步声也确实带着一股潜行的、悄然的味道，像是出自一个不愿让人知晓自己到来的家伙。这就难怪一只小小的囊鼠（gopher）——两腮肥得像害了腮腺炎——紧贴着树根一动不动地趴着，直到那家伙走过去。它可不想让美洲狮的爪子像鱼钩扎蚯蚓那样把自己钉住。

囊鼠的视力并不特别锐利，因此松针小径上的这位过客，对它来说注定永远是个谜。它只觉察到一个高大的黑影悄然滑过、又悄然离去；生活重又变回先前那片令人费解的灰蒙蒙。然而一只花栗鼠——趴在褐色树干上，像一小片明暗斑驳——眼力可要好得多。它一动不动地趴着，只有一双眼睛忙个不停，直到弄清了真相。

那过客终究只是一个人，一个通常懒得费神去猎花栗鼠的生物。可它还是害怕，而它那小小的头骨里那颗素来浆糊一般的迷你脑袋，能不能给它说出个缘由来，实在大可怀疑。真相是：那个偷偷摸摸的身影上，有种东西骇人地叫人联想到猛兽本身——那些家伙可没少把不受欢迎的殷勤花在花栗鼠身上。这人潜行穿过森林，用的正是同一份小心。他的眼睛异样地发亮，像猞猁在枝叶间攀爬时的那双眼睛。而在他周身——笼着一层对人类迟钝感官来说过于幽微难辨的气息——悬着一种荒野生灵再熟悉不过的东西：那股古老的欲望与狂热，正是断牙扑倒猎物时涌上来的那一股。

===Original===

He was shivering all over; and it was to be remembered that the wolf﻿—in certain dread moments at the end of the chase﻿—shivered the same way. One would have been given cause to wonder what stress, what dreadful events had occurred beyond the edge of the meadow that had caused this queer inebriation.

But as strong drink dies in the body, the fever seemed to fall away from him as he made turn after turn in the trail. He stooped now, rather than crouched, his footfall had a fumbling, heavy, dragging quality that was not at all like the stealth it had possessed at first. The surface lights passed from his dark eyes, leaving them somewhat languorous and lifeless. The lines of his face were of inordinate fatigue; he no longer trembled in excitement, and for all the heat of the July night he felt cold.

José Mertos was no stranger to the blood-madness. It had been upon him plenty of times in his own land, and he had shivered and exulted with it beneath far southern stars. Yet it never grew old to him. Its rapture seemed ever greater. But unfortunately, when it died, it left disquieting pictures in his brain. They always took the fine edge from his satisfaction after a particularly skilful affair such as this had been. He was a tried hand in such work, innured to wickedness, yet he still retained the same troublesome tendency toward afterimages that had spoiled his sleep, one night and another, in the past.

===Chinese===

他浑身发着抖；要知道，狼——在追猎终局的某些可怖时刻——也是这样发抖的。人不免要纳闷：草甸边缘之外究竟出了什么变故、什么可怕的事情，才弄得他这般奇异的酩酊。

可是，正如烈酒会在身体里渐渐散去，随着他在小径上拐过一道又一道弯，那股狂热也仿佛从他身上褪落了。此刻他是弯着腰赶路，而不是弓着身子潜行，脚步声带着一种摸索的、滞重的、拖沓的味道，同起先那股悄然全然不像。他深色眼睛里的浮光散尽了，只剩下几分倦怠与无神。他脸上的纹路刻着过度的疲惫；他不再兴奋得发抖，而且在七月夜晚的燠热里，他竟觉得冷。

何塞·梅托斯对嗜血疯魔（blood-madness）并不陌生。在老家，这疯魔不知多少回附上他的身；在南天遥远的星辰底下，他曾伴着它发抖，也曾伴着它狂喜。然而这疯魔在他身上从不变得陈旧，它的迷狂反倒似乎一回浓似一回。只可惜，疯魔退去之后，会在他脑子里留下些叫人心神不宁的画面。像今夜这样一桩格外利落的活计过后的快意，总要让这些画面磨去锋刃。干这一行他是把老手，早已惯于为恶，可他仍旧保留着那个恼人的毛病——事后影像会缠上他，过去就曾一夜又一夜地搅坏他的睡眠。

===Original===

At present he couldn’t forget the ludicrous look of bewilderment with which Dan the herder had received the rifle shot. It was only a thing to laugh at, to tell as a good joke when he sat with his employer, yet he found no pleasure in thinking of it now. The herder had simply looked amazed﻿—not afraid, not awed by the Gates of Darkness that had rushed up to him, nothing at all but deeply bewildered and unbelieving. He hadn’t seemed to realize that José had shot him, that even as the blank expression of astonishment had come upon his face the lead ball had forced a passage through his breast. Perhaps he died too quickly for any sort of realization. And José could not forget the queer swaying and staggering with which he fell back upon his tree-bough pallet﻿—just as if he were drunk and falling into bed.

The pictures came in a series, one after another. First this falling, then the glimpse of the still form on the fir boughs. The black shepherd dog had come bounding toward him, and José had drawn his pistol for this work. It was just as good at close range. And he remembered with amazing clearness how the light had died, slowly and unmistakably, in the dog’s eyes. He had watched the sheep for the last time.

Even now José didn’t understand how he had missed the other, larger shepherd dog. He had shot, the animal had rocked down, he had looked back once to the dead herder in the tent (perhaps to see how *he* was taking this slaughter of his pets) and when he had turned his eyes again, the animal had risen and was fleeing from him about the flank of the flock. The cur was wounded, anyway: perhaps the injury was severe enough to end his life before the morning. His watch of the sheep was surely done. And Landy Fargo﻿—the man who even now waited for his report﻿—would find the whole matter to his satisfaction.

===Chinese===

此刻他忘不掉的，是牧羊人丹挨那记步枪子弹时那副滑稽的茫然神情。这本是桩可笑的事，是往后同雇主闲坐时可以当个好听笑话讲的段子，可现在想起来，他竟品不出半点快意。那牧羊人只是显得惊愕——不害怕，也没有被猛然冲到自己面前的黑暗之门（the Gates of Darkness）慑住，全然只有深深的迷惑与不敢相信。他似乎没有意识到开枪的是何塞，没有意识到就在那副茫然的错愕爬上他面孔的同时，铅弹已经在他胸膛里强行开出了一条路。也许他死得太快，快得来不及生出任何意识。而何塞忘不掉他倒回树枝铺就的卧榻时那阵古怪的摇晃与踉跄——就像喝醉了酒、栽倒在床上一样。

画面一幅接一幅地连着涌来。先是这倒下的样子，继而是冷杉枝上那具一动不动的身形。随后黑色牧羊犬朝他扑跳过来，何塞便拔出手枪来干这一桩。近距离里它照样好使。而他还以惊人的清晰记着，那狗眼睛里的光是怎样慢慢地、确凿无疑地熄灭的。它看护羊群，这已是最后一回。

直到此刻，何塞还是不明白，另一条更大的牧羊犬他是怎么放过了的。他开了枪，那畜生晃了晃栽了下去，他回头往帐篷里那个死掉的牧羊人身上望了一眼（也许是想看看本人对这场宰杀他爱犬的戏码作何感受），待他再把眼光转回来时，那畜生已经爬起身，绕着羊群侧翼从他跟前逃开了。反正那贱狗是受了伤：伤势说不定重得足以叫它活不到天亮。它看羊的差事是必定到头了。而兰迪·法戈——那个此刻正等着他回报的人——会觉得这桩事从头到尾都合他的心意。

===Original===

A few minutes later José came to the thicket where he had left his horse; then he headed on down the trail. Through the night hours he rode. Not in one chance out of a thousand would the murder be discovered before Alice Crowson returned, three days later, but he didn’t care to take that thousandth chance. It might be embarrassing﻿—considering his past record﻿—to explain his presence near the murder. “If you can’t buy him over and things *do* get to the shooting stage,” Landy Fargo had said, “no one will find the stiff for three days. You’ll be miles away by then, the flock’ll be torn to pieces, and we’ll be settin’ pretty. It’s the safest deal you were ever in.”

The destruction of the flock, José considered, was not his business. His work was done and the sooner he got out of the immediate vicinity the better it would be for him. He spurred the horse into a slow, easy gallop.

The moon came up, falling dimly upon his burnished skin. It would be no longer possible to mistake his race. He was even darker than Pete, the Indian, his eyes were like jet, his lips were thin and dark and cruel. But he rode well. He seemed to hang almost limp in the saddle, utterly without effort, and the long miles sped beneath him.

===Chinese===

几分钟后，何塞走到他藏马的灌木丛，随后便顺着小径往下去了。他连夜赶路。这桩谋杀要在三天后爱丽丝·克劳森回来之前被人发觉，机会不到千分之一，可他连这千分之一也不愿赌。念着他过往的记录，要解释自己何以出现在凶案近旁，难免叫人尴尬。『要是你收买不动他，事情当真闹到动枪的地步，』兰迪·法戈当时说，『三天里没人会找见那具死尸。到时候你早已在几英里外，羊群会被撕成碎片，咱们就稳稳当当地坐享其成了。这是你干过的最保险的一票买卖。』

在何塞看来，羊群的覆灭不关他的事。他的活计已经干完，越快离开这是非之地的近旁，对他越有好处。他磕了磕马刺，催马放起一个从容的慢步奔驰。

月亮升上来了，淡淡地照在他油亮的皮肤上。这下再也不可能认错他的种族了。他比那个印第安人皮特还要黑，眼睛像两粒墨玉，嘴唇薄而黯黑，透着狠劲。不过他骑术很好。他几乎是软软地悬在马鞍上，全然不费力气，长长的里程在马蹄下飞掠而过。

===Original===

But the night was almost done when he came to Landy Fargo’s house on the lower waters of Silver Creek. Fargo himself was dozing in a great chair in his stuffy living room, waiting for his envoy to return.

There were several noticeable features about this room. The principal one was its dirt. The floors were stained, the carpets soiled, the corners cluttered with rubbish of all kinds; the window glass was so spotted that it did not let the moonlight in, and the soft light lingered﻿—like an unwelcome ghost﻿—against the windowpane. The effect that wise interior decorators try to obtain in furnishing﻿—that in which the pieces, not holding the eye in themselves, give an atmosphere, a sense of unity﻿—was quite lacking here. The mantel was clustered with gaudy ornaments, the chairs were tawdry, cheap pictures covered the walls. But yet the room reflected the individuality of the man in the chair. He was just as cheaply gaudy, just as unclean as the room itself.

He got up, and it was to be seen that he had a rather formidable physique. He had imposing muscles, stocky legs, and it was wholly possible that before prosperity had come upon him he had been an agile, muscular cattleman. But the gaze left his heavy frame and was held by the unmistakable brutality of his face. There was none of the fine-edged cruelty﻿—that with which a puma pats and plays his prey to death﻿—that was to be seen in the swarthy face of José. He was blunt, dull; his savagery was that of the bulldog﻿—heavy lips closed over strong teeth, little lurid eyes looking out from under heavy brows.

===Chinese===

待他赶到银溪下游的兰迪·法戈家，夜已差不多过完。法戈本人正闷在那间不透气的客厅里，靠在一把大椅子上打盹，等他的使者回来复命。

这间屋子有几处惹眼的地方，头一处便是它的脏。地板上渍痕斑斑，地毯污浊不堪，墙角里堆满各式各样的破烂；窗玻璃斑斑点点，连月光也透不进来，那柔光像个不受欢迎的幽灵，滞留在窗玻璃外。聪明的室内装饰师布置屋子时追求的效果——家具本身并不攫住人的眼光，却烘出一种气氛、一种浑然一体的感觉——在这里全然不见踪影。壁炉架上挤满俗艳的摆设，椅子俗气花哨，墙上挂满了廉价的画片。然而这屋子倒也照出了椅中那个人的个性。他本人正同这屋子一样俗艳而廉价，一样不干净。

他站起身，看得出他有一副相当骇人的体格。肌肉堂堂，双腿粗壮；在他发迹之前，他多半曾是个身手矫健、筋肉结实的牧牛人。可是目光一离开他沉重的躯干，便被那张脸上明白无误的凶残攫住了。他脸上没有那种锋刃精致的残忍——美洲狮把猎物拍来戏去、慢慢弄死它的那种——那种东西，在何塞黝黑的面孔上倒看得到。他这人又粗又钝；他的凶悍是斗牛犬式的——厚嘴唇抿住结实的牙齿，一双阴惨惨的小眼睛从浓眉底下望出来。

===Original===

José rolled a cigarette, lighted it, drew its smoke into his vitals, then lolled in one of the chairs. Fargo watched him with hard eyes, not a little admiring. Most of his understrappers did not come into his presence with this same ease and self-possession. He was used to seeing men cower before him. He had learned to look for a certain cringing and servility, and many a time when it was lacking he forced it with his heavy, flailing fists. But José was different. It might not be healthy to strike José. They met eyes to eyes.

“Well?” Fargo demanded.

He was somewhat anxious about the verdict. This had been no child’s play﻿—what José had been sent to do. It was really a new departure for the little clique that he headed. There had been deaths before, open riding and fast shooting, but deliberate and premeditated killing had never been necessary. Slumber hadn’t come easily to him tonight. And now he didn’t like to be kept waiting.

“Well, what?” José answered. “You mean﻿—what luck?”

“That’s it.” Fargo uttered a short syllable of a laugh. “What luck?”

“I said I’d do it, didn’t I, if he didn’t come around? Well, I’ve done it. There’s nobody watchin’ them sheep tonight.” And thus it was to be seen that José had lived long enough among Americans to acquire the vernacular. Only a hint of the Latin, a softening of consonants, remained in his tone.

===Chinese===

何塞卷了一支烟，点上，把烟深深吸进五脏六腑，然后懒洋洋地靠进一把椅子。法戈目光冷硬地打量着他，不无佩服。他手下的喽啰，大多数到他跟前可没有这份从容自若。他看惯了男人在他面前瑟缩。他早已学会指望一份卑躬屈膝的奴态，多少回缺了这份奴态，他便用那双沉重的、抡起来鞭子似的拳头硬给打出来。可何塞不一样。动手打何塞，未必有益健康。两人四目相对。

「怎么样？」法戈问道。

他有些急着想听结果。派何塞去干的这件事，可不是儿戏。对他领头的那一小撮人来说，这实在是一步新棋。先前也不是没死过人——明着骑马闯来、拔枪快射——可有预谋、经盘算的杀戮，还从来不曾用过。今夜安眠迟迟不肯降临到他身上。而眼下，他不喜欢干等人。

「嗯？什么怎么样？」何塞答道。「你是问——手气如何？」

「就是这个。」法戈发出一声只有一个音节的短笑。「手气如何？」

「我说过我会干的，不是吗——要是他不肯回心转意的话？好啦，我干完了。今夜可没有人看那群羊了。」由此可以看出，何塞在美国人中间已经住得够久，把土话学到手了。他的口音里只剩一丝拉丁人的痕迹——子音稍稍发软。

===Original===
