Fargo uttered a short sigh of relief. “Clean job, eh?”

“All except the big dog. Killed the black one. Wounded the shepherd﻿—think he’ll kick in before morning.”

Fargo leaned back in his chair. “Then there’s nothin’ to it. I guess that’ll show ’em, eh, José?” He fell to boasting. “I guess that makes it plain that when I say get out, I mean get out. You know I told that devil this was his last warning﻿—told him myself what would happen to him if he didn’t switch over to us﻿—and I guess he got what he wanted. But I’m sorry you missed the dog. He might keep away a lot of cats and coyotes that would otherwise be busy for the next few days.”

“He’s wounded﻿—don’t think he can.” José breathed an oath in his own tongue. “But I don’t see what it’s all about. Crowson had that tract rented﻿—”

===Chinese===

法戈松了口气，短短叹了一声。「干得干净吧？」

「都干净，就差那条大狗。黑的那条打死了。那条看羊的打伤了——我看它天亮前就得断气。」

法戈往椅背上一靠。「那就没什么可说的了。我琢磨这下能给他们一点颜色瞧瞧了，嗯，何塞？」他自吹自擂起来。「我琢磨这下说得再明白不过了：我说滚蛋，就是滚蛋。你知道我给那恶棍下过最后通牒——亲口告诉过他，不改换门庭投到我们这边来会是什么下场——如今我看他是如愿以偿了。只可惜你放过了那条狗。往后这几天，本来会有不少美洲狮和郊狼要来忙活，有它在，兴许能给挡开。」

「它受了伤——我看它挡不了。」何塞用自己老家的话低低咒了一声。「可我不明白这到底为的是什么。克劳森那块地是正经租下的——」

===Original===

“You don’t, eh?” Fargo stiffened. “I don’t know it’s necessary that you see what it’s all about. That happens to be my business﻿—and don’t go making any mistake about that. But I’ll explain it a little better. I’ve been told﻿—by the men that own the herds with me﻿—to keep out sheepmen at all cost. That one piece of range that Crowson rented has been worth ten thousand a year to us. Do you think we’re goin’ to let that slip out of our hands for a bunch of measly sheep?”

“But why didn’t you have enough sense to rent that tract yourself?”

“Because we’re trying to make a cleanup out of this deal, that’s why. Who’d ever dream that old hag would ever find a renter﻿—and as long as we were gettin’ it free, what was the use? We’ve been here a long time﻿—if this flock prospered there’d be more of ’em come in﻿—and where’d be our monopoly of the range then? You know that our policy has always been to squeeze out the little fellow﻿—cattle as well as sheep. We’ve got to set an example with this flock of Crowson’s﻿—and to have ’em all get killed﻿—in a few days﻿—or even part of ’em, is going to discourage any more sheepmen coming into a cattle country. You don’t know cattlemen, José, or you wouldn’t question. Just the same﻿—the job’s only half done. A shepherd dog, wounded or not, ’ll stay to fight to the last inch of hair on his body, especially that big devil of Crowson’s. And he sure can bluff out the coyotes.”

===Chinese===

「你不明白，嗯？」法戈绷起了脸。「我看没什么必要让你明白这为的是什么。那碰巧是我的事——这一点你可别弄出差错。不过我不妨稍微给你讲明白些。有人吩咐过我——就是同我一起拥有牛群的那些人——要不惜一切代价把羊主挡在外头。克劳森租下的那一块草场，一年给我们挣一万块。你以为我们会为了一帮下贱的臭羊，让它从手里溜出去？」

「可你为什么就没想到自己把那块地租下来？」

「因为我们要的就是从这桩买卖里大捞一笔，就是这么回事。谁做梦想得到那老巫婆居然能找着个租户——再说了，既然我们一直白用着那块地，何必多此一举？我们在这儿盘踞很久了——这群羊要是发了家，往后还会有更多的羊群拥进来——到那时我们还怎么独霸这片草场？你知道我们的方针从来就是挤走小户——养牛的也好，养羊的也好。我们非得拿克劳森这群羊开刀立威——叫它们几天里死个精光——哪怕只死掉一部分也好——好断了别的羊主进牧牛地界来的念头。你不了解牧牛人，何塞，不然你就不会这么问了。不过话说回来——这活计只干了一半。一条牧羊犬，不管带没带伤，都会守着羊群战到身上最后一寸毛，尤其是克劳森那条大恶狗。而且它准能把郊狼唬住。」

===Original===

José discarded his cigarette, and lit a fresh one. “Well, say what’s to be done,” he said. “I’m not goin’ back after that other dog.”

“I’m not tellin’ you to, either. Your job is to stay away from there.” Fargo suddenly leaned forward, his eyes burning. “You know what I’d like to see?” he whispered. “I’d like to have that Crowson girl ride up there in a day or two and find every one of those damned woollies﻿—every one, not three or four hundred of ’em﻿—dead and rotting in the grass. Then people’d know this was a cattle country. Since we’ve gone as far as we have, the thing to do is to go all the way. And we might work it yet.”

José’s face showed that he was interested. “Poison?” he asked.

“You can’t never tell about poison. Sheep are queer critters. When they’re well fed they’ll shy of anything that tastes queer. Think again﻿—”

===Chinese===

何塞丢了烟头，又点上一支新的。「那么，说说底下怎么办，」他说。「我可不回去再收拾那条狗了。」

「我也没叫你回去。你的差事就是离那儿远远的。」法戈忽然朝前探过身子，两眼冒火。「你知道我想看见什么？」他压低嗓门说。「我想让那个克劳森家的小丫头过一两天骑马上去，发现那些该死的毛团子（woollies）——每一只，不是三四百只，是一只不剩——死在草里、烂在草里。到那时人们就知道这是牧牛人的地界了。既然已经走到这一步，索性就走到底。这事儿咱们兴许还办得成。」

何塞的脸上露出他来了兴趣的样子。「下毒？」他问。

「毒药这东西可说不准。羊是种古怪性子的畜生。吃得饱饱的时候，味道一古怪它就不肯沾。再想想——」

===Original===

“The only other way’s rifles﻿—and that would take a carload of shells. But I tell you﻿—the coyotes will slash a lot of ’em and run the rest to death.”

“Maybe﻿—and maybe not. A coyote don’t run sheep. They kill all they can, and then start to eatin’. Of course there’s exceptions. It takes a dog to slash a hundred of ’em in one night﻿—and run the rest﻿—”

And at that instant his words were drowned out. A strange, formidable cry reached them from behind the house: a long, far-carrying chorus of savage voices. It rang shockingly loud in the silent darkness. It was a symphony of prolonged, deep bays﻿—a sound as terrifying and menacing as any voice of the wilderness. And an evil glitter came into Fargo’s eyes.

===Chinese===

「另一条路就只剩步枪了——那可得一整车的弹药。可我告诉你——郊狼会咬翻一大片，把剩下的活活撵死。」

「也许成——也许不成。郊狼不撵羊。它们是能杀多少杀多少，杀完就埋头吃。当然也有例外。要一夜里咬翻一百只、再把剩下的撵得没命地跑——那得是狗——」

就在这一刹那，他的话被淹没了。屋后传来一声古怪而骇人的嚎叫：一长串凶蛮嗓音的大合唱，远远地传开去。它在寂静的黑暗里响得骇人。那是一支由绵长而低沉的吠声汇成的交响——同荒野里任何一种声音一样吓人、一样带着威胁。法戈的眼睛里泛起一道邪恶的凶光。

===Original===

The explosion of sound, blaring out so suddenly in the stillness, had startled José; but he caught himself at once. The cry ceased, the stillness fell again. “Your pack of bear dogs!” he exclaimed.

“Yes. It was as if they heard us talkin’ about sheep. It’s like they was tryin’ to tell us what to do.”

It was true. It might have been the voice of an evil genius, prompting their vicious designs. Fargo was a superstitious man, and now he was tingling all over with hatred and malice, inspired to the depths of his wicked being by the cry in the darkness.

“Yes,” he whispered. “My pack of bear dogs﻿—ten of ’em, savage as wolves﻿—and not to be afraid of no wounded shepherd dog, and tearin’ to pieces anyone that tries to stop ’em. They’ve told us how to solve our problem. And I don’t see why I didn’t think of ’em before.”

===Chinese===

这突如其来的声响在寂静里炸开，吓了何塞一跳；但他立刻稳住了自己。嚎声止息，寂静重又落下。「你那群猎熊犬（bear dogs）！」他失声道。

「可不是。倒像是它们听见我们说羊了。像是它们正想告诉我们该怎么办。」

倒真是这么回事。那兴许就是一个邪灵的声音，在给他们的毒计出主意。法戈本是个迷信的人，此刻他浑身上下都因憎恨与歹毒而刺痛发热，黑暗里的这声嚎叫把他那副恶毒心肠的最深处都煽动起来了。

「是啊，」他低声说。「我那群猎熊犬——十条，凶得像狼——不会怕什么受了伤的牧羊犬，谁想拦它们就把谁撕成碎片。它们把解难题的法子都告诉我们了。我不明白，我怎么先前就没想到它们。」

## XIV / 第十四章

===Original===
It didn’t take Fargo long to perfect his plans. As the dawn emerged he talked them over with the Mexican, José, and the latter was ready with any little suggestions that did not occur to his chief. And the gray, soft, mysterious light of early morning came into that conference﻿—through the stained and cobwebbed windowpane﻿—and found a darkness it could not alleviate.

As they talked, the very atmosphere of the room seemed to change. It was tense, poignant as if with the remembered lusts and passions of an earlier, more savage world. The little sounds of a room in which two men talk together﻿—the stir of moving bodies, the creak of furniture, the soft whisper of easy breathing seemed lacking here. Both were motionless as two serpents that lay in their sunbaths on their ledges, and thus an insidious stillness dropped down in the little intervals between their sentences.

The almost total absence of motion on the part of those two conspirators could not have been ignored. It implied only one thing: that their thoughts were so commanding and engrossing that even the almost unconscious movements of the body were suspended. What motion there was, was mostly only the deepening of the lines on their dark faces.

This tenseness, this silence, these submerged passions pointed to but one end. And the crime would not be outside the pale of the laws of man alone, but the basic laws of the forest as well. The feast of death was to take place after all﻿—the same delight of which Broken Fang, the puma, was even then dreaming beside the sheep camp on the far headwaters of Silver Creek. But men, not wild beasts, were to be the debauchers.

===Chinese===
法戈没费多少工夫，便把计划打磨周全了。晨光初露，他便同那墨西哥人何塞把这计划商议了一遍，凡是头儿没有想到的小处，后者都乐于补上几条。清晨那灰白、轻柔而神秘的微光，透过污渍斑斑、结满蛛网的窗玻璃照进这场密议——却照见一片它驱不散的黑暗。

两人说话间，这屋子的气氛本身仿佛都变了。它紧绷刺人，仿佛浸透着一个更早、更蛮荒的世界所遗下的、仍被记忆着的贪欲与凶情。两个男人同屋交谈时惯有的那些细碎声响——身体的挪动、家具的吱呀、匀停呼吸的轻息——在这里似乎一概不见。两人纹丝不动，恰似两条伏在岩台上晒太阳的蛇，于是一股阴悄悄的凝滞，便降落在他们语句之间的短短空隙里。

这两个密谋者身上那种近乎全然的静止，是不容忽视的。它只意味着一件事：他们的心思是那样咄咄逼人、那样攫住了全副精神，连身体上那些近乎无意识的动作都停摆了。要说还有什么动静，多半也只是他们黝黑面孔上的皱纹又深了几道。

这种紧绷、这种沉默、这些沉在底下的凶情，指向的只有一个结局。而这桩罪行将要越出的，不止是人间的法律，还有森林的基本法则。死亡盛宴到头来终究要开席——此刻，在银溪遥远的源头、牧羊营旁边，断牙那头美洲狮正梦着的，也正是这一场快意。只是这回纵情肆虐的，将是人，不是野兽。

===Original===
“It’s the simplest way yet,” Fargo had whispered. The veins stood out in his brutal hands. “That pack of mine are devils﻿—there’s no other word for ’em﻿—and once they get goin’, they’d sweep through that flock of woollies like lightning. You’ve heard of sheep-killin’ dogs before﻿—”

“Yes﻿—but your dogs ain’t never been sheep killers,” the Mexican protested.

“What of that? They can learn fast enough. They’d tear a man to pieces just as quick if I didn’t keep ’em chained. I don’t see why I didn’t teach ’em long ago﻿—they’d be worth a thousand coyotes for keepin’ this country clear of sheep. Maybe you don’t know about sheep-killing dogs. You might not have heard that in the sheep country in the East one dog that once got the habit will spoil the business for miles around. You see, José, most animals don’t kill more than they need; it’s an instinct with ’em, for if they did they’d pay for it by going hungry later. Nature has a way of teachin’ the wild varmints what to do. But dogs have been domesticated so long that they’ve forgotten most of their instincts, and once they get started, once the killin’ fever gets a hold of ’em, they don’t know when to quit. There’s many a dog that has slashed a hundred sheep in one night﻿—jumpin’ from one to another, tearing out one throat after another, and runnin’ the rest till they die. It’s kind of a madness that gets a hold of ’em once they get started. True, my dogs ain’t ever got the habit, but one taste will teach ’em. And they’re half-wild already, as any man well knows who seen ’em tear that little black cub-bear to pieces last week. Just tore him to little scraps of black fur.”

Fargo leaned back in his chair and laughed. The sound burst out suddenly above the even murmur of his talk, and it was no less terrible to hear than the bay of the pack a few minutes before. It was a wild, harsh sound﻿—and African travelers might have been given cause to remember the hyenas, laughing on the sunbaked hills. It pleased him to recall that scene in the forest beyond the creek, in which his pack of dogs had killed the cub. It moved him in unlovely, dark ways. The little bear had been a clumsy, furry, amiable little creature﻿—representing what is perhaps the most lovable breed of all the wild animals﻿—and the pack had made short and terrible work of him.

===Chinese===
「这是到如今为止最省事的一个法子。」法戈低声说道。他那双凶蛮的手上青筋绽起。「我那群狗是一群恶魔——再没有别的字眼好形容它们——一旦撒开了跑，它们扫过那群毛团子，就跟闪电一样快。杀羊狗这种东西，你从前总听说过吧——」

「听说过——可你那些狗从来不是杀羊的狗啊。」墨西哥人提出异议。

「那又怎么样？它们学得快着呢。我要不是拿链子拴着，它们把人撕成碎片也一样快当。我不明白自己怎么早没想到调教它们——论着替这地方清掉羊，它们抵得上一千只郊狼。杀羊狗的门道，你兴许不甚了了。你大概没听说过，在东部养羊的地界，一条狗只要沾上这习惯，方圆多少里的买卖都得叫它搅黄。你要明白，何塞，多数野兽杀生不超过自己的需要；那是它们的天性，因为多杀了，往后就得挨饿还账。造化自有一套法子，教导野地里的害兽什么当做什么不当做。可狗被人豢养得太久，天性差不多忘光了，一旦开了头，一旦那股杀瘾攥住了它们，它们就不知道什么时候收场。有多少条狗一夜之间撕开一百只羊——从一只跳到另一只，一只接一只咬断喉咙，把余下的赶得活活跑死。它们一开了头，就有一种疯病攥住不放。不错，我那些狗还不曾沾过这习惯，可尝一回就学会了。再说它们本来就带着半分野性，上礼拜它们把那只小黑熊崽撕成碎块，见过的谁不清楚。硬是把它撕成了一条条的黑毛碎片。」

法戈往椅背上一靠，笑了起来。那声音陡然从他匀匀的低语声之上炸开，听着不比几分钟前那一阵群狗的吠叫少半分可怕。那是一阵粗野刺耳的声响——非洲的旅人听了，怕要想起烈日炙烤的山丘上鬣狗的笑声。回想起小溪那边林子里、他的狗群咬死小熊的那一幕，他很是受用；那一幕以不体面的、阴暗的路数撩动着他。那头小熊本是个笨拙、毛茸茸、和和气气的小东西——在一切野兽里，那或许是最讨人喜欢的一族——而狗群三下两下便把它结果了，干得又快又惨。

===Original===
“There’s ten of ’em,” Fargo went on, “and there ain’t no one to guard the flock. That one big shepherd dog would last quick﻿—he wouldn’t be able to bluff off them hounds of mine like he could bluff coyotes. And then they’d have the time of their lives﻿—the time of their lives.”

“You mean﻿—take ’em and sick ’em on?” José asked.

“I’ve got a better way than that. Of course one of us will have to take ’em and show ’em the way until they get on the track of the flock, and of course that one will have to be me. I’m the only living man that can handle ’em﻿—you remember the night that old Ben got out and how he pretty near killed that little cowman from Naptha. There’s a little medicine I’ve got to give ’em before I go, and that means﻿—for you to take a little ride over to Newt Hillguard’s.”

José half-closed his eyes. He had begun to understand.

“I’ve always cussed at Newt for keepin’ that little band of Shropshires in his back lot, but I’m glad now he didn’t get rid of them,” Fargo went on. “You’re to bring back a sheep in your saddle﻿—a lamb’ll do, or any old ewe he was about to slaughter. Then, after we get through here, all I’ll have to do is start up the old Horse Creek trail with that pack of dogs. It’ll be a couple of hours before I can get started, and it’ll take till dark to get to the sheep camp, but dark’s the best time for a sheep-killin’ dog to work, anyhow. The sheep are bunched together, and it doesn’t take so much runnin’. And I got to be on hand to call ’em back and round ’em up when they’re done.”

===Chinese===
「它们一共有十条，」法戈接着说，「又没有一个人守着羊群。那一条大个的牧羊犬转眼就会完蛋——它唬得住郊狼，可唬不住我那群猎犬。接下来它们就要过上一辈子最痛快的时辰了——一辈子最痛快的时辰。」

「你是说——牵上它们，撒出去咬？」何塞问。

「我有个比那更妥帖的法子。当然，我们两个里头总得有一个领着它们、给它们引路，一直引到它们咬上羊群的踪迹，而这个人当然得是我。普天之下能驾驭它们的活人，只我一个——老本（Ben）逃出来那天夜里，差一点把纳普撒（Naptha）来的那个小个子牧牛人弄死，你总还记得。我动身之前，还得先给它们喂上一剂小药，这就意味着——要劳你骑马跑一趟，上纽特·希尔加德（Newt Hillguard）家去。」

何塞把眼睛眯起了一半。他开始明白了。

「纽特在他后院里养那一小群什罗普郡羊（Shropshires），我一向没少骂他，如今倒庆幸他没把这群羊打发掉。」法戈接着说，「你回来时，鞍子上要驮回一只羊——羊羔就成，或是他正要宰的随便哪只老母羊也行。然后，等这边的事一完，我只消带着那群狗，顺着马溪的老路出发就是。我得过上一两个钟头才能动身，到牧羊营也得到天黑，不过天黑本来就是杀羊狗下手的最好时辰。羊都挤作一堆，也省得多跑腿。等它们办完了事，我还得在场把它们唤回来、收拢住。」

===Original===
The face of the Mexican was suddenly crafty. “I suppose wantin’ to see the fun hasn’t anything to do with your goin’?” he suggested.

Fargo laughed again. “I’m not sayin’ it won’t be worth watching,” he agreed. “But you know I can always round ’em up with my whistle. José﻿—tonight will see the end of the sheep business for time to come.”

They went about their preparations. They ate their breakfast in the unsavory kitchen, then José rode off to the ranch where Newt Hillguard kept his little flock of thirty Shropshires. There was no particular good in making full explanation to Newt. He was a cattleman surely, his little flock was just a diversion with him, but he might not take fondly to any plan that would make sheep killers out of Fargo’s pack of dogs. Some night they might escape from their yard and visit his own little flock. “The boys say they’re tired of beef and want a mutton blowout,” José explained, “and I’m sent over here to supply it. Will you sell me one of them sheep of yours?”

“Seems funny to me,” Newt returned, “that gang of Fargo’s wantin’ mutton. But I suppose I can sell you one.”

“Any old ewe’ll do,” José went on. “We don’t want ’em to like it so well they’ll want it often. The one you can sell me cheapest. We’ll want it alive, too, ’cause we ain’t goin’ to have him for a day or two. And once I’ve got to pack him on the horse, maybe I’d better take a lamb.”

===Chinese===
墨西哥人的脸上蓦地浮起狡黠。「照我想，想亲眼看一看这场热闹，跟你亲自跑这一趟，不能说全无关系吧？」他试探道。

法戈又笑了。「我不是说这不值得一看。」他认了这一条，「可你知道，我一吹口哨，总能把它们收拢回来。何塞——到今天夜里，养羊这桩买卖，往后就算到头了。」

两人分头着手准备。他们在那间气味难闻的厨房里吃了早饭，随后何塞骑马去了纽特·希尔加德养着一小群三十只什罗普郡羊的牧场。跟纽特把内情挑明，没有什么特别的好处。他固然是个牧牛人，那一小群羊在他不过是个遣兴的玩意儿，可要把法戈那群狗调教成杀羊凶手的主意，他未必乐意领受。保不齐哪天夜里它们逃出围栏，光顾他自己那一小群羊呢。「弟兄们说牛肉吃腻了，想来一顿羊肉的大嚼。」何塞解释道，「打发我过来置办。你那群羊，肯卖我一只不？」

「法戈那伙人竟想吃羊肉，我瞧着倒新鲜。」纽特答道，「不过卖你一只，我想倒也使得。」

「随便一只老母羊就行。」何塞接着说，「我们还不想叫他们吃得那么顺口，隔三差五又想吃。拣你卖得最便宜的那只给我。还得是要活的，这一两天还轮不上吃它。再一说，横竖得把它驮在马背上，我看倒不如牵只羊羔。」

===Original===
The money changed hands, Newt gave in exchange a few pounds of living flesh that blatted feebly and struggled in José’s arms﻿—with a strange, frantic terror﻿—as if in premonition of its doom. The lips of the man set in a straight, cruel line, and he rode with an unjustified swiftness back to Fargo’s house.

There was only one element of mercy in the unmentionable scene that followed in the little, tightly fenced enclosure behind Fargo’s house. None whatever dwelt in the drawn faces of the men, or in the savage, white-fanged creatures that leaped so fiercely at the picket fence as the men approached. But the time, at least, was swift. It went almost too fast for Fargo’s liking.

There was only a single second of strange and dreadful clamor within the enclosure, a glimpse of white in the ravening circle of browns and blacks, a smear of red and a faint cry that the men strained to hear but which was lost in the baying of the dogs. The pack had been given its lesson. Fargo foresaw in their glaring eyes﻿—as they came rushing back to the palings again﻿—the success of his plan.

“Get inside the house and shut the door,” he commanded. “I can’t answer for this bloodthirsty crowd. And do it quick.”

===Chinese===
钱货两讫，纽特交出来的，是几磅活的血肉——它在何塞怀里微弱地咩咩叫着、挣扎着，带着一种古怪的、疯也似的恐怖，仿佛预感到了自己的劫数。那男人的嘴唇抿成一条冷酷的直线，打马便走，以一股毫无来由的火急，赶回了法戈的住处。

随后在法戈屋后那座围得严严实实的小围栏里上演的一幕，简直不堪形诸笔墨，其中只有一点慈悲的成分。而这点成分也绝不在两个男人紧绷的脸上，不在那些扑向尖桩围栏、白牙森森的凶暴畜生身上——两人走近时，它们扑得那样凶狠。只是那工夫，至少是干脆的。快得几乎叫法戈嫌太快了。

围栏里只有短短一秒钟古怪而可怕的喧嚣，贪婪的棕狗黑狗围成一圈，圈中白影一闪，一道红痕，一声细弱的哀叫——两个男人侧耳去听，却叫狗群的吠声吞没了。这一课，狗群算是领过了。当它们重新扑回栅栏跟前时，法戈从它们圆睁的眼睛里，预见了自己的计划必成。

「进屋里去，把门关上。」他命令道，「这帮嗜血的东西，我可担保不了。快去。」

===Original===
For Fargo knew that the sooner he got started the more successful would be his undertaking. He didn’t want their wild excitement to have a chance to die down. The great hounds saw his gun in his arms, a pungent stain of red was still upon their fangs, and they seemed to know that rapturous events were in store for them. The Mexican withdrew, Fargo unlatched the gate of the enclosure, and the animals leaped forth around him. In a moment he had swung on his horse; and followed by the crying pack galloped up the trail.

He rather hoped he would meet no pedestrian on the trail. He had never seen his dogs in such a savage mood, and he began to doubt his own ability to control them. There could be no doubt about the effectiveness of the medicine he had administered. The hunting lust was upon them as never before. And perhaps his eager spirit, his own madness went into them, and excited them all the more.

It was a long, hard ride before he came to that pine-clad heart of Smoky Land which Crowson had rented for a sheep range. He had been able to lope only a small part of the way: the trails were too narrow and steep. The dogs ran more silently now; yet with noticeable eagerness. During the long, still afternoon they were grimly patient, and they skulked like wolfine ghosts through the growing shadows of the twilight.

But their excitement began to return to them as the dark came down. It was the hunting hour: everywhere through the forests the beasts of prey were emerging from their lairs. The hounds were domestic animals, but some of the old wilderness madness revived when the wind came whispering its breathless messages through the trees. Their blood seemed to turn to fire. Time and time again Fargo had to utter the shrill whistle that called them to heel﻿—a signal that he had laboriously taught them and which they now seemed to be forgetting.

===Chinese===
因为法戈心里明白，动身越早，这桩买卖便越有把握。他可不想让这股疯狂的兴奋有机会冷下去。那些高大的猎犬看见他怀里抱着枪，牙尖上还挂着一抹刺鼻的红痕，似乎知道有狂欢的大事正等着它们。墨西哥人退了出去，法戈拔开围栏的门闩，畜生们便绕着他一涌而出。转眼间他已翻身上了马，带着那群嗥叫的狗，顺着小路疾驰而去。

他倒巴望小路上一个行人都碰不上。他从没见过他的狗凶性发作到这般地步，也开始怀疑自己到底管不管得住它们。他喂下去的那剂药灵不灵，倒是无可怀疑了。猎杀的欲火附在它们身上，烧得空前地旺。兴许还有他自己那股急切的心气、他自己那一份疯狂，也渗进了它们身体里，把它们撩拨得愈发按捺不住。

好一段又长又苦的马程过去，他才来到烟之乡那片松林覆盖的腹地——克劳森租下作羊群草场的地方。一路上只有一小段路能放开小跑：小径又窄又陡。这会儿狗群跑得安静多了，却仍带着掩不住的急切。在漫长而岑寂的午后，它们阴沉沉地忍耐着，像狼魂似的潜行，穿过愈来愈浓的暮影。

可随着夜幕四合，它们的兴奋又回来了。这是行猎的时辰：森林里处处有猛兽正出巢离穴。猎犬本是家畜，然而当风穿过树间、气也不喘地低递着它的讯息时，某种古老的荒野疯狂又在它们身上还了魂。它们的血仿佛烧成了火。法戈不得不一次又一次吹出那声唤它们回脚边的尖哨——那是他费了大力气才教会的一个信号，如今它们却像是要忘掉了。

===Original===
Fargo knew these mountains end to end, and he did not often mistake the trails. But soon after nightfall the conviction grew upon him that he was taking much too long to reach the sheep camp. Besides, the mountains didn’t lie in just their proper places. The tall top of Grizzly Peak was too far to the right to suit him. He knew perfectly that he was within a very few miles of the camp, yet he chafed under the delay. And the darkness steadily deepened, dimming the landmarks by which he kept his directions.

He headed on, at first irritated, then apprehensive, finally wrathful and savage. He began to fear that possibly he would have to wait until dawn for the work. The dogs, however, were growing constantly more excited and harder to control.

The last grayness faded into the gloom, and Fargo could hardly see the trail. But he was a mountain man, and he knew what to do. In the last dim glimpses of late twilight the peaks had begun to wheel around where he wanted them to be, and he knew that the camp was now only three or four miles distant at most. And the proper course was to sit down, rest, and wait for the moon to rise. Then he could locate his landmarks, start down the ridge to the river, then up its banks to the camp.

He thought that as far as human beings went, he had the mountains all to himself. He supposed that in the camp﻿—now certainly nearing﻿—a dead herder lay face buried in his tree-bough pallet; but Fargo was not the kind of man to whom such a fact as this preys on the mind. He did not dream that the body had already been found, that the herd was guarded, and that even now the daughter of his enemy was waiting for this same moon﻿—to start forth to find the missing members of the flock.

===Chinese===
这片山从这头到那头法戈都摸得烂熟，认错小径的事他不常犯。可入夜不久，一个念头在他心里越来越坐实：赶到牧羊营这段路，他耗得实在太久了。再者，山也没有待在本该在的位置上。灰熊峰（Grizzly Peak）那高高的峰顶偏向右边，偏得叫他心里不痛快。他明明知道自己离营地不过寥寥几英里，却叫这段耽搁磨得心头发焦。黑暗一个劲儿加深，把他借以辨认方向的地物，一处处都抹暗了。

他继续往前赶，起初烦躁，继而忧心，末了只剩下狂怒与凶暴。他开始害怕，这桩活计说不定得捱到天亮才能做。而狗群却越走越兴奋，越来越难驾驭。

最后一线灰色没入了幽暗，法戈几乎看不清小径了。可他是山里人，他知道该怎么办。凭着迟暮最后几眼朦胧的瞥视，群峰已经开始转动，转到他指望它们在的位置上，于是他知道，营地如今至多不过三四英里远。而稳妥的办法是坐下来歇脚，等月亮上来。到时他再认准地物，从山脊下到河边，再沿岸溯到营地。

就人类而论，他以为这整片山是他一个人独占的。他以为在营地——这会儿肯定挨近了——一个死了的牧羊人脸埋在树枝铺就的铺位里躺着；可是这种事会在心头上啮咬，法戈可不是那号人。他做梦也不曾想到，尸体早已被人发现，羊群已有人看守，而且此时此刻，他仇人的女儿正等着这同一轮月亮——好动身去找羊群里走失的那些成员。

===Original===
Fargo watched the silver glint in the sky; he saw the white disk roll forth. The light grew, it leaped down between the trees, it worked nebulous magic on the floor of the forest, it enchanted the whole wilderness world. He located his peaks. And all at once he knew his exact position﻿—scarcely three miles from the camp and squarely across the ridge.

He got up and started on with the great hounds. At first they were curiously silent and alert. They did not frisk and run as in the first hour on the trail, and to a casual eye their excitement had died within them. For the moment they seemed perfectly under control. Yet Fargo watched them, wondering. They were moving with a peculiar stealth, and once the man caught the unmistakable glare of their yellow-green wolfine eyes in the moonlight.

At that instant Ben, the old pack leader, spoke in the silence. It was a sharp bay, and momentarily every dog stood lifeless. And then with a wild cry they darted into the shadows.

Fargo whistled frantically, but the pack didn’t seem to hear. Their loud and savage bays obscured all sound. Oaths fell from the man’s lips﻿—sounds scarcely less savage than the bay of the dogs themselves﻿—and at first he could see nothing but failure for his scheme.

===Chinese===
法戈望着天际那一线银光；他看着白色的圆盘滚了出来。光亮渐涨，跃下树隙，在林地上施起一片云雾似的魔法，把整个荒野世界都迷住了。他认准了自己的几座山峰。陡然间他知道了自己的确切位置——离营地不到三英里，同营地正好隔着那道山脊。

他起了身，带着那群高大的猎犬继续上路。起初它们出奇地安静而警觉。它们不像上路头一个钟头那样欢蹦乱跑了，在寻常人眼里，它们身体里那股兴奋劲儿像是死掉了。这一刻它们似乎服服帖帖，全在掌控之中。可法戈望着它们，心里犯疑。它们走动时带着一种说不出的蹑手蹑脚，而且有一回，这人撞见了它们那双狼似的黄绿眼睛在月光下无可错认的凶光。

就在那一瞬，狗群的老领头犬本出了声。那是一声尖利的吠叫，刹那间每条狗都僵立不动了。随后，一声狂嗥起处，它们窜进了暗影里。

法戈发了狂似的吹哨，狗群却像什么也没听见。它们又响又凶的吠声盖没了一切声响。咒骂从这人嘴边滚落——那声音之凶暴，比狗群的吠叫本身也不遑多让——起初，他眼里这桩计划，除了失败再看不见别的。

===Original===
It might be, however, that they were right and he was wrong. The dogs were not heading toward the sheep camp. But it was wholly possible that the flock had fed out far that day, had found another drinking place, and thus had not returned to the vicinity of Silver Creek; and the hounds were already on their trail. This theory had to include the death of the wounded shepherd dog, for the first instinct of the animal likely would have been to keep the flock near the tent of his dead master.

His own course was clear. He would cross the ridge to the camp, try to locate the flock, and then attempt to collect his hounds and bring them to it. He believed that if they were on a false scent they would return to him soon.

But the moon looked down and saw that their instincts had told them true. They had crossed the tracks that the flock had made on the outward feeding from the camp that day: they had only to follow it, circle about where Spot, the flock leader, had led, and find the sheep where they had bedded down at night. Yet they seemed to know that certain sport was in store for them before ever they made the long loop around to the sheep camp. For somewhere between was huddled a little group of a hundred stragglers﻿—the band that Hugh had lost during the day’s grazing and which even now Alice had gone forth to find.

===Chinese===
然而，兴许它们是对的，错的倒是他。狗群奔去的方向并不是牧羊营。但完全有可能，羊群这一天放牧放得远了，又另觅了一处饮水的地方，因此不曾回到银溪左近；而猎犬早已咬上它们的踪迹了。这套推想还必须把那条受伤牧羊犬的死也包进去，因为这畜生的头一桩天性，十有八九是把羊群留在亡主帐篷的近旁。

他自己这一边的路数是清楚的。他可以翻过山脊到营地，设法找到羊群，再设法把狗群收拢来，带到羊群跟前。他相信，它们若是追错了踪迹，很快就会回到他身边来。

可月亮俯视下来，看见它们的天性告诉它们的句句是实。它们已经撞上了羊群当天出营吃草时留下的蹄迹：只消顺着蹄迹走，照着领头羊斑点引队的路线兜过去，就能找到羊群夜里卧倒的地方。然而它们似乎知道，在绕那个大圈、兜到牧羊营之前，还有一场现成的快活先等着它们。因为两头之间的什么地方，正蜷缩着一小群百来只的离群之羊——那正是休白天放牧时丢掉的那一群，此时此刻，爱丽丝已经动身出来寻它们了。

## XIX / 第十九章

===Original===
Beyond all other joys and compensations for these three months in Smoky Land, there remained﻿—a subject for humble thanks to the skies﻿—the comradeship of Alice. Every day it made the struggle more worth while, his happiness more complete.

She made periodical trips to his camps, bringing food for himself and salt for his sheep, and assisting him as he moved the flock from range to range. He counted the days of her absence, and the nights that she sat beside his fire simply opened a new world to him. They had a thousand things to tell each other: Hugh’s little victories over coyotes and cougars, the new tricks of Spot the flock leader, some little faithfulness on the part of Shep, the dog, and their exultation at the passing of the days. For if they passed the present month in safety, if Fargo and his gang did not rout them before October first, the victory was won.

She cooked his meals and mended some of the holes in his soiled clothing, and her laugh rang with the bells of fairyland over their silent camp. They had serious moments in which﻿—with far-reaching intimacy﻿—they told each other their own secret thoughts and philosophies of life﻿—sober talk such as was never heard in the rooms of the Greenwood Club. For the first time in his life Hugh had really known self-expression. The inmost springs of his nature flowed forth. And he was constantly amazed and delighted by the girl’s ideas and points of view. There was nothing particularly modern about them. In fact, Hugh thought that they had passed from the earth in his grandmother’s time. Of course they were trite, perhaps they could have been proven ridiculous by the logic of a realist, and the only reason she had for them was the impulse of her own conscience; yet Hugh found himself staring at her as if she were a being from another planet. But Hugh’s amazement was due to his own lack of knowledge of the heart of humanity. He would have been surprised to know that ninety out of every hundred girls, east and west and south and north, had the same illogical conscience, the same high heart, the same ideals. Although he would have tumbled over backward if anyone had told him so, Hugh just hadn’t been around. He had known but one little circle, and heretofore he hadn’t been able to see outside it.

===Chinese===
在烟之乡这三个月里，一切欢愉与报偿之外，还留存着一桩值得向上天谦卑致谢的事——爱丽丝的情谊。它让这场奋斗日日更值得，让他的幸福日日更圆满。

她隔一阵子就往他的营地跑一趟，给他自己捎来吃食，给他的羊捎来盐，还在他把羊群从一片草场转移到另一片草场时搭上一把。她不在的日子，他一天天数着过；而她坐在他火边的那些夜晚，简直给他打开了一个新世界。他们有千言万语要彼此倾诉：休对郊狼和美洲狮的小小胜仗，领头羊斑点玩出的新花样，牧羊犬谢普（Shep）的某桩小小的忠勤，还有他们对时日一天天过去的欢欣。因为只要这个月平安度过，只要法戈和他那伙人没能在十月一日之前把他们撵走，胜利便到手了。

她替他做饭，替他把脏衣服上的破洞缝上几个，她的笑声像仙境的银铃，在他们寂静的营地上空回响。他们也有郑重其事的时刻——彼此怀着深彻的亲密，吐露各自心底的秘密念头和人生哲学——那是格林伍德俱乐部的房间里从来听不到的正经谈话。有生以来，休头一回真正尝到了畅所欲言的滋味。他天性最深处的心泉奔涌而出。而姑娘的种种想法和见解，又时时让他惊异、让他欣喜。这些想法毫无特别新潮的地方。事实上，休觉得它们早在她祖母的时代就已从世上绝迹了。它们当然陈腐，或许用现实主义者的逻辑一推敲，就能证明荒唐，而她信奉它们的唯一理由，不过是自己良心的驱使；然而休发觉自己直勾勾地盯着她，仿佛她是另一个星球上来的生灵。其实，休的惊异只怪他自己不了解人类的心。他若知道东西南北、每一百个姑娘里有九十个都怀着同样不合逻辑的良心、同样高昂的心气、同样的理想，准会大吃一惊。虽然谁要是这么告诉他，他准会仰面跌个跟头，可休实在是没见过世面。他只认识过一个小小的圈子，而且直到如今，他也始终没能望到圈子外面去。

===Original===
Alice was girlish, spontaneous, unaffected to the last degree. Sometimes they romped and raced like children about their camp fire. They exulted over their meals, they told high secrets, they tingled and thrilled in the mystery of the nights. She had a businesslike, matter-of-fact way in going about her tasks that delighted the man past words. She was as clean and sweet and unspoiled as the mountain flowers that grew about her﻿—but wholly able to take care of herself. As she camped beside the flocks her pistol was always in reach of her hand. And the very ideas that Hugh so loved in her simply filled his nights with horror.

Of course it was ridiculous﻿—but the thoughts of lovers often are. And he did love her, with all the earnestness of his wakened being, with a growing, breathless love that seemed natural as living. The point was passed, long ago, wherein he could doubt that fact. And now he didn’t even have self-wonder that he should have come to these mountain realms and loved a mountain girl. It seemed simply his own inevitable destiny.

And he was humble and fearful as a callow boy about the whole thing. Nothing of his herdsman’s life would have been so amusing to the members of the Greenwood Club as this. For Hugh had known women in plenty; he had been an eligible in a circle where eligibles were scarce, and these clubmen thought that his last delusion in regard to them was gone. He had been husband-hunted﻿—and the deer that has been stalked by a cougar no longer sees beauty in the lights of its eyes! But now his old arrogance and sophistication had fallen away from him. He searched frantically and in vain for qualities within himself by which he might hope to deserve this girl’s love.

===Chinese===
爱丽丝一身孩子气，天真直率，朴实到十分。有时他们绕着营火追逐嬉闹，像一对孩子。他们吃一顿饭也欢天喜地，互诉天大的秘密，在夜的神秘里心头发麻、周身颤动。她做事时那副干脆利落、就事论事的做派，让这个男人欢喜得无以言表。她像开在她四周的山花一样洁净、芬芳、未被玷污——却又全然能够照顾自己。她在羊群旁扎营过夜时，手枪总搁在伸手可及的地方。而恰恰是休在她身上如此钟爱的那些想法，偏偏又让他的夜晚充满惊惧。

这自然是荒唐的——可恋人的念头本来就常常荒唐。他确确实实爱着她，以他苏醒的生命全部的赤诚，以一种与日俱增、令人屏息的爱——这爱自然得如同活着本身。能够怀疑这一事实的关口，早已过去了。如今他甚至不再诧异，自己竟会来到这些山国、爱上一个山里的姑娘。这似乎本就是他无可逃避的命运。

而在这一切上头，他谦卑而胆怯，像个乳臭未干的毛头小子。他的牧人生活里，再没有哪一桩会比这更叫格林伍德俱乐部的会员们觉得好笑了。因为休结识过的女人多得很；在一个合适婿选稀缺的圈子里，他曾是个抢手的适婚男子，这些会员们都以为，他对女人最后的一点痴念早已消尽。他曾遭人猎婿——被美洲狮悄悄跟踪过的鹿，再也不会觉得那双眼睛里的光有多少美了！可如今，他旧日的傲慢与世故都已从他身上剥落。他发狂似的在自己身上搜寻种种品质，指望凭它们配得上这姑娘的爱，却一无所获。

===Original===
Had he not been a waster, a slacker from the works of men? Had he anything in general or particular because of which he might expect a girl to love him? The things on which he used to pride himself had been suddenly revealed as so much dust in his hands﻿—inherited wealth, social distinction, an influential ancestry. Here, he rightly concluded, these three things were not worth the powder to blow them up. He did remember, with some pride, that he had a certain amount of physical strength. Already that had been of use to Alice, and the time might come wherein it would be of use again. He was a fairly able sheepman, and he had already earned over a hundred dollars. But he felt like a peasant, offering his heart to the queen of the realm. It got down to a serious matter that as yet he did not feel that he had justified his own existence, that he had established himself in the great world of men and toil, and that he had not yet been purged by the fires of trial and stress, and redeemed in battle.

He mourned her when she left to bring supplies, and welcomed her with the abandon of a child when she came back to him. In the early nights, in the fire’s glow, dreams of her were ever with him, filling him with strange, happy glowings and warm little quivers of delight. But his love for her did not make him forget his sheep. The great test by which his metal was tried lay in his degree of success as herder of the flocks.

Never did he get away from a haunting feeling that here﻿—beside the white sheep﻿—he was face to face with life at last. Here, not in his cities, were the realities, the essentials of life: the feeding flock, the shelter, the circle of firelight into which the powers of the wilderness dared not stalk. Fresh and fresh he felt the age-old appeal of the soil, the love of the throbbing earth, the inner warmth, an undying and wondrous communion with nature. He waged his war against the forces of the wild, and the sense of destiny fulfilled was ever with him. And why not﻿—for were men strangers to the sheep? Could their ancient acquaintance be forgotten in a few little centuries of exile in cities? Had they not been out﻿—through the long course of the ages﻿—under the same stars, felt the same winds, endured the same dangers? In the first dawn of civilization, dim and far away through the mists of the past, the herdsman cared for his sheep in the green pastures﻿—and it was in the blood.

===Chinese===
难道他不曾是个虚掷光阴的浪子、一个逃避人间劳作的懒汉？无论笼统还是具体，他可有哪一样东西，是能指望姑娘因此爱上他的？他从前引以为傲的那些东西，忽然显了原形，不过是他掌中的一把尘土——继承来的财富、社交场上的地位、有势力的门第。在这里，他断得不差：这三样东西，连把它们炸掉的火药钱都不值。他倒确实记得，并且带着几分自豪：自己有一把子气力。这气力已经帮过爱丽丝的忙，日后或许还会再派上用场。他也算得一个相当能干的羊主，而且已经挣下了一百多块钱。可他觉得自己像个庄稼汉，把心捧给这国度的女王。归根结底，事情严重在这里：他至今不觉得自己已经证明了自身存在的理由，不觉得自己已经在人间劳作的大世界里立住了脚，也不觉得自己已经受过了磨难与重压之火的淬炼、在战斗中赎回了自身。

她动身去驮运给养时，他黯然神伤；她回到他身边时，他又像个孩子似的纵情欢喜。初入夜的时分，在火光的红辉里，他的梦里总有她，让他心里充满奇异而幸福的暖光，和一阵阵又暖又微颤的欢喜。可他对她的爱并没有让他忘掉自己的羊。检验他成色的大试炼，就系在他牧放羊群的成功到什么地步上。

有一个感觉他始终摆脱不开，萦绕心头：在这里——在白色羊群的旁边——他终于与生活面对面了。在这里，而不是在他那些城市里，才有着生活的真实与根本：觅食的羊群，栖身之所，荒野的势力不敢贸然踏进的那一圈火光。泥土那古老的召唤，他一回又一回感到新鲜；对这搏动的大地的爱，那内在的暖意，那与自然的一场不灭而奇妙的交融。他向荒野的力量开战，而使命得偿的感觉总伴随着他。为什么不呢——难道人与羊是素不相识的陌路吗？短短几个流放在城市里的世纪，就能把他们古老的交谊忘个干净吗？在漫长岁月的整个进程中，他们岂不曾在同一片星空下露宿、吹过同样的风、熬过同样的凶险？在文明的最初曙光里——隔着往昔的重重迷雾，朦胧而遥远——牧人就在青青牧场上看守自己的羊——而这一切，本就镌刻在血脉里。

===Original===
Sometimes this old acquaintance was recalled to Hugh in dreams. There was one dream in particular that came to him night after night. It never seemed to vary, and its spell always endured a few moments after wakening. So real it was, so vivid, it was almost as if it had been some actual experience in his own life, rather than a remembered vista from the immeasurable past. He always seemed to be sitting, half-dozing, before a fire﻿—a fire not greatly different from that which burned before him in reality. It was always so red, so cheering, that the love of it seemed to shiver his heart to pieces. The forest always stretched about him, silent, mysterious, sinister past all words. And there were always the sheep.

Always, in his dream, he guarded the sheep. It was a matter of life itself. And Death was always waiting for him the instant he relaxed his vigilance. It was not an easy passing, a swift crossing to a happy, bright, quiet land from which he might return and whisper in the night. It was always darkness and cold and pain, and most of all it was fear. The sheep were white in the same moon, the same stars were in the sky. But the tent was absent. Such a thing was without his bourn of thought. In the darkness he dared not leave the fire to seek shelter from the rain. And as he dozed he sat in a somewhat different position﻿—usually leaning forward, his hands locked behind his head. And in the summer nights the hair on his arms drained off the rain.

Shep was still beside him, and the communion between them was even more close than in life. They seemed almost like brothers, rather than master and servant. A peace that was almost rapture abided in their companionship. And it was necessary that Shep remain awake while he himself dozed. But the dog’s outline was vaguely different. The ears were always pointed and erect, the tail was not so lifted, and sometimes when Hugh caught a sudden glimpse of him out of the corner of his eye, a swift wave of icy terror swept over him. The dog was like Cry-in-the-night﻿—that was it! He was gray and white-fanged just like the wolves themselves.

===Chinese===
有时，这场古老的交谊会在梦里被唤醒，回到休的心头。有一个梦尤其特别，一夜又一夜地来找他。它仿佛从不改变，而它的魔力在醒来之后总要持续片刻。它那么真实，那么鲜明，几乎像是他自家生平的一段实历，而不是从无可计量的往昔里记起的一片远景。他总像是半睡半醒地坐在一堆火前——那火同他现实中面前烧着的并没有多大分别。它总是那么红，那么暖人心，对它的爱几乎要把他的心抖成碎片。森林总在他四周铺展，寂静、神秘、阴凶得言语无法形容。而羊群总在那里。

在梦里，他永远在看守羊群。这是性命本身的事。死神永远守候着，只等他一松懈。那不是一场轻松的离世，不是一渡便到一片光明、快乐、安静的国土、还能回来在夜里低语。那永远是黑暗、寒冷和痛苦，而最厉害的是恐惧。月亮还是那个月亮，羊群照样洁白；天上还是那些星星。可是没有帐篷。这样的东西，出离了他所思所想的疆界。黑暗里他不敢离开火去寻个避雨的地方。打盹的时候，他坐的姿势也有些两样——通常是身子前倾，双手交锁在脑后。夏天的夜里，臂膀上的毛发淌着雨水。

谢普仍在身边，他们之间的交融比在现实里还要亲近。他们几乎像一对兄弟，而不是一主一仆。一种近乎狂喜的安宁，栖留在他们的伙伴情谊中。而且在他自己打盹时，谢普必须醒着。只是那狗的轮廓隐隐有些不同。耳朵总是尖尖竖立，尾巴扬得不那么高，有时休的眼角忽然扫到它，一阵冰冷的恐怖便猛地掠过全身。那狗像夜嗥——对了，正是这样！它一身灰毛、一口白牙，跟狼本身一模一样。

===Original===
Even the wind﻿—the air﻿—was different. It was full of smells that stirred the flesh, and hushed little sounds to freeze the blood with dread. It was vibrant and shuddering and alive, and the world had not yet begun to grow old. And he always felt an overwhelming pride in his own strength. His arms﻿—curiously long and quite black with hair﻿—could crush the ribs of Cry-in-the-night like an eggshell. His chest was huge, his legs were knotted and gigantic, and when he saw his reflection in the water of the spring, the hairy growth was long and matted about his throat. And clearest of all the dream was the ring of deadly shadows that ever encroached upon the space of firelight, and the strange twin lights that ever glowed from their depths.

Two and two, everywhere he looked. They always glowed hungrily, and their watch was never done. Curious blue-green and yellow disks of fire, close together and glowing ever in the darkness. Sometimes he would open his lips and shout﻿—and for an instant they would draw back. They were afraid of that wild cry of his, but they were more afraid of the flint dagger that lay at his side. Ah, they died quickly﻿—with a scream and a howl﻿—when the Death Flint went into them. It was a good thing to see, but it also filled the heart with fear. He laughed and exulted when he remembered how it was even quicker in its stroke than the leap of a wolf.﻿—But most of all the watchful circle feared the camp fire. They could not rub the wood and strike the flame: in this he was master, ruler and monarch of the earth! Cry-in-the-night might kill him in a fair fight, but still he could not build a fire. The dreamer always felt a great wave of exultation.

One night he dreamed that the fire burned almost down, and the circle drew close. He wakened from his doze and shouted at them none too soon. And a strange, wild cry from his own lips wakened Hugh, in the sheep camp of the twentieth century, from his dream. He had cried out in his sleep﻿—a hoarse, wild, savage cry that left him curiously awed. And the coyote that had crept close to the flank of the flock slipped quickly back into the forest.

===Chinese===
连风——连空气——都不一样了。它满是搅动血肉的气味，满是压低了嗓子的细响，听得人血液冻僵、心头发怵。它震颤着、悸动着、活着，世界还没有开始变老。他也永远为自己的气力感到一种压倒一切的自豪。他的手臂——长得古怪，覆着乌黑的毛——能像捏碎蛋壳一样捏碎夜嗥的肋骨。他胸膛宽阔巨大，双腿虬结而魁伟，等他在泉水里照见自己的倒影，喉咙四周的须发又长又乱，纠结成毡。而整个梦里最清晰分明的，是那一圈步步侵占火光之地的致命黑影，和从黑影深处永远亮着的、奇怪的一对对光点。

两个两个，他望到哪儿都是。它们永远饿灼灼地亮着，它们的守望永无了时。奇异的蓝绿色和黄色的火盘，紧紧相挨，在黑暗里永远发亮。有时他张开嘴唇一吼——刹那间它们便退开去。它们怕他那一声野性的吼叫，可它们更怕躺在他身边的那柄燧石匕首。啊，死亡燧石（Death Flint）扎进它们身体的时候，它们死得多快——一声尖叫、一声嗥叫。看着是件痛快事，可它也叫人心里充满恐惧。想起它出手比狼的扑跃还要快上一线，他便大笑，便欢腾。——可那一圈守望者最怕的还是营火。它们不会钻木，不会引火：单在这一桩上，他是大地的主人、主宰和君王！夜嗥在堂堂正正的搏斗里兴许杀得了他，可它还是生不起一堆火。做梦的人总感到一阵巨大的欢腾浪头。

一天夜里，他梦见火几乎烧尽了，那一圈黑影逼了上来。他从瞌睡里醒来，朝它们喝了一声，再晚一步就来不及了。而正是他自己唇间发出的这一声奇异、狂野的喊叫，把二十世纪牧羊营里的休从梦中惊醒了。他在睡梦里喊出了声——一声嘶哑、狂野、蛮荒的喊叫，过后连他自己都莫名地生出敬畏。那只早已悄悄蹭近羊群侧翼的郊狼，一溜烟缩回了森林。

===Original===
Hugh got up, stood a moment in the gleam of the late September moon, then piled more wood upon the fire. The circle of twin lights was a reality tonight. The days of drought had brought ever more of the wild hunters about his flock, and almost anywhere he looked he saw their luminous eyes, two by two, as they waited in the darkness. He glanced down﻿—but a rifle, not a dagger of flint, lay at his side. He looked out over the flock, instinctively counting up his markers. Most of the sheep were asleep, but one﻿—the greatest of them all﻿—stood erect, with lowered horns, as if on guard. It was Spot the yearling ram, and his horns looked oddly large in the soft light of the moon.

“Spot, old boy,” the man said, “I believe you’ve got memories too. I believe you’ve been dreaming﻿—just as I have.”

===Chinese===
休站起身，在九月下旬的月光里立了片刻，然后往火里添了些柴。那一圈成对的亮光，今晚是确确实实的现实了。干旱的日子把他羊群四周引来的荒野猎手越来越多，几乎无论朝哪儿望，他都能看见它们在黑暗里守望的一双双发亮的眼睛。他往下一瞥——身边躺着的，是一支步枪，不是一柄燧石匕首。他望向羊群，本能地清点起他的记号羊。羊多半睡了，唯有一只——它们全体中最雄伟的一只——笔直站立着，垂着双角，像在守夜。那是那头一岁公羊斑点，在柔和的月光下，它的角看上去出奇地大。

「斑点，老伙计，」男人说，「我相信你也有记忆。我相信你也一直在做梦——跟我一样。」

## XV / 第十五章

===Original===
Like a song soaring over the camp fire, clear and wonderfully sweet in the hushed and tremulous darkness, Alice’s laugh had been her only goodbye to Hugh. He hadn’t approved of this night journey after the lost sheep. He was new to the mountains, yet he had sensed a vague and whispered menace in the dark forest into which the trail would lead her. He had protested to the last instant; he wanted to go himself, and he had felt a weight of apprehension and concern that she could not explain away. The very breathlessness in the air was in some way sinister; the light patches of moonlight in the big timber only accentuated its mystery, only darkened﻿—by light of contrast﻿—the gloom of the thickets. Yet her only answer had been a laugh.

It was true that the laugh was not really one of mirth. She felt no fear, but she was not amused at his. Rather she had laughed in simple and undeniable joy, and she didn’t quite know its source. It was a little gratifying that this bronzed, well-bred man should be anxious about her. It touched her more than she was ready to admit, even to herself. Yet she really thought that his concern was unjustified.

She knew these mountains. She felt that in some measure at least she had mastered them. She could shoot straight with her pistol: if indeed Broken Fang﻿—the great cougar that had begun to display a rather disquieting arrogance in the presence of men﻿—should find and follow her trail, she had full confidence that the fire from her six pistol cartridges would frighten him away. She had lived long enough in the mountains to know that rare and few are the wild animals that will even menace human beings. The coyotes were abject cowards, the ordinary run of cougars﻿—although sometimes given to trailing a night wanderer for long and unpleasant hours on the mountain trails﻿—always seemed to lack courage to attack, and the wolves would not have their pack strength until the winter. The wolf pack in the snow, she knew perfectly, was not a thing to trifle with. But the gray creatures were living singly now, or in pairs, and starvation had not yet come upon them. In these days of full feeding they were scarcely braver than the coyotes. It seems to be true that any animal that hunts in packs or groups acquires a ferocity, a wild and frenzied courage not possessed by the lone hunter. Partly it is a matter of mob psychology, partly a sense of resistless strength﻿—but its reality naturalists are unable to deny. It can be seen even in the swarms of miniature ants that﻿—with appalling ferocity﻿—will attack the greatest creatures of the wild. But there were no packs here. The moonlit aisles were safe.
===Chinese===
像一支掠过营火升上夜空的歌，在屏息而微颤的黑暗里清亮得出奇、甜美得出奇——爱丽丝的笑声，便是她给休的全部道别。他不赞成这次为走失的羊群夜出的行程。他到山里日子还浅，却已从那条将引她深入的黑森林里，觉出一股隐隐约约、如低语般的凶险。他一直抗议到最后一刻；他想自己去，心头压着一副她解释不开、也宽解不下的忧惧与挂念。连空气里那股屏息凝滞的劲儿都有几分不祥；巨木林间一片片斑驳的月光，反倒把林子的神秘衬得更浓，借着对比之光，把灌木丛的幽暗映得更黑了。可她唯一的回答，仍是一声轻笑。

说实在的，那声笑并非真出于欢愉。她并不害怕，却也不是被他的忧惧逗乐了。她那一笑，毋宁是发自一种单纯而无可否认的喜悦，连她自己也说不清这喜悦打从何处来。这位肤色黝黑、教养良好的男子竟会为她悬心，多少叫她心里受用。这份触动之深，超过了她肯承认的限度——哪怕对她自己承认。然而她确实认为，他的担心全无道理。

她认得这些山。她觉得至少在某种程度上，自己已经把它们降伏了。她的手枪打得很准：倘若断牙——那头在人前渐渐露出一股颇为不安的傲气的大美洲狮——当真寻见并跟上她的踪迹，她也满心相信，手枪里六发子弹喷出的火光足以把它吓退。她在山里住得够久，知道肯主动招惹人类的野兽何等稀少罕见。郊狼是十足的懦夫；寻常的美洲狮——虽说有时会沿着山径尾随夜里赶路的人，一跟就是漫长而不痛快的几个钟头——却似乎总是欠缺攻击的胆量；狼则要到入冬才攒得起集群的力量。她心里透亮，雪地里的狼群可不是好惹的。可眼下这些灰色的家伙还各自单飞，或成双成对，饥饿也还没有降临到它们头上。在这些吃得饱足的日子里，它们不见得比郊狼勇敢多少。凡是成群结伙捕猎的动物，都会生出一种孤身猎手所没有的凶悍、一种狂野而癫烈的胆气——这规律似乎确实成立。一半是群体心理作祟，一半是自觉力量不可抗拒——但它的真实存在，连博物学家也无法否认。就连那一群群小小的蚂蚁——以骇人的凶暴——去攻击荒野中最庞大的巨兽时，也显出这一点。可这里没有狼群。月光洒下的条条林间廊道，是安全的。

===Original===
Yet she did not want to go. It was as if the voice of reason within her﻿—the voice that urged her forth﻿—were obscured by the incorporeal voices that spoke in her inner being. One was that of the forest, and it spoke in warning. “Come not on to my darkened trails,” it might have said. “They are thine in the daylight hours, but at night they belong to the beasts. This is the time of talon and fang, and thy flesh is tender. I am old and wise, but also I am new, with the newness of the young world. And my spirit is Death.” And the other voice was that of the ruddy camp fire behind her. “Stay, stay, stay,” it crackled. “Here is thy hearth and thy heart. Stay, tender one.”

As the shadows encroached and the firelight grew ever more dim, she found herself looking back﻿—again and again﻿—to the bright blaze. Tonight it was home, and these forest trails were dark and silent. She entered the forest, but ever she saw its cheering gleam between the trees﻿—a bright haven of refuge where Danger could not come. She didn’t entirely understand. She had ventured forth into the forests at night before, but never with such regret.

She could still see the form of Hugh seated beside the fire; and the sight moved her strangely. But she would not admit﻿—even to herself﻿—that part of the appeal of that bright circle of light was due to his presence. It had never seemed so dear, so much like home before, but it could not be because of the straight, manly form that abode there. Was he not just a lowly sheep herder, a weakling whose metal had been proven false in the crucible of life? What did he mean to her: just an employee that would soon pass on to other occupations. Yet she had felt wholly secure and comforted beside his fire. She remembered the play of his shoulders as he had hewed the fir log; and she found herself longing for his protection now. Yet what right had she to think that this weakened city man would be a fort in time of stress? She was of the mountain strain, and unlike many of her city cousins she did not accept the fact of his masculinity alone as being a tower of strength. Such dangers as did abide in the Smoky Land forest were no respecters of the males of the species: strength and courage alone must be tried and proven. Hugh had failed in life, she thought; why should he not also fail in courage and strength? The mountain women do not love weak men. They are down to realities, life is a constant battle for existence, and they want a warrior﻿—whether gentleman or not﻿—beside them through the long, dangerous hours of night. Hers were the mountain standards. And what could she expect from Hugh?
===Chinese===
然而她并不想走。仿佛她心中理智的声音——那催她动身的嗓音——被在她内心深处说话的两个无形无质的声音遮蔽了。一个是森林的声音，说的是警诫。『不要踏进我幽暗的小径，』它仿佛这样说。『白昼时分它们归你所有，到了夜里，便归于走兽。这是爪与牙的时辰，而你的血肉娇嫩。我苍老而智慧，却也崭新，带着年轻世界的新鲜。我的魂灵，是死亡。』另一个声音，是她身后红彤彤的营火。『留下吧，留下吧，留下吧，』它噼啪作响。『这里是你的炉灶，是你的心。留下吧，娇嫩的人儿。』

暗影步步进逼，火光愈见昏微，她发觉自己一次又一次回望那团明亮的火焰。今夜它就是家，而这些林间小径又暗又静。她走进森林，却始终望得见火光在树隙间那令人心安的闪亮——一座危险不能近身的明亮避风港。她不十分明白这道理。从前她夜里也不是没有闯进过森林，可从不曾怀着这样的怅惜。

她还能望见休坐在火旁的身影；那景象令她心头异样地一动。可她不肯承认——哪怕对自己承认——那圈亮光的魅力，有一半是因为有他在那里。它从前从不曾显得这样亲切、这样像个家，可这绝不可能是因为守在那里的那个挺直的、有男子气概的身影。他不就是个卑下的牧羊人，一个在生活的坩埚里验出成色有假的软汉子？他对她又算什么：不过是个雇工，很快就要转投别的营生去。然而在他的火旁，她曾感到全然的安全与熨帖。她记得他劈那截冷杉木时肩头运转的劲道；此刻她发觉自己竟在渴望他的护持。可她有什么权利指望，这个在城里虚了元气的男人到了危难时会是一座堡垒？她生就山地的血统，不似她那些城里的表亲，并不单凭『他是男人』这一条，就把他当作力量的高塔。烟之乡林间确实潜伏的种种凶险，并不对世上的男性另眼看待：力量与胆气，唯有经过试炼才算数。休在人生里已经败过一场，她想；凭什么他在胆气与力量上就不会再败一场？山地的女子不爱软弱的男人。她们只认实打实的东西，生活是一场为求生存而不歇的鏖战，她们要的是一个战士——管他绅士不绅士——陪在身旁，共度夜里那漫长而凶险的时辰。她持的是山地的标准。而她又能指望休什么呢？

===Original===
She headed into the forest, and she saw the light of the camp fire wink out behind her. It left her singularly alone. A vague depression came upon her, an uneasiness that she could not name or place. There was a sense of utter isolation never felt before. The feel of her pistol butt in her pocket﻿—her belt had been packed among the supplies﻿—reassured her. Then she hastened on, down the moonlit trail.

The forest was never so mysterious. The moonlight had struck away all sense of familiarity. The silver patches between the trees were of fairyland, the dusk of the shadowed thicket was incredibly black; even the changeless pines, majestic and inscrutable emblems of the wilderness, were like great, nebulous ghosts of giants. The woodland was full of ghosts: fleet-footed phantoms that sped along the trail before her, ghostly shadows that leaped behind; little, feeble ghosts of noises that couldn’t be real, and ghostly messages in the wind that whimpered and cried in the distant thickets. Yet she could not feel the wind’s breath on her face. Rather the forest was breathless, tense, vaguely sinister as never before.

Steep was the trail she took, and ever the silence seemed to deepen. She kept watch ahead for the flock, pale white in the moonlight. She found herself listening closely for any sound that might indicate their position, either the faint bleat of the ewes or the triumph cries of such beasts of prey that had killed from their numbers. But while she did not hear these things, the silence was full of other, lesser sounds. All the creatures of the forest were stirring in their night occupations, and so deep and unfathomable was the stillness that it seemed to her that she could discern their each little motion. Sometimes it was the distant tread of the deer in the buckbush. No rains had fallen since April; the brush was dead and dry as she had never before seen it, and even the stalking folk made a misstep occasionally. She heard the Little People stirring in the leaves; once a gopher, once a porcupine rattled his quills with a pretended fierceness a short distance off the trail, and once a lynx mewed like a domestic cat behind her.
===Chinese===
她朝森林里走去，眼见营火的火光在身后倏地一闪，灭了。这一灭，把她孤零零地撂下了。一阵茫然的低沉袭上心头，一种她叫不出名目、也寻不着来处的惶惶不安。有一种从未体味过的、彻头彻尾的孤绝之感。口袋里手枪枪柄的触感——她的腰带已经随给养打进了驮包——让她稍觉安心。随后她加快脚步，顺着月光铺洒的小径走下去。

森林从不曾这样神秘。月光拂去了一切熟悉之感。树与树之间的银斑恍如仙境，背阴灌木丛里的昏暝黑得叫人难以置信；就连那些亘古不变的松树——荒野庄严而莫测的徽记——也像巨人们庞大而朦胧的幽灵。林地里满是鬼影：疾足的幻魅在她前头沿小径掠过，幽灵似的暗影在身后腾跳；还有那些细碎而虚弱的、不似真有的声响之鬼，以及风里捎来的鬼魅口信——那风正在远处的灌木丛中呜咽、啼泣。可她脸上感觉不到风的气息。毋宁说，是森林屏住了呼吸，紧绷着，透出一股前所未有的、隐隐的凶邪。

她走的这条小径陡得很，四下的沉寂似乎愈陷愈深。她一路提防着前方的羊群——月光下一片煞白。她不由得竖起耳朵，细听任何能透露它们方位的声响：或是母羊隐约的咩叫，或是已从羊群中得了手的食肉兽那得意的嗥号。可这些她都没有听见，寂静里却满是别的、更细碎的声音。森林里的众生灵都在忙着各自的夜活计，那静谧深不见底，她仿佛能分辨出它们每一个细小的动作。有时是远处鹿蹄踏过鹿灌木（buckbush）丛的声响。四月以来滴雨未落；灌木枯槁焦干，干得她从未见过，连那些潜行的族类偶尔也会踏错一步。她听见小小部族（Little People）在落叶间窸窣活动；有一回是一只囊鼠（gopher），有一回是一只豪猪在离小径不远的地方，带着佯装的凶态把一身针毛抖得簌簌作响，还有一回，一只猞猁在她身后喵喵地叫，活像一只家猫。

===Original===
She hastened on. She turned into a little valley that she knew﻿—a place the mouth of which was obscured by brush and in which a wing of the flock might have been easily lost. And then she found the white band, bedded down for the night.

There had been casualties earlier in the evening, but mostly the beasts of prey had not yet found them. She started to drive them the long three miles back to camp.

Still the moonlight worked its conjurations in the forest; and she felt a growing discomfiture. At first she laid it to nerves. She had been tired after the day’s ride, and perhaps the long walk after the sheep had overtaxed her already exhausted body. The sense of oppression, of distant and unfamiliar peril in the forests about her grew ever more pronounced. She tried in vain to hurry the sheep. Never, it seemed to her, had they moved so slowly. The first mile took interminable ages, the second was too long for belief. No luxury, no achievement by which women usually gauge happiness could mean so much to her tonight as that little bright circle in the distant camp over which Hugh stood guard.
===Chinese===
她加紧赶路。她拐进一条自己熟识的小山谷——谷口被灌木遮掩，羊群的一翼最容易走失在这样的地方。随后她找到了那一条白色的带子——已经卧下过夜的羊群。

傍晚早些时候这里已经折损了几只，不过大体上说，食肉兽还没有找上它们。她开始把它们往回赶——长长的三英里路，一直赶回牧羊营。

月光依旧在林中施行着它的幻术；她觉出一种愈来愈重的不自在。起初她把这归之于神经过劳。白日里骑了一天的马，她本已困乏，追着羊群走了这一长路，只怕是把早已疲惫的身子又透支了一回。那种受压迫之感，那种四下林莽间遥远而陌生的凶险之感，愈来愈分明。她想催快羊群，却是徒劳。在她看来，它们从不曾走得这样慢。头一英里走过了无穷无尽的久远，第二英里长得叫人不敢相信。今夜，任何奢华的享受、任何女子惯用来掂量幸福的成就，都比不上远处牧羊营里那一小圈亮光——休守卫着的那一圈——在她心上的分量。

===Original===
She was on the last mile by now, and she kept straining to see the first gleam of the camp fire through the trees. Perhaps all her apprehension had been but fancy, after all. It was only a little way farther﻿—scarcely a mile﻿—and the adventure would soon be over. The forest was oddly hushed and breathless.

And at that instant a strange cry came tingling up to her through the unfathomable depths of forest. It was such a sound as does not catch the consciousness immediately, beginning too dim and faint even to recognize as sound, and at first she found herself doubting its reality. The least rustle of the thickets beside her, the faintest stir of the distant wind drowned it out. Yet with such gradual encroachments as the hour hand makes on the face of the watch, it swelled and grew until all disbelief was dead, and all other sensation transcended.

The deep silence of the primeval forest alone had enabled her to detect it at first, but its quality of obscurity slowly passed away. Soon she had begun to have some idea of the quality of its tones. It was slowly, steadily gaining, and the only inference could be that whatsoever made the sound was coming toward her at an incredible pace﻿—something that ran behind her and cried out in beastial savagery.
===Chinese===
此刻她已走上最后一英里，一路极力张望，想从树隙间望见营火的第一线微光。说不定她那些忧惧，到头来不过是凭空的想象。再走一小段——不足一英里了——这场历险就要收场。森林出奇地静，静得屏住了呼吸。

就在这一瞬，一声怪异的嗥叫穿过森林深不可测的深处，铮铮地传上她的耳畔。这样的声音并不能立刻攫住人的意识，它起头太微太弱，弱得甚至认不出是声音，起初她简直疑心它是否真实。身旁灌木最轻的一下窸窣，远处微风最淡的一丝拂动，都能把它淹没。然而它像钟面上的时针那样步步侵移，渐渐胀大、渐渐增长，直到一切怀疑尽数死灭，一切别的感觉都被它盖过。

全凭原始森林的深沉寂静，她才起初便捕捉到了它；而它那层晦暗的质地慢慢褪去。很快，她开始摸出这声音的调子有个什么路数。它在缓慢而稳步地逼近，唯一的推断只能是：无论那发声的是什么，正以难以置信的速度朝她而来——那东西在她身后奔跑，带着兽性的凶残嚎叫。

===Original===
A frantic flood of thoughts swept over her: blind hopes that the cool depths of her subconsciousness refused to accept. Perhaps the cries were of some wilderness hunter on the trail of deer﻿—a trail that closely coincided with her own. In a moment it would be just a thing to laugh at and forget. But even her own prayers, her own unquenchable spirit of optimism could not make the truth untrue. The grim fact slowly grew and strengthened that whatever ran behind her was on her own trail, that she was being remorselessly hunted through the still aisles of the forest.

The wild cries were louder now, evolving from vague and distant rumblings to prolonged and savage bays, ferocious as any wilderness cry she had ever heard. It sounded like a *pack*﻿—that terrible organization that knows no fear and against which not even the stately elk can stand. The cries had a strange exultant quality, a sense of power, and at the same time the hunting lust that can be discerned in the yell of the wolf pack in their first strength of autumn. Yet this was no autumn. The leaves were yet unfallen. The wolves were still mated, or else ran in pairs. And a great fear began to creep like a poison through her veins.

And ever the chorus grew louder, swelling into a veritable thunder that seemed to shudder, with long, undulating waves of sound, through the hushed air. It seemed to her that she heard the distant pound of running feet on the trail. And now she no longer gave thought to what these hunters might be. It no longer mattered. She only knew that some new and terrible peril was leaping forth upon her, a ferocious enemy that would contest her effort to reach the safety of the fire. The sheep broke into a run, and sobbing she sped after them.
===Chinese===
一阵狂乱的思绪洪流漫过她心头：尽是些盲目的指望，她潜意识清凉的深处不肯接受。说不定那嗥叫来自某个在兽道上追鹿的荒野猎手——一条恰好与她自己的路径几乎重合的道儿。再过片刻，这就只是一桩可以一笑置之、转身便忘的事了。可哪怕是她的祷告，她那股浇不灭的乐观心气，也没法把事实变成不是事实。那冷峻的事实慢慢壮大、慢慢坐实：在她身后奔跑的不管是什么，追的正是她自己的踪迹；她正在森林寂静的廊道里，被毫不容情地追猎着。

那野性的嗥叫此刻更响了，从模糊而遥远的低吼，演成一声声悠长而凶蛮的吠嗥，凶烈如她平生所闻的任何荒野之声。那听起来像是一支*狼群*——那个不知恐惧为何物的可怕组织，连仪态庄严的马鹿在它面前也站不住脚。嗥声里有一种奇异的得意，一种力量之感，同时又带着秋初狼群初盛时那声声嚎叫里辨得出的猎杀欲狂。然而现在并不是秋天。树叶还没有落。狼还在配偶相伴，或至多两两同行。于是一种巨大的恐惧，像毒液一样，开始在她的血管里爬行。

那合唱愈来愈响，涨成一片真正的雷霆，仿佛带着一道道悠长起伏的声浪，震颤着穿过屏息的空气。她仿佛听得见小径远处奔跑的脚步擂鼓般敲响。此刻她已不再去想这些猎手可能是什么。那已经无关紧要了。她只知道，一场崭新而可怕的凶险正向她扑来，一个凶残的敌手，要同她争夺抵达火光庇护的一番努力。羊群撒腿奔逃，她呜咽着，飞快追上前去。

## XVI / 第十六章

===Original===
Landy Fargo tried to approach the sheep camp with some caution. It was an instinctive effort: he had not the slightest idea that the lone human occupant of the little meadow could waken from his deep sleep to hear him. Nevertheless, Fargo didn’t believe in taking unnecessary chances. He rode slowly, trying to avoid the whip of dry brush against the horse’s body. Yet it was to be noticed that the coyotes, lingering hungrily at the flanks of the flock, slipped from the trail when he was still two hundred yards distant.
The thickets were unusually dry: they cracked and popped as the horse passed through. Miniature explosions of popping twig and crackling brush followed every step of the horse.
Still there was no one to hear him. The forest was breathless. And all at once, through a rift in the underbrush, he saw the gleam of Hugh’s camp fire.
===Chinese===
兰迪·法戈接近牧羊营时，多少存了几分小心。这纯粹是本能使然：他压根儿没有想到，小草甸上那位独居的人竟会从沉睡中醒来听见他的动静。话虽如此，法戈向来不肯冒无谓的风险。他骑得很慢，尽量不让干枯的灌木抽打着马身。可即便这样也看得出：那些贪馋地盘桓在羊群两侧的郊狼，在他还隔着两百码时，就悄悄溜出了小径。
灌木丛干得异乎寻常：马匹穿行其间，一路噼啪炸裂。枝条迸断、枯柴脆响，马的每一步后面都跟着一串小小的爆响。
仍旧没有谁听见他。森林屏着气息。蓦地，透过下木丛的一道豁缝，他望见了休的营火闪出的微光。

===Original===
For an instant Fargo’s human faculties simply and utterly deserted him; and he stood gaping like a beast at the guttering flame. A strange little shiver of cold and fear crept over him. He had expected only moonlight and silence, perhaps the bedded sheep unwatched and a heap of gray ashes, but a herder’s well-mended fire had not had a place in his calculations at all. Twenty-four hours had passed since the murder, and yet the flame still flickered like a soul that could not pass. Was it a ghost fire: was the shapeless shadow that he thought he could make out beside it the specter of one who had risen from Death to watch the sheep? The sight went straight home to his dark superstitions.
Just for a moment he sat motionless in the saddle; then he started to turn back. His eyes bulged ever so slightly. And then a great cold seemed to come down, stab, and transfix him.
For a voice spoke from the camp. It came clear and strong into the darkness where he waited. “Who’s there?” someone asked.
===Chinese===
刹那间，法戈身上属于人的官能干脆彻底弃他而去；他张着嘴，呆呆望着那行将熄灭的火苗，活像一头野兽。一阵又冷又怕的细微战栗悄悄爬上他的身子。他原本只料到月光和寂静，顶多再加上无人看管、卧地过夜的羊群和一堆灰白的余烬，至于一堆牧羊人添得匀匀实实的旺火，压根不在他的算计之列。凶案至今已过了二十四小时，那火苗却仍在闪动，像一个不得超度的亡魂。莫非是鬼火？他自以为在火旁辨出的那团不成形的暗影，莫非是从死神那里爬起来看守羊群的幽魂？这光景直截戳进了他心底阴暗的迷信里。
有那么一瞬，他僵坐鞍上，一动不动；随即他开始拨马回身。他的双眼微微凸了出来。跟着，一股巨大的寒气仿佛兜头压下，一刺，把他钉在了原地。
因为营地里响起了一个说话的声音。它清晰而有力，传进他候着的那片黑暗里。「什么人？」有人问。

===Original===
Except for his sudden gusty breathing, Fargo made no sound in reply. He started to turn his horse.
“If you don’t answer, I’ll think it’s a coyote and shoot,” the voice came again. “I give you till I count three﻿—”
Fargo had won his point by bluffing many times, he had known how to call the bluffs of other men; but he had no delusions about the hard, quiet voice that came out to him from the fireside. Very plainly the man meant what he said. But at least it wasn’t Dan the herder who had risen and spoken. The tones and words were not the melodious utterances of the Italian laborer who had been Hugh’s predecessor. The only other explanation that occurred to Fargo was that the murder had been discovered, and the man who had called him was an officer of the law who had been put on guard.
===Chinese===
除了一阵突然发作的、呼哧带喘的呼吸，法戈没有作声回答。他动手拨转马头。
「再不回话，我就只当你是郊狼，开枪了，」那声音又传了过来。「我数到三——」
法戈靠虚声恫吓赢过许多回，也懂得怎么揭穿别人的唬人把戏；可对火边传来的这个冷硬沉静的声音，他不存半点妄想。明摆着，这人说得到做得到。但至少，起身开口的不是牧羊人丹。那嗓音、那措辞，都不是休的前任、那个意大利劳工说话时那副悦耳如乐的腔调。法戈能想到的另一种解释只有一条：凶案已经败露，出声喝住他的是奉命看守的执法官员。

===Original===
Fargo instantly decided it would not be wise to attempt to disobey these summons. Like most wrongdoers he had an abject horror and fear of the law, and the moment was of the greatest terror he had ever known. Yet he dared not turn and flee. In his panic he was unable to remember that not in one chance out of a hundred could a bullet find him in the darkness. For all he knew the man at the fire was already staring at him through his sights, and possibly the whole camp was surrounded by the officers. His mouth felt dry, his hands numb as he rode out into the circle of firelight.
And then his fear changed in a moment to devastating rage. The form was revealed quite clearly now: simply that of a lowly sheep herder in soiled clothes and with unshaven face. Had José lied about the murder? Yet this man was not Dan, the herder. There was nothing to believe but that Crowson had already discovered the crime and had hired a substitute.
There was nothing to fear here. His arrogance swept back to him and his eyes leaped savagely over the trim form that now had risen to greet him. It was a slender figure, the kind he could hammer to paste beneath his flailing fists. He swung down from the saddle, once more feeling himself completely master of the situation.
===Chinese===
法戈当即断定，想违抗这声传唤绝非明智之举。像大多数作恶之徒一样，他对法律怀着卑贱的恐怖和畏惧，而眼下的时刻，是他平生所知最深的一场惊怖。可他又不敢拨马逃走。慌乱里，他记不起这样一个道理：黑暗之中，子弹一百发里也未必有一发找得上他。说不定火边那人已经透过准星盯死了他，说不定整座营地都叫执法官员们围了个水泄不通。他口干舌燥，双手发麻，催马走进了火光的圈子里。
转瞬之间，他的恐惧化作了一场灭顶的狂怒。那人的身形此刻已看得分明：不过是个衣裳污秽、连脸也没刮的低贱牧羊人。莫非何塞那桩凶案办得不实、扯了谎？可这人又不是牧羊人丹。除此之外再无可信的：克劳森已经发觉了罪行，雇了个替工。
这儿没什么好怕的。他的骄横一齐涌回身上，两眼凶野地扫过那具此刻已站起身来迎客的利落身形。那是副瘦长的身架，正是他一双抡开的拳头可以砸成肉酱的那一类。他翻身下了鞍，重新觉得自己又是局面的十足主宰。

===Original===
“What do you mean?” he demanded savagely, “hollerin’ out and threatenin’ me that way.”
Hugh looked at him, considering just what he had meant. And perhaps his lips drew up in a faint smile. If there was one thing his experience with the sheep had taught him, it was to smile: smile at misfortune, smile at the little, everyday comedy of life﻿—and smile with real amusement at such storming, bullying men as this. But it was true that a moment before he had not been in the humor for mirth. He had known at once that the step in the darkness was not that of the coyote, or any of the hunters of the wild. It was a horse, and its rider could kill from a distance: perhaps it was the same foe that had crept into camp the previous night and had murdered Dan. His voice﻿—he remembered with a strange, inward pleasure﻿—had sounded level and clear; but nevertheless a wholly justified apprehension had been upon him. He had been entirely at a disadvantage. He made a fair target beside the leaping flame and he could not see his enemy at all. And he was still somewhat white about the lips as he stood up.
And if Fargo had only remained silent, Hugh would have been willing to have welcomed him at his fire. The loneliness of the wild places was already upon him, and any stranger that walked those darkened hills might have found shelter in his own tent. And this was the man who﻿—a few days before﻿—had been inwardly proud that he never made chance acquaintances, that he never accepted another for friendship or discourse except through the channels of his own social plane. It wasn’t being done by the men he knew: to arrive at any comradeship without first a correct introduction and then a certain amount of preliminary. How great had been the change! Yet in this case the man was obviously unfriendly; and Hugh slowly stiffened beneath his angry gaze.
===Chinese===
「你这是什么意思？」他凶横地喝问，「嚷嚷出声，还这么样威吓我。」
休望着他，掂量这话到底是什么意思。他的嘴唇兴许微微一牵，浮起一丝笑意。倘若说跟羊群打交道的经历教会了他哪一样本事，那就是微笑：对厄运微笑，对生活里日日上演的小小喜剧微笑——而对这等咆哮霸道的人物，则由衷觉得好笑。不过方才那一刻，他实在没有打趣的心情，这倒是真的。他一听便知，黑暗里那阵脚步不是郊狼的，也不是荒野中任何猎手的动静。那是匹马，马上人能隔空取人性命：说不定正是前一夜摸进营地、害了丹的那个仇敌。他的嗓音——他怀着一种奇异的、发自心底的快意记起——听着平平稳稳、清清亮亮；可他身上当时确实压着一份全然有理的忧惧。他那时吃足了亏：在跳动的火苗旁边，他是个再合适不过的靶子，而敌人却半点看不见。此刻站起来，他嘴唇边上仍带着几分苍白。
而倘若法戈肯一声不响，休本会乐得迎他到火边坐坐。荒僻之地的孤寂已经缠上了他，任何一个走在这片昏黑山岭里的生人，都可能在他自己的帐篷里寻到安身之处。而就是这么一个人——就在几天之前——还曾暗自得意：他从不去结交萍水相逢的路人，除了经由自己那个社交圈层的门路，他从不接纳任何人做朋友、做谈伴。在他认识的人里头，没人这么办事：不经一回正规的引荐、再补上相当数目的铺垫，就想结下任何交情。这变化何其之大！可眼下这一位，明摆着不怀好意；在那双怒眼的逼视下，休慢慢地绷紧了。

===Original===
“What do you mean by it?” the man demanded. “Hollerin’ out?”
“Why, I meant﻿—” Hugh replied, in a perfectly casual tone, “exactly what I said. That I’d shoot if you didn’t reveal yourself. I’m against coyotes, wild or human. And what are you doing here?”
Fargo noted with some amazement that the tables had been﻿—as if by a magician’s magic﻿—instantly turned about; and that he himself was no longer the inquisitor. He bristled, furious that this lowly herder should not instantly yield to his own superiority. Yet he suddenly remembered certain little facts that tended to restrain him. The man was in his rights: and perhaps it was best to have some explanation for his presence on the night following a murder.
===Chinese===
「你这话到底什么意思？」那人追着质问。「嚷嚷什么？」
「哦，我的意思是——」休答道，语调随便极了，「正是我说过的那句话。你不肯露面，我就开枪。我跟郊狼过不去，野的也罢，人形的也罢。你在这儿做什么？」
法戈颇为惊愕地觉察到：局面竟像被魔术师变戏法一般，霎时间翻转了过来；他自己反倒不再是那个盘问人的了。他毛发倒竖，气恼这个低贱的牧羊人竟不立马屈从于他的威势。可他蓦地想起几桩小小的事实，教他不得不有所收敛。这人占着理：再说了，一起凶案过后的当夜自己在此地出现，有个说法总归妥当些。

===Original===
“Don’t go making any inferences you’ll regret later,” he warned. “I’m bear huntin’﻿—got a pack of dogs out there somewhere, and they got away from me.” He stepped one pace nearer. “And I want you to know I’m not expectin’ any back talk from such as you. All I’d have to do was to say the word, and old Crowson would fire you in a minute.”
“You’re one of his friends, are you?” Hugh asked easily.
“He’ll do what I say﻿—don’t you mind about that.”
===Chinese===
「可别由着性子瞎推想些什么，回头叫你自己后悔，」他警告道。「我是出来猎熊的——一群狗撒在外头什么地方，跑得没了踪影。」他朝前跨近一步。「我还要你知道，你这么号人，我可不打算听你还嘴。我只消说一句话，老克劳森一分钟里就把你开革了。」
「这么说，你是他的一路朋友喽？」休随口问道。
「我说什么他做什么——这一层你不用操心。」

===Original===
“Then perhaps”﻿—Hugh struggled an instant and caught at a name that Alice had spoken﻿—“you’re José Mertos.”
Fargo started﻿—hardly perceptibly﻿—and caught himself at once. “Do I look like a Mexican?” he demanded.
“Just a bit stout for a Mexican,” Hugh went on appraisingly. He didn’t know why, but a slow anger had begun to take hold of him. “Then maybe you’re﻿—Fargo.”
===Chinese===
「那么，说不定」——休迟疑了一刹那，抓过一个爱丽丝说起过的名字——「你就是何塞·梅托斯。」
法戈猛地一震——几乎察觉不出来——随即就稳住了。「我看着像墨西哥人吗？」他反诘。
「拿墨西哥人的标准量，就是身量略胖了些，」休一面打量，一面接着说。他不知道为什么，一股缓缓的怒火开始攥住了他。「那么，你兴许就是——法戈。」

===Original===
“And what if I am?”
The eyes of the two men met, and Hugh saw the bulldog lips drawing back over the strong teeth. The lids half-dropped over his own eyes, and he stood as if deep in thought.
He had been a little afraid, at first. Even now he was not blind to the evident strength of the formidable body, the huge fists, the brutal jaws. Yet﻿—he suddenly knew to his vast amazement﻿—these things no longer mattered. Instinctively he knew that he was face to face with a mortal foe; but he felt a miraculous trust in his own strength.
===Chinese===
「我是又怎么样？」
两双眼睛碰在了一处，休看见那两片斗牛犬般的嘴唇朝后一咧，露出了坚实的牙齿。他自己的眼皮半垂下来，站在那里，仿佛沉入了深思。
起初，他是有几分怕的。即便此刻，他也不是看不见那具可畏躯壳里显然的蛮力、那两只巨大的拳头、那副凶横的腮颊。然而——他猛然明白过来，自己也大大吃了一惊——这些都不再要紧了。他凭着本能知道，自己正同一个殊死的仇敌面对面；可他对自己的力气，怀着一种不可思议的信赖。

===Original===
“I know something about Landy Fargo,” Hugh answered quietly. “He’s not the man I let sit by my fire. And the sooner you get away I think the better it will be.”
Fargo glared, and there ensued a half-second of strained silence, of curious immobility on the part of them both. The fire blazed beside them, the shadows leaped and danced, far away the moon gleamed on the white peaks of the Rockies. The whole forest world was wrapped in impenetrable silence. Fargo snarled, then started to turn.
And at that instant each of them forgot﻿—for a little while﻿—each other’s presence. They stood wholly silent, scarcely breathing﻿—listening as men listen when life itself is at stake. From far away in the still forest﻿—in the direction that Alice had gone﻿—both of them heard the faint, savage bay of the hounds.
===Chinese===
「兰迪·法戈其人，我知道一些来历，」休静静答道。「他不是我会让在火边坐下的那种人。我想，你走得越早越好。」
法戈怒目而视，随后是半秒钟紧绷的沉默，两个人都古怪地一动不动。营火在他们身旁烧得正旺，影子腾跳扑舞，远处月光照着落基山雪白的峰峦。整个森林世界裹在一重化不开的寂静里。法戈喉咙里滚出一声低吼，随即转身要走。
而就在这一瞬间，两人都把对方——暂且——忘到了脑后。他们一声不出地站着，几乎不敢喘气——像人命悬一线时人们侧耳的那样听着。从寂静森林的远处——从爱丽丝去的那个方向——两个人都听见了猎犬隐隐约约、凶蛮狂野的吠嗥。

===Original===
No human being, at that distance, could mistake the cry. The pack was hunting. It was running its game. And from the wild excitement and exultation of the clamoring voices, it was plain that the trail was hot, that the hounds were almost upon their prey.
Hugh suddenly turned his eyes to Fargo, trying to interpret the strange, exultant look in his brutal face. His own eyes narrowed. Then he started﻿—a strange convulsive jerk that no man had ever seen in him before. It was an instinctive recoil at a great dread and horror that suddenly swept over him. There had been no time for thought. It was as if a voice had spoken, instantly and clear, and had told him the real character of that wild hunt in the darkness.
For he had heard, infinitely dim but sharp as a needle prick through almost a mile of silent forest, the explosion of Alice’s pistol. Some great danger was upon her and her little flock; even now, perhaps, she was fighting for her life. It was a moment of crisis not alone for her but for him: the time in which his metal would be tried in the fire. He knew, surely as if a voice had told him, that there were no seconds to waste.
===Chinese===
隔着那样的距离，没有一个人会听错那叫声。狗群正在行猎。它们正撵着猎物。而从那片喧嚷的犬声里狂野的亢奋与得意听来，明摆着踪味正热，猎犬眼看就要扑上猎物了。
休猛地掉眼看向法戈，想解读他那张凶脸上那副异样而得意的神色。他自己的眼睛眯细了。随即他猛地一颤——一阵痉挛似的抽动，此前谁也不曾在休身上见过。那是一阵巨大的惊惧骇怖兜头罩下时，本能的一缩。根本容不得思索。就像有个声音当场清清楚楚地开了口，告诉他这黑暗中那场野猎的真正底细。
因为他听见了爱丽丝手枪的爆响——穿过将近一英里沉静的森林传来，微弱至极，却尖利如针刺。某种巨大的凶险已经落到她和她那一小群羊的头上；说不定此刻，她正在为性命厮拼。这是一个生死攸关的关头，不只是她的，也是他的：正是他的成色要放进火里受验的时刻。他知道得确凿无疑，就像有声音告诉过他一样：一秒钟也糟蹋不得。

===Original===
“No,” he said clearly, “I believe you’d better stay here. I’ll take your horse.”
There was no time to catch and saddle Alice’s animal, feeding at the edge of the meadow. There was no tone of request in the words. He had simply given an order: with his very life he would see that it was obeyed.
“You will, will you?” Fargo howled. “We’ll see about that﻿—”
===Chinese===
「不，」他说得清清楚楚，「我看你最好留在这儿。你的马，我要了。」
再没有工夫去草甸边上逮爱丽丝那匹啃着草的马、替它备鞍了。这几句话里没有半点请求的口气。他干脆就是下了一道命令：他将以性命作保，非看到它照办不可。
「你要，你倒要试试？」法戈嚎了起来。「咱们走着瞧——」

===Original===
Hugh reached for the reins, and it seemed to him that Fargo’s hand was fumbling at his hip. That in itself didn’t matter. Hugh only knew that he wanted the horse and that nothing must stand in the way. Fargo was shouting, his dark mouth was open. And Hugh lashed out with his fist, aiming straight for the savage lips.
He struck with all his strength, scarcely in rage but just as a means to an end. He had never fought before, yet the blow came unerringly and with terrific power. There could be only one result to such a blow as that. He dimly heard Fargo grunt﻿—like a beast as it falls below the butcher’s stroke﻿—then saw him reel and fall. He started to swing into the saddle.
It was better, he thought, that this man remain unconscious until he returned. He didn’t forget that he was still shepherd of the flocks and that Fargo was an enemy. Some great test lay before him, and the fewer his foes the better. He leaped down﻿—like a cougar springing from his ambush﻿—and struck once with each fist into the soggy, brutal face.
They were terrific blows, but expedience, rather than cruelty, was the motive behind them. Hugh did not even wonder at himself. He swung lightly on to the horse and lashed it to a gallop.
===Chinese===
休伸手去抓缰绳，恍惚间只觉法戈那只手正往腰际摸索。那本身无关紧要。休只知道他要这匹马，什么东西也休想拦道。法戈吼叫着，那张黑洞洞的嘴巴张得老大。休一拳抡了出去，照准那两片凶恶的嘴唇直捣过去。
这一拳他使足了全身的力气，与其说出于狂怒，不如说只当作成事的一个手段。他此前从未跟人动过手，可这一拳落点分毫不差，力道骇人。这样的拳头只可能有一种结果。他隐约听见法戈一声闷哼——像一头牲口挨了屠刀栽倒——接着便见他打了个趔趄，扑地倒了。他便抬身要翻上马鞍。
他想，这人不如就这么人事不省地躺到他回来。他没有忘记自己仍是羊群的牧人，而法戈是个仇敌。一场重大的考验横在他面前，仇敌越少越好。他跳下去——像一头从埋伏里扑出的美洲狮——左右拳各捣一记，直打进那张绵软凶横的脸。
这两拳打得吓人，可拳背后的动机是权宜，不是残忍。休甚至没工夫对自己的所作所为感到惊奇。他轻身一荡翻上马背，一鞭抽下去，催得马撒开四蹄疾驰起来。

## XVII / 第十七章

===Original===
These were not wolves. This fact dawned upon Alice Crowson, running her little flock at top speed toward the camp, before ever she saw their savage forms burst forth from the thickets behind and even before she discerned the twitch and leap of their shadows in the distant stretch of moonlit canyon. Only in the starving time of winter had wolves approached with such terrible fearlessness and frenzy. Nor was the cry that long, strange running song of the wolf pack. She knew their breed. They were enormous hounds: such a savage pack as might have started forth from some awful Underworld of fable.

And it would have been better were they wolves. Not for nothing has man waged immemorial centuries of warfare, not only upon wolves but on the great felines as well. They have been taught a wholesome respect for the tall breed that has come to dominate the earth, and much hunger and madness must be upon them before they will dare raise fang or claw against him. But it is not this way with dogs. They have lived among men since the first days of the cave dwellers; they have found men out; they have been willing slaves and faithful servants, and once the impulse comes to attack, there is no ingenerate barrier of instincts to hold them back.

Alice glanced behind, and the Little People that watch with such bright eyes all the dramas of the forest heard her utter an unfamiliar sound. It sprang instinctively to her lips. “Hugh!” she cried to that beetling silence. “Help me, Hugh.” Yet she knew that she cried in vain. She was still more than half a mile from the camp; and Hugh could not hear her, and he could not save.

===Chinese===
这些不是狼。爱丽丝·克劳森赶着她那一小群羊全速奔向营地，还没看见它们狰狞的身形从身后的灌木丛里窜出，甚至还没辨清远处月光峡谷里那些影子的一攒一跃，这个事实便已在她心头明朗。只有在冬天闹饥荒的时节，狼才会带着这般可怕的无畏与疯狂逼近。那嗥叫也不是狼群那种悠长而怪异的行猎之歌。她认得这一族的底细。它们是硕大无比的猎犬：这样凶残的一群，仿佛是从传说中某个可怕的冥府里放出来的。

倘若是狼，反倒好了。人类向狼群、也向大型猫族宣战，打过了亘古的千百个世纪，这绝非枉然。狼族已被驯出一份对那个已经统治大地的高大种族的应有敬畏，若非饥火与癫狂逼到了极处，它们绝不敢向人类张牙舞爪。狗却不是这样。自穴居人的最初岁月起，它们便生活在人类当中；它们早已把人类摸得透熟；它们甘当驯顺的奴隶、忠心的仆役，而一旦攻击的冲动袭来，与生俱来的本能里却没有一道栅栏拦得住它们。

爱丽丝回头瞥了一眼，那些睁着晶亮眼睛观看森林一切悲欢的小小部族，听见她口中发出一声陌生的喊叫。那声音是本能地涌上她唇边的。「休！」她朝着那片压顶般森然的寂静喊道，「救救我，休。」然而她知道喊也是徒劳。她离营地还有半英里多；休听不见她，也救不了她。

===Original===
For the pack had revealed itself. There was a stretch behind her almost bare of underbrush; the great trees laid shadows across it like iron bars across the windows of a cell, and shapeless black shadows were leaping across it. There was a countless number of them, and they seemed to be overtaking her with heartbreaking rapidity. Instantly she knew that she could not hope to reach the camp before they would be upon her.

She must not permit herself to lag behind. Her place was with the sheep. The savage hounds were on their track, and her one hope was that her presence, with the aid of the pistol, might hold them off until she could head the helpless band into the camp. She tried to blind her eyes to another, more dreadful, possibility. This was no time to admit it into her thoughts. But surely, surely they would not attack *her*. Dogs always barked and menaced, but they rarely really attacked human beings. Yet the thought kept creeping back, haunting her, filling her—in those little seconds of stress in which the pack leaped nearer—with an unnamable horror. It couldn’t be that they were so frantic that they would tear her down in order to get at their prey. Fate did not have that in store for her at least—their cruel fangs at her tender flesh, their leaping, frenzied bodies lingering just an instant beside her, then racing on after the terrified sheep.

It occurred to her that there might be time, even yet, to spring up a tree and leave the sheep to their fate. Yet was not she the shepherdess, the guardian of the flock? But she saw the issue clearly, and her eyes glanced about for some tree with branches low enough where she could climb to safety. But already that chance was lost. She was in a region of open forest—great trees standing one by one with branches starting fully fifty feet above ground—and even now the dogs were at her heels.

===Chinese===
因为狗群已经露了形。她身后有一段几乎不长下层灌丛的空地；大树把影子横投在上面，活像牢房窗上的铁条，一团团不成形状的黑影正从上面跃过。它们多得数不清，而且似乎正以令人心碎的迅疾朝她追近。她当即明白，自己别指望在它们扑到之前赶到营地了。

她决不能容自己落到羊群后头。她的位置在羊群身边。凶残的猎犬正循着羊的踪迹追来，她唯一的指望，是凭自己的在场、再借手枪之力把它们逼开，撑到她把这无助的一小群引进营地为止。她竭力不去看另一种更可怕的可能。此刻还不是容它进心里的时候。可它们总不至于、总不至于攻击*她*吧。狗向来只会吠叫、恫吓，极少真的向人下口。然而那念头仍不断潜回来，纠缠着她，充塞着她——在狗群又跃近几步的那几小秒煎熬里——让她满怀一种无以名状的恐怖。它们不至于疯魔到为了扑到猎物而把她撕倒吧。命运至少不曾为她预备下这一幕——那残虐的獠牙咬进她娇嫩的血肉，那些腾跃的、癫狂的身躯在她身旁只逗留一瞬，便又去追赶那些吓破胆的羊。

她心头闪过一个念头：也许还来得及蹿上一棵树，任羊群去听天由命。可她不是牧羊女、不是羊群的守护人吗？但她把眼前的险境看得分明，目光不由四下搜寻枝干低得能攀上去避难的树。可这机会已经丢了。她正处在一带疏朗的林子里——大树一棵棵孤零零立着，枝条要到离地足足五十英尺的高处才生出来——而狗已经追到她脚跟了。

===Original===
She swung about, she saw their huge, dark forms in the moonlight. No man could look into their yellow-green eyes and doubt the madness that was upon them. The pack seemed to divide, some of them closed in, the others circled about and arrested the flight of the sheep. They drew up, wholly enclosed by the savage ring. And Alice reached for her pistol.

Through its long years that forest had never beheld a stranger scene: the huddled band of white sheep, the girl—the eternal shepherdess of the wilderness—standing guard over them, the savage pack in its grim circle. Over it all abided the mystery of the moon, and ranged about were the tall, impassive pines. The high range behind was a sweep of incredible beauty. But just an instant the tableau endured. It broke, the enchanted immobility of the forms gave way to lightning movement, the shadows leaped—met—sprung apart with the sense of infinite motion, the whole scene was in an instant the wild confusion and stress of a nightmare. The hounds sprang toward the sheep.

Alice screamed, cried out, then fired her pistol. Her aim was none too good the first shot. Her terror cost her the steadiness of her hand. Yet at once she recalled the dreadful fact that the only shells she carried were in her pistol, her reserve supply having been packed among her camp supplies, and she must not waste one. But the leaping dogs were an almost impossible target: her second shot succeeded only in scratching along the shoulder of Old Ben, the pack leader, arousing his savagery all the more.

===Chinese===
她霍地转过身，看见月光下它们巨大而黢黑的形影。任谁看进它们那黄绿色的眼睛，都不会怀疑附在它们身上的疯魔。狗群像是在分散，一部分围拢上来，其余的兜着圈子截住了羊群的奔逃。羊群收住脚，被那凶残的包围圈整个圈在当中。于是爱丽丝伸手去摸手枪。

这片森林历尽悠悠岁月，从未见过比这更奇异的景象：蜷作一堆的白羊群，姑娘——荒野里永恒的牧羊女——立在一旁守卫，凶残的狗群围成冷森森的一圈。月光的那份神秘笼盖着这一切，四下里排立着高峻、无动于衷的松树。背后高高的山岭铺展成一片美得难以置信的长卷。可这幅静画只撑了一瞬。它碎了，那中了魔咒般的凝定化作闪电般的动作，黑影跃起——相撞——带着无穷的动势弹开，整个场景顷刻间化作梦魇一般的狂乱与紧逼。猎犬朝羊群扑了过去。

爱丽丝尖叫、呼喊，接着开了枪。第一枪瞄得不怎么准。恐惧夺走了她手的沉稳。但她立刻想起一个可怕的事实：她随身的子弹只有枪膛里的这些，备用的都打包在营地给养里了，一发也糟蹋不得。可那些腾跃的狗几乎是打不中的目标：第二枪只擦着狗群领头的老本（Old Ben）的肩头犁过一道，反倒把他一腔凶性激得更旺。

===Original===
The sheep were dying now. Already the scene was one of unspeakable carnage. Yet it was true that the full madness of the feast of death was not yet upon them. They lingered to worry their dead before springing to a fresh victim. And such times gave Alice her only feeble chance to use her pistol.

The third shot went true: the dog fell kicking in the dry pine needles. Two of the others, maddened by the sight of the death struggle, sprang with revolting fury upon his defenseless body. She fired at them, sobbing when she saw she missed again. It was her fourth shot; and only two remained.

A pistol has never the power of a rifle. It lacks the ability to shock and stun, and, worse, has nothing of its deadly accuracy. And it was all too plain that she had not succeeded in terrifying the pack. Still they plunged at the sheep, avoiding only those that were crowded about her knees, and their fury was increasing with every instant. Every breath saw more of their domestic instincts fall away from them, giving way to the deadly passions of the wild.

She fired the fifth time, and once more she shot true. The dog died in the pine needles. Then she heard the pistol’s sharp sound again.

===Chinese===
